\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(\log\rho)_{r}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C_{0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big(|u|_{2}+|v|_{\infty}\big)\leq C(T).

(3.27)

Next, multiplying
\eqref{cosingu}_{1}
by
\rho^{-1}
and integrating the resulting equality over
r\in[0,1]
,
we then obtain from (
3.26
) and the
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate of
v
that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\log\rho\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C(T)\big(\|\log\rho_{0}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+1\big)\leq C(T),

(3.28)

which, along with (
3.26
)–(
3.27
) and the fact that
H^{1}(0,1)\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(0,1)
,
implies that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\log\rho\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}\leq C_{0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big\|\big(\log\rho,(\log\rho)_{r}\big)\big\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C(T).

(3.29)

This indicates that
\rho
admits a strictly positive lower bound in
[0,T]\times[0,1]
.
The pointwise estimate of
\rho(t,r)
on
[0,T]\times[0,R]

(R>0)
can be derived similarly,
which requires precisely analyzing the dependence of the constants in (
3.27
)–(
3.29
) on
R
(see §
6
).

3.3.5.
Global-in-time uniform energy estimates

Based on the
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimates of
(\rho,v)
, we consider the enlarged system (
3.2
) in spherical coordinates,
i.e.
,
\eqref{e2.2}_{1}
–
\eqref{e2.2}_{3}
in §
7
, for establishing the desired global estimates
in §
7
–§
8
. We observe that the derivation of global estimates for spherically symmetric solutions in the exterior domain
is similar to that for the 1-D case; while the derivation of global estimates near the origin, due to the coordinate singularity
\frac{1}{r}
in (
7.2
), more closely resembles that for the M-D case.

We consider the
2
-order regular solutions in the 3-D case.
First, since any spherically symmetric vector field is curl-free,
by employing the classical div-curl estimates and
Lemma
B.1
in Appendix
B
,
we establish the equivalences of the
W^{k,p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
-norms
(
0\leq k\leq 3
and
p\in(1,\infty)
) between the gradient
\nabla\boldsymbol{f}

and the divergence
\diver\boldsymbol{f}=f_{r}+\frac{2}{r}f
of the vector
function
\boldsymbol{f}=\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{r}f
, namely, Lemma
7.1
in §
7
.
For example, according to Lemma
7.1
, deriving the uniform
L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
-estimate
for
\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}
is equivalent to proving

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\Big(r\big(u_{r}+\frac{2}{r}u\big)_{rr},\big(u_{r}+\frac{2}{r}u\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}\leq C(T).

Thus, Lemma
7.1
, combined with the structure of system
\eqref{e2.2}_{1}
–
\eqref{e2.2}_{3}
,
subsequently assists us in establishing higher-order spatial derivative estimates for
(\phi,u,\psi)
.

Second, for establishing the tangential estimates of
u
, we need two crucial estimates—the
L^{2}([0,T];L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
-estimates for
(\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{u})
.
By Lemma
B.1
, one actually needs

\int_{0}^{T}|u|_{\infty}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C(T),\qquad\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{\infty}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C(T).

(3.30)

\eqref{66}_{1}
can directly be derived from (
3.26
) and the Sobolev
embedding
H^{1}(I)\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(I)
.
For
\eqref{66}_{2}
, we can first build a bridge between the
L^{\infty}(I)
-norm of
ru_{r}

and the
L^{2}(I)
-norm of
ru_{t}
via the Sobolev
embedding
H^{1}(1,\infty)\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(1,\infty)
,
the Hardy inequality,
Lemmas
7.1
and
B.1
,
equation
\eqref{e2.2}_{2}
, and some lower order tangential estimates of
u
:

\displaystyle|ru_{r}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|ru_{r}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}+\|ru_{r}\|_{L^{\infty}(1,\infty)}\leq C_{0}\big(\big\|r^{\frac{3}{2}}(u_{r},u_{rr})\big\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\big\|r(u_{r},u_{rr})\big\|_{L^{2}(1,\infty)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|(ru_{r},ru_{rr})|_{2}\leq C(T)\big(|ru_{t}|_{2}+1\big).

Subsequently, the above inequality and some detailed analysis enable us to close the second-order tangential
estimates for
u
, that is,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|ru_{t}|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\Big|r\big(u_{tr}+\frac{2}{r}u_{t}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C(T),

which, along with Lemmas
7.1
and
B.1
,
\eqref{e2.2}_{2}
, the Sobolev embedding
H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, and the lower-order estimates of
u
,
leads to
\eqref{66}_{2}
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\Big|r\big(u_{tr}+\frac{2}{r}u_{t}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}t+C(T)\leq C(T).

3.4.
Some comments for the case that
\bar{\rho}>0

Compared with the case that
\bar{\rho}=0
in (
1.3
), the major difference here lies in the estimates in the
exterior domains. Now, consider the case
n=3
as an example. We provide some brief comments on establishing the global boundedness of
(\rho,v)
.

3.4.1.
Global uniform upper bound of the density

We divide the proof of the upper bound of
\rho
into two parts:
the estimates in
B_{1}=\{\boldsymbol{x}:\,|\boldsymbol{x}|<1\}

and those in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{1}
.
In fact, one can directly derive the uniform upper bound of
\rho

in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{1}
based on the fundamental energy estimate
and the BD entropy estimate (see Lemmas
10.2
–
10.5
in §
10.5
).

To establish the uniform upper bound of
\rho
in
B_{1}
,
by (
3.16
) in §
3.3.2
, it suffices to derive the
L^{p}(I)
-estimates of

(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}(u,v)
for some
p>3
.
First, repeating the derivation of (
3.19
) and using the uniform upper bound of
\rho

in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{1}
to handle the estimates in the exterior domain
imply that

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\!+\!\big|(r^{2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\!\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\leq C(p)\big|(r^{2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p-2}}u\big|_{p-2}^{p-2}+C(p)\mathcal{J}_{1},

where
\mathcal{J}_{1}
is defined in (
3.19
).
Then, using the same arguments as in (
3.20
)–(
3.22
) and the Grönwall inequality, we obtain

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|(r^{2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}(u,v)\big|_{p}^{p}\leq C(p,T)\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|(r^{2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p-2}}u\big|_{p-2}^{p-2}+C(p,T).

(3.31)

Finally, taking
p=2N+2
with
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
in the above inequality and using Lemma
10.2
,
we recursively obtain the
L^{2N}(I)
-estimates for
(r^{2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2N}}(u,v)
and hence, via the interpolation,
the
L^{p}(I)
-estimates for
(r^{2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}(u,v)
for
p>3
.

3.4.2.
Global
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate of the effective velocity

The global
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate of
v
follows from the same argument as in §
3.3.3
,
with
p\in[2,\infty)
replaced by
p\in[4,\infty)
.
This adjustment is due to the term:

\mathcal{I}_{3}=A(p-1)\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r
in (
10.48
),
which arises in establishing estimate (
3.24
).
In fact,
\mathcal{I}_{3}
can be handled via the
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate of
\rho
,
and the Hölder and Young inequalities: for all
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{\frac{p}{2}}\big|(r^{2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-4}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}

(3.32)

\displaystyle\leq C(\epsilon,p,T)\underline{\big|(r^{2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-4}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}}_{:=\mathcal{J}_{5}}+\epsilon\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}.

Note that
\mathcal{J}_{5}
can be treated via the
L^{p}(I)
-energy estimates
of
u
(Lemma
10.9
in §
10.6
) only if
p\in[4,\infty)
.
By (
3.32
), we obtain (
3.24
) from the same argument as in §
3.3.3
.

3.4.3.
Global uniform lower bound of the density

Based on the
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimates of
(\rho,v)
, we can show
that
\rho
admits a global uniform lower bound in
[0,T]\times I
.
To achieve this, it suffices to establish the global
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate for
\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})
.

First, we can obtain (
3.26
) in a similar way as in §
3.3.4
.
Then we obtain from (
3.26
), the
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate of
v
, Lemma
A.4
,
and
2\alpha(\log\rho)_{r}=v-u
that

\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\big(\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}+1\big).

(3.33)

Next, multiplying
\eqref{cosingu}_{1}
by
\rho^{-1}\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})

and integrating the resulting equality over
I
, we obtain
from (
3.26
) and the
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate of
v
that

\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}\leq C(T)\big(\big|\log(\rho_{0}/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}+1\big)\leq C(T).

(3.34)

Note that the
L^{2}(I)
-boundedness of
\log(\rho_{0}/\bar{\rho})
can be obtained
by following the calculations (
10.65
)–(
10.66
) in the proof of
Lemma
10.15
.
Finally, combining (
3.33
)–(
3.34
), we derive
the desired global
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate of
\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})
.

4.
Global Uniform Upper Bound of the Density

The purpose of this section is to establish the global-in-time upper bound of the density
for the case that
\bar{\rho}=0
.
In §
4
–§
9
, we denote
C_{0}\in[1,\infty)
a generic constant depending only
on
(\rho_{0},u_{0},n,\alpha,A,\gamma)
, and
C(\nu_{1},\cdots\!,\nu_{k})\in[1,\infty)
a generic constant depending on
C_{0}

and parameters
(\nu_{1},\cdots\!,\nu_{k})
, which may be different at each occurrence. Moreover, we emphasize that, throughout this paper,
for any function space
X
and functions
(h,g_{1},\cdots\!,g_{k})
,

\|h(g_{1},\cdots\!,g_{k})\|_{X}:=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\|hg_{i}\|_{X}.

4.1.
Reformulation in the spherical coordinates

Let
T>0
be any fixed time, and let
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
be
the unique
s
-order
(s=2,3)
regular solution
of the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
(
n=2,3
),
which is spherically symmetric with the form:

\rho(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\rho(t,r),\quad\boldsymbol{u}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=u(t,r)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{r}\qquad\,\,\mbox{for $r=|\boldsymbol{x}|$}.

(4.1)

Such local well-posedness results have been established in Theorems
3.3
–
3.4
.
Then the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) can be transformed to the initial-boundary
value problem of
(\rho,u)(t,r)
in
(t,r)\in[0,T]\times I
:

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\rho_{t}+(\rho u)_{r}+\frac{m\rho u}{r}=0,\\[3.0pt]
\displaystyle\rho u_{t}+\rho uu_{r}+A(\rho^{\gamma})_{r}=2\alpha\big(\rho u_{r}+\frac{m\rho u}{r}\big)_{r}-\frac{2\alpha m\rho_{r}u}{r},\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle(\rho,u)|_{t=0}=(\rho_{0},u_{0})\qquad\text{for $r\in I$},\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle u|_{r=0}=0\qquad\qquad\qquad\,\,\,\text{for $t\in[0,T]$},\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle(\rho,u)\to\left(0,0\right)\qquad\qquad\,\,\,\text{as $r\to\infty$ \ for $t\in[0,T]$}.\end{cases}

(4.2)

Here, based on relation (
4.1
), we say that
(\rho,u)(t,r)
is the
s
-order
(s=2,3)
regular
solution of problem (
4.2
) in
[0,T]\times I
if the vector function

(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
is the
s
-order
(s=2,3)
regular solution (as defined in Definition
2.1
) of the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.3
)
with
\bar{\rho}=0
in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
(
n=2,3
).

Next, by Lemma
B.1
in Appendix B, one can transform the statements of Theorems
3.3
and
3.4

into the following conclusions in spherical coordinates.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let (
3.7
) hold. Assume the initial data
(\rho_{0},u_{0})(r)
satisfy

\displaystyle r^{m}\rho_{0}\in L^{1}(I),\qquad r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{(\log\rho_{0})_{r}}{r},(\log\rho_{0})_{rr}\big)\in L^{2}(I),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big((\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{r},\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{r}}{r},(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{rr},u_{0},\frac{u_{0}}{r},(u_{0})_{r},(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{r},(u_{0})_{rr}\big)\in L^{2}(I),

and, in addition,

\displaystyle(\log\rho_{0})_{r}\in L^{\infty}(I)\qquad\text{when $n=3$}.

Then there exists
T_{*}>0
such that problem (
4.2
) admits a unique
2
-order regular solution
(\rho,u)(t,r)
in
[0,T_{*}]\times I
that satisfies

\displaystyle r^{m}\rho\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{1}(I)),\quad\,(\log\rho)_{r}\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}]\times I),

(4.3)

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big((\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r},\frac{(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}}{r},(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{rr},(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{t},(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{tr}\big)\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{(\log\rho)_{r}}{r},(\log\rho)_{rr},(\log\rho)_{tr},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r},(\frac{u}{r})_{r},u_{rr},u_{t}\big)\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{1}{r}(\frac{u}{r})_{r},(\frac{u}{r})_{rr},\frac{u_{rr}}{r},u_{rrr},\frac{u_{t}}{r},u_{tr},t^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{tt},t^{\frac{1}{2}}(\frac{u_{t}}{r})_{r},t^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{trr}\big)\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{1}{r}(\frac{u}{r})_{r},(\frac{u}{r})_{rr},\frac{u_{rr}}{r},u_{rrr},\frac{u_{t}}{r},u_{tr}\big)\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

and

\displaystyle\big(\rho,\rho_{r},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r}\big)\in C((0,T_{*}];C(\bar{I})).

(4.4)

Theorem 4.2
.

Let (
3.7
) hold. Assume the initial data
(\rho_{0},u_{0})(r)
satisfy

\displaystyle r^{m}\rho_{0}\in L^{1}(I),\qquad r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{(\log\rho_{0})_{r}}{r},(\log\rho_{0})_{rr},(\frac{(\log\rho_{0})_{r}}{r})_{r},(\log\rho_{0})_{rrr}\big)\in L^{2}(I),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big((\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{r},\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{r}}{r},(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{rr},(\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{r}}{r})_{r},(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{rrr}\big)\in L^{2}(I),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{0},\frac{u_{0}}{r},(u_{0})_{r},(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{r},(u_{0})_{rr},\frac{1}{r}(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{r},(\frac{u_{0}}{r})_{rr},\frac{(u_{0})_{rr}}{r},(u_{0})_{rrr}\big)\in L^{2}(I).

Then there exists
T_{*}>0
such that problem (
4.2
) admits a unique
3
-order regular solution
(\rho,u)(t,r)
in
[0,T_{*}]\times I
that satisfies

\displaystyle r^{m}\rho\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{1}(I)),\qquad(\log\rho)_{r}\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}]\times I),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big((\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r},\frac{(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}}{r},(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{rr},(\frac{(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}}{r})_{r},(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{rrr}\big)\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big((\rho^{\gamma-1})_{t},(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{tr},\frac{(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{tr}}{r},(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{trr}\big)\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{(\log\rho)_{r}}{r},(\log\rho)_{rr},(\frac{(\log\rho)_{r}}{r})_{r},(\log\rho)_{rrr}\big)\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big((\log\rho)_{tr},\frac{(\log\rho)_{tr}}{r},(\log\rho)_{trr}\big)\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{tt}\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),\qquad r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big((\rho^{\gamma-1})_{ttr},(\log\rho)_{ttr}\big)\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

(4.5)

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r},(\frac{u}{r})_{r},u_{rr},\frac{1}{r}(\frac{u}{r})_{r},(\frac{u}{r})_{rr},\frac{u_{rr}}{r},u_{rrr},u_{t},\frac{u_{t}}{r},u_{tr}\big)\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big((\frac{1}{r}(\frac{u}{r})_{r})_{r},(\frac{u}{r})_{rrr},(\frac{u_{rr}}{r})_{r},u_{rrrr},(\frac{u_{t}}{r})_{r},u_{trr}\big)\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big((\frac{1}{r}(\frac{u}{r})_{r})_{r},(\frac{u}{r})_{rrr},(\frac{u_{rr}}{r})_{r},u_{rrrr},(\frac{u_{t}}{r})_{r},u_{trr},u_{tt}\big)\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{u_{tt}}{r},u_{ttr},u_{trrr},\frac{u_{trr}}{r},(\frac{u_{t}}{r})_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u_{tr}}{r}\big)_{r}\big)\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

and

\big(\rho,\rho_{r},\rho_{t},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r}\big)\in C([0,T_{*}];C(\bar{I}))\ \ \text{and}\ \ \big(u_{t},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r},u_{rr}\big)\in C((0,T_{*}];C(\bar{I})).

(4.6)

4.2.
Upper bound of the density

Let
T>0
be any fixed time, and let
(\rho,u)(t,r)
be the
s
-order
(s=2,3)
regular solution of
problem (
4.2
) in
[0,T]\times I
obtained in Theorems
4.1
–
4.2
.
We define the characteristic functions
(\chi^{\flat}_{\sigma},\chi^{\sharp}_{\sigma})
(
\sigma>0
) as

\chi^{\flat}_{\sigma}(r):=\begin{cases}1&r\in[0,\sigma),\\
0&r\in[\sigma,\infty),\end{cases}\qquad\chi^{\sharp}_{\sigma}:=1-\chi^{\flat}_{\sigma}.

(4.7)

First, we give the definition of the so-called effective velocity.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
(\rho,u,\alpha)
be defined as in
§
1
.
Then

v:=u+2\alpha(\log\rho)_{r}

(4.8)

is said to be the effective velocity in spherical coordinates.
In addition, define
v_{0}:=v|_{t=0}=u_{0}+2\alpha(\log\rho_{0})_{r}
.

Then we have the following standard energy estimates and the BD entropy estimates:

Lemma 4.1
.

There exists a constant
C_{0}>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\left(\rho u^{2}+\rho^{\gamma}\right)(t,\cdot)\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\Big(\rho|u_{r}|^{2}+\rho\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C_{0},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\left(\rho v^{2}+|(\sqrt{\rho})_{r}|^{2}+\rho^{\gamma}\right)(t,\cdot)\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma-2}|\rho_{r}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1.
First, multiplying
\eqref{e1.5}_{2}
by
r^{m}u
, along with
\eqref{e1.5}_{1}
, yields

\begin{split}&\Big(\frac{r^{m}}{2}\rho u^{2}+\frac{A}{\gamma-1}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}\Big)_{t}+2\alpha r^{m}\rho u_{r}^{2}+2\alpha mr^{m-2}\rho u^{2}\\
&=\Big(-\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}u+2\alpha r^{m}\rho uu_{r}-\frac{r^{m}}{2}\rho u^{3}\Big)_{r}.\end{split}

(4.9)

Integrating the above over
I
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}r^{m}\rho u^{2}+\frac{A}{\gamma-1}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r+2\alpha\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\Big(\rho|u_{r}|^{2}+m\rho\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r

(4.10)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\underline{-\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}u+2\alpha r^{m}\rho uu_{r}-\frac{r^{m}}{2}\rho u^{3}}_{:=\mathcal{B}_{1}}\Big)_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\mathcal{B}_{1}|_{r=0}=0.

To verify this, we need to show that
\mathcal{B}_{1}\in W^{1,1}(I)
and
\mathcal{B}_{1}|_{r=0}=0

for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, which allows us to apply Lemma
A.4
to obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}(\mathcal{B}_{1})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\mathcal{B}_{1}|_{r=0}=0.

(4.11)

On one hand, it follows from (
4.3
)–(
4.4
) (or (
4.5
)–(
4.6
)) that

r^{m}\rho\in L^{1}(I),\quad\Big(\rho,\rho_{r},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r}\Big)\in C(\bar{I}),\quad r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big((\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r},u_{rr}\Big)\in L^{2}(I)

for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, so that we can conclude that
\mathcal{B}_{1}|_{r=0}=0
.
On the other hand, it follows from the Hölder inequality
that
\mathcal{B}_{1}\in W^{1,1}(I)
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
:

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{1}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|r^{m}(\rho^{\gamma}u,\rho uu_{r},\rho u^{3})\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|r^{m}\rho|_{1}|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|u|_{\infty}+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+|u|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}\big)<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{B}_{1})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|r^{m-1}(\rho^{\gamma}u,\rho uu_{r},\rho u^{3})\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\,+C_{0}\big|r^{m}\big(\rho^{\gamma-1}\rho_{r}u,\rho^{\gamma}u_{r},\rho_{r}uu_{r},\rho(u_{r})^{2},\rho uu_{rr},\rho_{r}u^{3},\rho u^{2}u_{r}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|r^{m}\rho|_{1}|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}\Big(|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\,+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}\big|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}+C_{0}|r^{m}\rho|_{1}|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|u_{r}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\,+C_{0}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}^{2}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\,+C_{0}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}|u_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}<\infty.

Integrating (
4.10
) over
[0,t]
, we obtain the energy estimates, provided that

(r\rho_{0})^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{0}\in L^{2}(I)
and
r^{\frac{m}{\gamma}}\rho_{0}\in L^{\gamma}(I)
.
Indeed, it follows from Lemmas
A.1
,
A.11
, and
B.1
that

\displaystyle\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{0}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|r^{m}\rho_{0}|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}|u_{0}|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{0},

\displaystyle|r^{\frac{m}{\gamma}}\rho_{0}|_{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq|r^{m}\rho_{0}|_{1}|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\leq C_{0}\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}}\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\gamma-1}\leq C_{0}.

2.
To obtain the BD entropy estimates, we can first apply
\partial_{r}
to
\eqref{e1.5}_{1}
to obtain

\rho((\log\rho)_{r})_{t}+\rho u(\log\rho)_{rr}+\Big(\rho\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)\Big)_{r}-\frac{m\rho_{r}u}{r}=0.

(4.12)

Then combining with
\eqref{e1.5}_{2}
and
(
4.12
)
leads to

\rho(v_{t}+uv_{r})+A(\rho^{\gamma})_{r}=0.

(4.13)

Thus, multiplying (
4.13
) by
r^{m}v
and
integrating the resulting equality over
[0,t]\times I
, we obtain from
\eqref{e1.5}_{1}
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\rho v^{2}+\frac{A}{\gamma-1}\rho^{\gamma}\Big)(t,\cdot)\,\mathrm{d}r+2A\alpha\gamma\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma-2}|\rho_{r}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s

(4.14)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\big|(r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}v_{0}\big|_{2}^{2}+\frac{A}{\gamma-1}|r^{\frac{m}{\gamma}}\rho_{0}|_{\gamma}^{\gamma}\leq\frac{1}{2}\big|(r\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}v_{0}\big|_{2}^{2}+C_{0}.

For the
L^{2}(I)
-boundedness of
(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}v_{0}
, it follows from Lemmas
A.1
,
A.11
,
and
B.1
that

\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}v_{0}\big|_{2}\leq|r^{m}\rho_{0}|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}|(u_{0},(\log\rho_{0})_{r})|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\nabla\log\rho_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{0}.

Finally, (
4.8
), together with the energy estimates and (
4.14
), yields

|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho})_{r}|_{2}=\frac{1}{2}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\log\rho)_{r}\big|_{2}\leq C_{0}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}(v,u)\big|_{2}\leq C_{0}.

The proof of Lemma
4.1
is completed.
∎

Clearly, by (
4.8
) and (
4.13
), we have

Corollary 4.1
.

The effective velocity
v
satisfies the following equation
:

v_{t}+uv_{r}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\rho^{\gamma-1}(v-u)=0.

(4.15)

Next, we show the
L^{1}(I)
and
L^{p}(\omega,\infty)
-estimates (
p\in(1,\infty]
) of
r^{m}\rho
for
t\in[0,T]
and
\omega>0
.

Lemma 4.2
.

There exists a constant
C_{0}>0
such that

|r^{m}\rho(t)|_{1}\leq C_{0}\qquad\text{for any $t\in[0,T]$}.

Moreover, for any
\omega>0
, there exists a constant
C(\omega)>0
such that

|\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega}r^{m}\rho(t)|_{p}\leq C(\omega)\qquad\text{for any $p\in(1,\infty]$ and $t\in[0,T]$}.

Proof.

First, integrating
\eqref{e1.5}_{1}
over
I
, we obtain
from
\eqref{e1.5}_{4}
,
r^{m}\rho u\in W^{1,1}(I)
,

r^{m}\rho u|_{r=0}=0
due to
(\rho,u)\in C(\bar{I})
for
t\in(0,T]
,
and Lemma
A.4
that

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\rho\,\mathrm{d}r=-\int_{0}^{\infty}(r^{m}\rho u)_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=r^{m}\rho u|_{r=0}=0.

Integrating above over
[0,t]
, along with Lemma
B.1
, yields

|r^{m}\rho(t)|_{1}=|r^{m}\rho_{0}|_{1}\leq C_{0}\qquad\text{for any $t\in[0,T]$}.

(4.16)

Next, let
\omega>0
. It follows from (
4.16
), Lemmas
4.1
and
A.4
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\begin{split}|\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega}r^{m}\rho|_{\infty}&\leq|\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega}(r^{m}\rho)_{r}|_{1}\leq C_{0}\big(|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{m}\sqrt{\rho}(\sqrt{\rho})_{r}|_{1}+|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{m-1}\rho|_{1}\big)\\
&\leq C_{0}\big(|r^{m}\rho|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho})_{r}|_{2}+|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{-1}|_{\infty}|r^{m}\rho|_{1}\big)\leq C(\omega).\end{split}

(4.17)

Consequently, it follows from (
4.16
)–(
4.17
) that,
for any
p\in(1,\infty)
and
t\in[0,T]
,

|\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega}r^{m}\rho|_{p}\leq|\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega}r^{m}\rho|_{\infty}^{1-\frac{1}{p}}|r^{m}\rho|_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq C(\omega),

where
C(\omega)\in[1,\infty)
is a generic constant depending on
C_{0}

and
\omega
, which may be different at each occurrence. This completes the proof.
∎

The next lemma concerns the weighted
L^{p}(0,1)
-estimates of
\rho
for
p\in[1,\infty]
.

Lemma 4.3
.

For any
t\in[0,T]
,

\mathrm{(i)}

When
n=2
, there exist two positive constants
C(p,\nu)
and
C(\nu)
such that

\displaystyle\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\nu}\rho(t)\big|_{p}\leq C(p,\nu)

\displaystyle\text{for any }\nu>-\frac{1}{p}\text{ and }p\in[1,\infty),

(4.18)

\displaystyle\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\nu}\rho(t)\big|_{\infty}\leq C(\nu)

\displaystyle\text{for any }\nu>0;

\mathrm{(ii)}

When
n=3
, there exist two positive constants
C_{0}
and
C(p)
such that

\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r\rho(t)\big|_{\infty}\leq C_{0},\qquad\,\,\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\rho(t)\big|_{p}\leq C(p)\quad\text{for any $p\in[1,\infty)$}.

(4.19)

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1.

Proof for the
2
-
D
case.

First, we can obtain from Lemmas
4.1
–
4.2
,
and
A.3
that, for any
\nu>0
and
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\nu}\rho|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{\nu}{2}}\sqrt{\rho}|_{\infty}^{2}\leq C(\nu)\big(\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{\nu+1}{2}}\sqrt{\rho}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{\nu+1}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho})_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}\big)

(4.20)

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu)|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\nu}|_{\infty}\big(|r\rho|_{1}+\big|r^{\frac{1}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho})_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}\big)\leq C(\nu).

Then, for all
p\in[1,\infty)
and
\nu>-\frac{1}{p}
,
letting
\varepsilon\in\big(0,\,\min\{p\nu+1,1\}\big)
be any fixed constant,
we obtain from (
4.20
) that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\nu}\rho|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}r^{p\nu-\varepsilon}r^{\varepsilon}\rho^{p}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq\Big(\int_{0}^{1}r^{p\nu-\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{\varepsilon}{p}}\rho\big|_{\infty}^{p}\leq C(p,\nu).

(4.21)

The proof of (i) is completed.

2.

Proof for the
3
-
D
case.

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
. It follows from Lemmas
4.1
–
4.2
and
A.3
that,
for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\rho\big|_{p}

\displaystyle=\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2p}}\sqrt{\rho}\big|_{2p}^{2}\leq C(p)\big(|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r\sqrt{\rho}|_{2}^{2}+|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r\sqrt{\rho}_{r}|_{2}^{2}\big)\leq C(p),

\displaystyle\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r\rho\big|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\rho}\big|_{\infty}^{2}\leq C_{0}\big(|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r\sqrt{\rho}|_{2}^{2}+|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r\sqrt{\rho}_{r}|_{2}^{2}\big)\leq C_{0}.

The proof of (ii) is completed.
∎

Based on Lemmas
4.2
–
4.3
, we can show
the
L^{p}(I)
-estimates of
(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u
for
p\geq 2
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
. For any
p\in[2,\infty)
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, there exist two positive constants
C(p)
and
C(p,\epsilon)
such that,
for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+p\alpha\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)

(4.22)

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\Big(1+\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)+C(p,\epsilon)+\epsilon\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1.
Let
p\in[2,\infty)
. Multiplying
\eqref{e1.5}_{2}
by
r^{m}|u|^{p-2}u
, along with
\eqref{e1.5}_{1}
, gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}(r^{m}\rho|u|^{p})_{t}+2\alpha(p-1)r^{m}\rho|u|^{p-2}|u_{r}|^{2}+2\alpha mr^{m-2}\rho|u|^{p}

(4.23)

\displaystyle=(p-1)Ar^{m}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u_{r}+mAr^{m-1}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u

\displaystyle+\Big(\underline{2\alpha r^{m}\rho|u|^{p-2}uu_{r}-Ar^{m}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u-\frac{1}{p}r^{m}\rho u|u|^{p}}_{:=\mathcal{B}_{2}}\Big)_{r}.

Here we need to show that
\mathcal{B}_{2}\in W^{1,1}(I)
and
\mathcal{B}_{2}|_{r=0}=0

for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, which allows us to apply Lemma
A.4
to obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}(\mathcal{B}_{2})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\mathcal{B}_{2}|_{r=0}=0.

(4.24)

On one hand,
\mathcal{B}_{2}|_{r=0}=0
follows from the fact
that
(\rho,u,u_{r})\in C(\bar{I})
for each
t\in(0,T]

due to (
4.4
) (or (
4.6
)).
On the other hand, it follows from (
4.3
)–(
4.4
)
(or (
4.5
)–(
4.6
)) that

r^{m}\rho\in L^{1}(I),\quad\Big(\rho,\rho_{r},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r}\Big)\in L^{\infty}(I),\quad r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big((\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r},u,u_{r}\big)\in L^{2}(I)

for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
.
Thus, we obtain from the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{2}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|r^{m}\big(\rho|u|^{p-1}|u_{r}|,\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-1},\rho|u|^{p+1}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|r^{m}\rho|_{1}|u|_{\infty}^{p-1}|u_{r}|_{\infty}+C_{0}|r^{m}\rho|_{1}|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|u|_{\infty}^{p-1}+C_{0}|r^{m}\rho|_{1}|u|_{\infty}^{p+1}<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{B}_{2})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|r^{m-1}\big(\rho|u|^{p-1}|u_{r}|,\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-1},\rho|u|^{p+1}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big|r^{m}\big(\rho_{r}|u|^{p-1}u_{r},\rho|u|^{p-2}u_{r}^{2},\rho|u|^{p-1}u_{rr}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big|r^{m}\big(\rho^{\gamma-1}\rho_{r}|u|^{p-1},\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u_{r},\rho_{r}|u|^{p+1},\rho|u|^{p}u_{r}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|r^{m}\rho|_{1}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}\big(|u_{r}|_{\infty}+|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}+|u|_{\infty}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\big(|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+|\rho|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}^{2}+|\rho|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big(|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}\big|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}+|r^{m}\rho|_{1}|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}|u_{r}|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big(|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|u|^{p-1}_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}+|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-1}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}\big)<\infty.

Then integrating (
4.23
) over
I
, together with (
4.24
), leads to

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+2\alpha(p-1)\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+2\alpha m\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\\
&=(p-1)A\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r+mA\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m-1}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u\,\mathrm{d}r:=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{G}_{i}.\end{split}

(4.25)

2. Estimate of
\mathcal{G}_{1}
.

For
\mathcal{G}_{1}
, it follows from Lemmas
4.2
–
4.3
,
and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big|_{2}

(4.26)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{8}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}u^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+C(p)\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big|_{2}^{2}+C(p)\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{8}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}u^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+C(p)\Big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{p+m-2}{p\gamma-p+1}}\rho\Big|_{p\gamma-p+1}^{\frac{2p\gamma-2p+2}{p}}\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p-2}

\displaystyle+C(p)|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{2m(1-\gamma)}|_{\infty}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{m}\rho|_{p\gamma-p+1}^{\frac{2p\gamma-2p+2}{p}}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p-2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{8}\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)+C(p)\Big(1+\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)

\displaystyle+C(p)\underline{\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{p+m-2}{p\gamma-p+1}}\rho\big|_{p\gamma-p+1}^{p\gamma-p+1}}_{:=\mathcal{G}_{1,1}}.

2.1. The estimate for
\mathcal{G}_{1,1}
when
n=2
or
n=3
and
\gamma\in(1,2]
.
Note that

\displaystyle\frac{p+m-2}{p\gamma-p+1}=\frac{p-1}{p\gamma-p+1}>0

\displaystyle\text{if $n=2,\ m=1$, and $\gamma\in(1,\infty)$};

\displaystyle\frac{p+m-2}{p\gamma-p+1}=\frac{p}{p\gamma-p+1}\geq 1-\frac{1}{p\gamma-p+1}

\displaystyle\text{if $n=3,\,m=2$, and $\gamma\in(1,2]$}.

Thus, we can simply use Lemma
4.3
to obtain

\mathcal{G}_{1,1}\leq C(p)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

(4.27)

2.2. The estimate for
\mathcal{G}_{1,1}
when
n=3
and
\gamma\in(2,3)
.
Set

a_{1}:=p,\quad\,\,b_{1}:=p\gamma-p+1.

It follows from Lemma
4.2
(with
\omega=\frac{1}{2}
), integration by parts, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that, for any
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1,1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}r^{a_{1}}\rho^{b_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}r=\frac{1}{a_{1}+1}\rho^{b_{1}}(1)-\frac{b_{1}p}{\gamma(a_{1}+1)}\int_{0}^{1}r^{a_{1}+1}\rho^{b_{1}-\frac{\gamma}{p}}(\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r

(4.28)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{a_{1}+1}\big|\chi_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\sharp}\rho\big|_{\infty}^{b_{1}}+\frac{b_{1}p}{\gamma(a_{1}+1)}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}r^{\frac{pa_{1}+p-2}{p-1}}\rho^{\frac{pb_{1}-\gamma}{p-1}}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\big|r^{\frac{2}{p}}(\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})_{r}\big|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(a_{1},b_{1})+\frac{C(p,a_{1},b_{1})}{\epsilon_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}r^{\frac{pa_{1}+p-2}{p-1}}\rho^{\frac{pb_{1}-\gamma}{p-1}}\,\mathrm{d}r+\epsilon_{1}\big|r^{\frac{2}{p}}(\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})_{r}\big|_{p}^{p}.

For
j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, define the following two sequences
\{a_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
and
\{b_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}

as

a_{j+1}=\frac{p}{p-1}a_{j}+\frac{p-2}{p-1},\quad\,\,b_{j+1}=\frac{p}{p-1}b_{j}-\frac{\gamma}{p-1}.

(4.29)

Then we can obtain from a calculation similar to (
4.28
) that,
for all
\epsilon_{j}\in(0,1)
,

\int_{0}^{1}r^{a_{j}}\rho^{b_{j}}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C(a_{j},b_{j})+\frac{C(p,a_{j},b_{j})}{\epsilon_{j}}\int_{0}^{1}r^{a_{j+1}}\rho^{b_{j+1}}\,\mathrm{d}r+\epsilon_{j}\big|r^{\frac{2}{p}}(\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})_{r}\big|_{p}^{p}.

(4.30)

Define
(\epsilon_{0},C(p,a_{0},b_{0}))=(1,1)
. Then collecting (
4.28
)–(
4.30
) yields that, for
j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1,1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{j}\frac{\prod_{l=0}^{k-1}C(p,a_{l},b_{l})C(a_{k},b_{k})}{\prod_{0=1}^{k-1}\epsilon_{l}}+\prod_{l=1}^{j}\frac{C(p,a_{l},b_{l})}{\epsilon_{l}}\,\underline{\int_{0}^{1}r^{a_{j+1}}\rho^{b_{j+1}}\,\mathrm{d}r}_{:=\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1,1}}

(4.31)

\displaystyle+\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{j}\frac{\prod_{l=0}^{k-1}C(p,a_{l},b_{l})}{\prod_{l=0}^{k-1}\epsilon_{l}}\epsilon_{k}\Big)\big|r^{\frac{2}{p}}(\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})_{r}\big|_{p}^{p}.

For the estimate of
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1,1}
, we can first obtain from (
4.29
) that, for
j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

a_{j+1}=2(p-1)\Big(\frac{p}{p-1}\Big)^{j}-p+2,\quad\,\,b_{j+1}=(p-1)(\gamma-1)\Big(\frac{p}{p-1}\Big)^{j}+\gamma.

(4.32)

Note that, for each
p\in[2,\infty)
and
\gamma\in(2,3)
,
both
\{a_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
and
\{b_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
are strictly increasing as
j\to\infty
.
Moreover, for these
(p,\gamma)
, since

\displaystyle\frac{a_{j+1}}{b_{j+1}}\geq 1-\frac{1}{b_{j+1}}

\displaystyle\iff\frac{a_{j+1}}{b_{j+1}-1}\geq 1

\displaystyle\iff\frac{2\Big((p-1)\big(\frac{p}{p-1}\big)^{j}+1\Big)-p}{(\gamma-1)\Big((p-1)\big(\frac{p}{p-1}\big)^{j}+1\Big)}\geq 1

\displaystyle\iff 3-\frac{p}{(p-1)\big(\frac{p}{p-1}\big)^{j}+1}\geq\gamma>2,

we can choose
j=j_{0}
to be sufficiently large such that

\frac{a_{j_{0}+1}}{b_{j_{0}+1}}\geq 1-\frac{1}{b_{j_{0}+1}}
.
Thus, fixing this constant
j_{0}
(depending only on
(p,\gamma)
),
we obtain from Lemma
10.4
that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1,1}=\int_{0}^{1}r^{a_{j_{0}+1}}\rho^{b_{j_{0}+1}}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C(p).

Substituting
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1,1}
into (
4.31
) then yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1,1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{j_{0}}\frac{\prod_{l=0}^{k-1}C(p,a_{l},b_{l})C(a_{k},b_{k})}{\prod_{0=1}^{k-1}\epsilon_{l}}+\prod_{l=1}^{j_{0}}\frac{C(p,a_{l},b_{l})}{\epsilon_{l}}\,C(p)

(4.33)

\displaystyle+\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{j_{0}}\frac{\prod_{l=0}^{k-1}C(p,a_{l},b_{l})}{\prod_{l=0}^{k-1}\epsilon_{l}}\epsilon_{k}\Big)\big|r^{\frac{2}{p}}(\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})_{r}\big|_{p}^{p}.

To further reduce the above inequality, for
\tilde{\epsilon}\in(0,1)
sufficiently small,
we set

\displaystyle\epsilon_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{j_{0}}\,\frac{\prod_{l=0}^{k-1}\epsilon_{l}}{\prod_{l=0}^{k-1}C(p,a_{l},b_{l})}=\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}^{2^{k-1}}}{j_{0}^{2^{k-1}}\prod_{l=0}^{k-1}C(p,a_{l},b_{l})^{2^{k-l-1}}}\qquad\text{for $1\leq k\leq j_{0}$}.

Then it follows from (
4.8
), (
4.33
),
and Lemma
4.3
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1,1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p,\tilde{\epsilon})+\sum_{k=1}^{j_{0}}\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{j_{0}}\big|r^{\frac{2}{p}}(\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})_{r}\big|_{p}^{p}=C(p,\tilde{\epsilon})+\tilde{\epsilon}\big|r^{\frac{2}{p}}(\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{p}})_{r}\big|_{p}^{p}

(4.34)

\displaystyle\leq C(p,\tilde{\epsilon})+C_{0}\tilde{\epsilon}\big(\big|(r^{2}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{2}{\gamma-1}}\rho\big|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u|_{p}^{p}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(p,\tilde{\epsilon})+C_{0}\tilde{\epsilon}\big(\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\big).

Thus, collecting (
4.26
)–(
4.27
) and (
4.34
) leads to

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{8}\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)+C(p)\big(1+\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\big)

(4.35)

\displaystyle+C(p,\tilde{\epsilon})+C(p)\tilde{\epsilon}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}\qquad\text{for all $\tilde{\epsilon}\in(0,1)$}.

3. Estimate of
\mathcal{G}_{2}
.
This can be derived similarly from Lemmas
4.2
–
4.3
, the bound of
\mathcal{G}_{1,1}
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities as follows: for any
\tilde{\epsilon}\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{m-1}{p-1}}\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{p-1}}u\big|_{p-1}^{p-1}+C_{0}\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m-1}{p-1}}\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{p-1}}u\big|_{p-1}^{p-1}

(4.36)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{p+m-2}{p\gamma-p+1}}\rho\Big|_{p\gamma-p+1}^{\frac{p\gamma-p+1}{p}}\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p-1}

\displaystyle+C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{m(1-\gamma)-1}|_{\infty}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{m}\rho|_{p\gamma-p+1}^{\frac{p\gamma-p+1}{p}}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{8}\big|(r^{{m-2}}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\big(1+\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\big)+C(p)\mathcal{G}_{1,1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{4}\big|(r^{{m-2}}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\big(1+\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\big)+C(p,\tilde{\epsilon})+C(p)\tilde{\epsilon}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}.

Setting
\tilde{\epsilon}=C(p)^{-1}\epsilon
in (
4.35
)–(
4.36
) for sufficiently small
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and then substituting (
4.35
)–(
4.36
) into (
4.25
) lead to the desired conclusion.

The proof of Lemma
4.4
is completed.
∎

In addition, we can show the following
L^{p}(I)
-estimates of
(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}v
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
. For any
p\in[2,\infty)
, there exists a constant
C(p)>0
such that,
for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{A\gamma p}{4\alpha}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}\leq C(p)\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big).

(4.37)

Proof.

First, multiplying (
4.15
) by
r^{m}\rho|v|^{p-2}v
with
p\in[2,\infty)
,
along with
\eqref{e1.5}_{1}
, gives

\frac{1}{p}\big(r^{m}\rho|v|^{p}\big)_{t}+\frac{1}{p}\big(\underline{r^{m}\rho u|v|^{p}}_{:=\mathcal{B}_{3}}\big)_{r}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}|v|^{p}=\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}uv|v|^{p-2}.

(4.38)

Here we need to show that
\mathcal{B}_{3}\in W^{1,1}(I)
and
\mathcal{B}_{3}|_{r=0}=0
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, which allows us to apply Lemma
A.4
to obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}(\mathcal{B}_{3})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\mathcal{B}_{3}|_{r=0}=0.

(4.39)

On the one hand, we can obtain
\mathcal{B}_{3}|_{r=0}=0

from (
4.8
) and the fact that
(\rho,\rho_{r},u)\in L^{\infty}(I)

for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
due to (
4.3
)–(
4.4
)
or (
4.5
)–(
4.6
).
On the other hand,
based on (
4.3
)–(
4.4
) (or (
4.5
)–(
4.6
)), we have

r^{m}\rho\in L^{1}(I),\quad r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\rho_{r},u,(\log\rho)_{rr}\big)\in L^{2}(I),\quad\Big(u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r},(\log\rho)_{r}\Big)\in L^{\infty}(I)

for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
. Thus, we obtain from the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{3}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big(\big|r^{m}\rho|u|^{p+1}\big|_{1}+\big|r^{m}\rho u|(\log\rho)_{r}|^{p}\big|_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big(|r^{m}\rho|_{1}|u|_{\infty}^{p+1}+|r^{m}\rho|_{1}|u|_{\infty}|(\log\rho)_{r}|_{\infty}^{p}\big)<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{B}_{3})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big(\big|r^{m-1}\rho|u|^{p+1}\big|_{1}+\big|r^{m-1}\rho u|(\log\rho)_{r}|^{p}\big|_{1}+\big|r^{m}\rho_{r}|u|^{p+1}\big|_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big(\big|r^{m}\rho_{r}u|(\log\rho)_{r}|^{p}\big|_{1}+\big|r^{m}\rho|u|^{p}u_{r}\big|_{1}+\big|r^{m}\rho u_{r}|(\log\rho)_{r}|^{p}\big|_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big|r^{m}\rho u|(\log\rho)_{r}|^{p-1}|(\log\rho)_{rr}|\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)|r^{m}\rho|_{1}\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}\big(|u|_{\infty}^{p}+|(\log\rho)_{r}|_{\infty}^{p}\big)+C(p)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|u|_{\infty}^{p}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|(\log\rho)_{r}|_{\infty}^{p}+C(p)|r^{m}\rho|_{1}|u_{r}|_{\infty}\big(|u|_{\infty}^{p}+|(\log\rho)_{r}|_{\infty}^{p}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|(\log\rho)_{r}|_{\infty}^{p-1}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\log\rho)_{rr}|_{2}<\infty.

Integrating (
4.38
) over
I
, we obtain from (
4.39
),

\frac{2}{\gamma-1}>1
whenever
\gamma\in(1,3)
,
Lemmas
4.2
–
4.4
, and the Young inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}uv|v|^{p-2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A\gamma}{4\alpha}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A\gamma}{4\alpha}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\Big(\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A\gamma}{4\alpha}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{2}{\gamma-1}}\rho\big|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\quad\,+C(p)|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-m}|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{m}\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A\gamma}{4\alpha}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big).

The proof of Lemma
4.5
is completed.
∎

Consequently, based on Lemmas
4.4
–
4.5
,
we can derive the following important estimates on
(u,v)
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
.
Then, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, there exists a constant
C(p,T)>0
such that,
for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u(t)\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}v(t)\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(p,T).

Proof.

First, multiplying (
4.37
) by
\frac{8\alpha\epsilon}{A\gamma p}

with
\epsilon\in(0,1)
leads to

\frac{8\alpha\epsilon}{A\gamma p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+2\epsilon\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}\leq C(p)\epsilon\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big).

Then summing the above inequality with (
4.22
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{8\alpha\epsilon}{A\gamma p}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)

\displaystyle+p\alpha\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)+\epsilon\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\Big(1+\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)+C(p,\epsilon)+C(p)\epsilon\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}.

As a consequence, one can set

\epsilon=\min\big\{\frac{\alpha}{C(p)},\frac{1}{2}\big\},

and then apply the Grönwall inequality to the resulting inequality to obtain
the desired result.
To achieve this, it still requires checking the
L^{p}(I)
-boundedness
of
(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}(u_{0},v_{0})
.
Indeed, it follows from Lemmas
A.1
,
A.11
, and
B.1
that

\displaystyle\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}(u_{0},v_{0})\big|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq|r^{m}\rho_{0}|_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}}|(u_{0},(\log\rho_{0})_{r})|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1}{p}}\|(\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\nabla\log\rho_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(p).

This completes the proof.
∎

We now show the uniform
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate of the density.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
. Then there exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

|\rho(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for any $t\in[0,T]$}.

Proof.

Based on Lemma
4.2
, it suffices to show the
L^{\infty}(0,1)
-estimate of
\rho
:

|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

(4.40)

It follows from Lemma
4.6
that, for
n=2,3
, there exists a constant
p_{0}>n
such that

\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p_{0}}}(u,v)(t)\big|_{p_{0}}\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

(4.41)

Thus, we obtain from Lemma
4.3
, (
4.8
), (
4.41
),

\frac{m}{p_{0}-1}\in(0,1)
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that,
for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho|_{1}+|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho_{r}|_{1}\big)\leq C_{0}\big(1+|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho(u,v)|_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}+C_{0}\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{-\frac{m}{p_{0}-1}}\big|_{1}^{\frac{p_{0}-1}{p_{0}}}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho|_{\infty}^{\frac{p_{0}-1}{p_{0}}}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p_{0}}}(u,v)\big|_{p_{0}}\leq C(T)+\frac{1}{2}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho|_{\infty},

which implies (
4.40
).
∎

5.
Global Uniform Bound of the Effective Velocity

This section is devoted to establishing the global uniform
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate of the effective velocity
v
in spherical coordinates for the case that
\bar{\rho}=0
.
Let
T>0
be any fixed time, and let
(\rho,u)(t,r)
be the
s
-order
(s=2,3)

regular solution of problem (
4.2
) in
[0,T]\times I

obtained in Theorems
4.1
–
4.2
.
Moreover, throughout §
5
–§
9
, we always assume that

\gamma\in(1,\infty)\,\,\,\,\text{when $n=2$},\qquad\,\,\gamma\in(1,3)\,\,\,\,\text{when $n=3$}.

(5.1)

To obtain the boundedness of
v
, we first derives the following
L^{p}(I)
-estimates of
\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u
:

Lemma 5.1
.

For any
p\in[2,\infty)
,
there exists a constant
C(p,T)>0
such that,
for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u(t)\big|_{p}^{p}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(p,T)\Big(\big(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|v|_{\infty}\big)^{2}+1\Big).

Proof.

Multiplying both sides of
\eqref{e1.5}_{2}
by
|u|^{p-2}u
with
p\in[2,\infty)
, together with
\eqref{e1.5}_{1}
and (
4.8
), leads to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}(\rho|u|^{p})_{t}+2\alpha(p-1)\rho|u|^{p-2}|u_{r}|^{2}+\frac{2\alpha m(p-1)}{p}\frac{\rho|u|^{p}}{r^{2}}-A(p-1)\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u_{r}

(5.2)

\displaystyle=\Big(\underline{2\alpha\rho u_{r}|u|^{p-2}u+\frac{2\alpha m}{p}\frac{\rho|u|^{p}}{r}-\frac{1}{p}\rho u|u|^{p}-A\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u}_{:=\mathcal{B}_{4}}\Big)_{r}-\frac{m}{p}\frac{\rho v|u|^{p}}{r}.

Here we need to show that
\mathcal{B}_{4}\in W^{1,1}(I)

and
\mathcal{B}_{4}|_{r=0}=0
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
,
which allows us to apply Lemma
A.4
to obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}(\mathcal{B}_{4})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\mathcal{B}_{4}|_{r=0}=0.

(5.3)

On one hand,
\mathcal{B}_{4}|_{r=0}=0
follows directly from
(
4.8
),
p\geq 2
,
u|_{r=0}=0
,
and the fact that
(\rho,u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r})\in C(\bar{I})
for each
t\in(0,T]
due to (
4.4
) (or (
4.6
)).
On the other hand, if
n=2

(m=1)
, (
4.3
)–(
4.4
)
or (
4.5
)–(
4.6
) imply that

\sqrt{r}\Big(u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r},u_{rr}\Big)\in L^{2}(I),\quad\Big(\rho,u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r},(\log\rho)_{r}\Big)\in L^{\infty}(I),\quad r\rho\in L^{1}(I)

for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, so that

|\rho|_{1}\leq|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho|_{1}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}\rho|_{1}\leq|\rho|_{\infty}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-1}|_{\infty}|r\rho|_{1}<\infty,

which, along with the Hölder inequality, yields that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{4}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\Big(\big|\rho|u|^{p-1}|u_{r}|\big|_{1}+\Big|\frac{1}{r}\rho|u|^{p}\Big|_{1}+\big|\rho|u|^{p+1}\big|_{1}+\big|\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-1}\big|_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\Big(\Big|\frac{u}{\sqrt{r}}\Big|_{2}|\sqrt{r}u_{r}|_{2}+\Big|\frac{u}{\sqrt{r}}\Big|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-1}|\sqrt{r}u|_{2}\Big|\frac{u}{\sqrt{r}}\Big|_{2}+C(p)|r\rho|_{1}|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{B}_{4})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|\big(|\rho_{r}||u_{r}||u|^{p-1},\rho|u_{rr}||u|^{p-1},\rho u_{r}^{2}|u|^{p-2}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\Big|\Big(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\rho|u|^{p},\frac{1}{r}\rho_{r}|u|^{p},\frac{1}{r}\rho|u|^{p-1}|u_{r}|\Big)\Big|_{1}+C(p)\big|\big(|\rho_{r}||u|^{p+1},\rho|u|^{p}|u_{r}|\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big|\big(\rho^{\gamma-1}|\rho_{r}||u|^{p-1},\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}|u_{r}|\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\Big|\frac{u}{\sqrt{r}}\Big|_{2}\!\big(|(\log\rho)_{r}|_{\infty}|\sqrt{r}u_{r}|_{2}\!+\!|\sqrt{r}u_{rr}|_{2}\big)\!+\!C(p)|\rho|_{1}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}|u_{r}|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|\rho|_{1}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}\Big(\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}+|(\log\rho)_{r}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}+|u_{r}|_{\infty}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big(|\rho|_{1}|u|_{\infty}^{p}+|\rho|_{1}|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\big)\big(|(\log\rho)_{r}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}+|u_{r}|_{\infty}\big)<\infty.

Furthermore, if
n=3

(m=2)
, (
4.3
)–(
4.4
) or (
4.5
)–(
4.6
) imply that

(u,ru_{r},ru_{rr})\in L^{2}(I),\quad\big(\rho,u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r},(\log\rho)_{r}\big)\in L^{\infty}(I),\quad r^{2}\rho\in L^{1}(I)

for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, so that

\displaystyle|\rho|_{1}\leq|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho|_{1}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}\rho|_{1}\leq|\rho|_{\infty}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-2}|_{\infty}|r^{2}\rho|_{1}<\infty,\qquad|\rho|_{2}\leq|\rho|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\rho|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}<\infty,

which, along with the Hölder inequality, yields that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{4}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\Big(\big|\rho|u|^{p-1}|u_{r}|\big|_{1}+\Big|\frac{1}{r}\rho|u|^{p}\Big|_{1}+\big|\rho|u|^{p+1}\big|_{1}+\big|\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-1}\big|_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(p)|\rho|_{1}|u|_{\infty}^{p-1}\Big(\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{\infty}+|u|_{\infty}^{2}+|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\Big)<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{B}_{4})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|\big(|\rho_{r}||u_{r}||u|^{p-1},\rho|u_{rr}||u|^{p-1},\rho u_{r}^{2}|u|^{p-2}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\Big|\Big(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\rho|u|^{p},\frac{1}{r}\rho_{r}|u|^{p},\frac{1}{r}\rho|u|^{p-1}|u_{r}|\Big)\Big|_{1}\!\!+\!C(p)\big|\big(|\rho_{r}||u|^{p+1},\rho|u|^{p}|u_{r}|\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big|\big(\rho^{\gamma-1}|\rho_{r}||u|^{p-1},\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}|u_{r}|\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)|\rho|_{2}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}\big(|(\log\rho)_{r}|_{\infty}|ru_{r}|_{2}+|ru_{rr}|_{2}\big)+C(p)|\rho|_{1}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}|u_{r}|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|\rho|_{1}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}\Big(\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}+|(\log\rho)_{r}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}+|u_{r}|_{\infty}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big(|\rho|_{1}|u|_{\infty}^{p}+|\rho|_{1}|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\big)\big(|(\log\rho)_{r}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}+|u_{r}|_{\infty}\big)<\infty.

As a consequence, integrating (
5.2
) over
I
, we obtain from (
5.3
),
Lemmas
4.2
and
4.7
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+2\alpha(p-1)\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\frac{2\alpha m(p-1)}{p}\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}

(5.4)

\displaystyle=A(p-1)\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r\underline{-\frac{m}{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\rho v|u|^{p}}{r}\,\mathrm{d}r}_{:=\mathcal{G}_{3}}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big(\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big|_{2}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}\rho^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big|_{2}\big)\,\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}+\mathcal{G}_{3}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\Big(\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho|_{\infty}^{\frac{p\gamma-p+1}{p}}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-m}|_{\infty}^{\frac{p\gamma-p+1}{p}}\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{m}\rho|_{p\gamma-p+1}^{\frac{p\gamma-p+1}{p}}\Big)\,\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}+\mathcal{G}_{3}

\displaystyle\leq C(p,T)\big(1+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\big)+\frac{\alpha}{8}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\mathcal{G}_{3}.

For
\mathcal{G}_{3}
, if
n=2
(
m=1
), we directly have

\mathcal{G}_{3}=-\frac{1}{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\rho v|u|^{p}}{r}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C(p)|v|_{\infty}\big|(r^{-1}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\leq C(p)(|v|_{\infty}^{2}+1)\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p};

(5.5)

while, if
n=3
(
m=2
), it follows from the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\rho v|u|^{p}}{r}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C(p)|v|_{\infty}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{\frac{p}{2}}\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{\frac{p}{2}}

(5.6)

\displaystyle\leq C(p)|v|_{\infty}^{2}\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{\alpha}{8}\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}.

Thus, collecting (
5.4
)–(
5.6
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}

(5.7)

\displaystyle\leq C(p,T)\big(1+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\big)+C(p)(|v|_{\infty}^{2}+1)\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p},

which, along with the Grönwall inequality and Lemma
4.6
, yields that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u(t)\big|_{p}^{p}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(p,T)\Big(\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p}}u_{0}\big|_{p}^{p}+\big(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|v|_{\infty}\big)^{2}+1\Big)\leq C(p,T)\Big(\big(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|v|_{\infty}\big)^{2}+1\Big),

provided that the
L^{p}(I)
-boundedness of
\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p}}u_{0}
can be obtained.
Indeed, it follows from Lemmas
A.1
,
A.11
, and
B.1
that

\displaystyle\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p}}u_{0}\big|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p}}u_{0}\big|_{p}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p}}u_{0}\big|_{p}\leq C_{0}|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{p}}|u_{0}|_{\infty}+|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{p}}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{p}}|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m}{p}}u_{0}\big|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{p}}\big)\leq C(p)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{H^{2}}\leq C(p).

The proof of Lemma
5.1
is completed.
∎

Next, we obtain the
L^{1}([0,T];L^{\infty}(I))
-estimate of
\rho^{\gamma-1}u
.

Lemma 5.2
.

For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
C(\epsilon,T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\int_{0}^{t}|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(\epsilon,T)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}|v|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)+\epsilon\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|v|_{\infty}.

Proof.

Let
q\geq 2
be determined later.
First, it follows from (
4.8
), Lemma
A.1
, and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}^{q}

\displaystyle=\big||\rho^{\gamma-1}u|^{q}\big|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{q\gamma-q}|u|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}r+C_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}|(\rho^{q\gamma-q}|u|^{q})_{r}|\,\mathrm{d}r

(5.8)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{q\gamma-q}|u|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}r+C(q)\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{q\gamma-q}(|v|+|u|)|u|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+C(q)\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{q\gamma-q}|u|^{q-1}|u_{r}|\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(q)(1+|v|_{\infty})\big|\rho^{q\gamma-q}u^{q}\big|_{1}+C(q)\big|\rho^{q\gamma-q}u^{q+1}\big|_{1}

\displaystyle+C(q)\big|\rho^{2q\gamma-2q-1}u^{q}\big|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{q-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(q)(1+|v|_{\infty})|\rho|_{\infty}^{q\gamma-q-1}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{q}}u\big|_{q}^{q}+C|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}|\rho|_{\infty}^{(q-1)\gamma-q}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{q}}u\big|_{q}^{q}

\displaystyle+C(q)|\rho|_{\infty}^{q\gamma-q-1}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{q}}u\big|_{q}^{\frac{q}{2}}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{q-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}.

Next, setting
q=\tilde{q}\geq 2
in (
5.8
) large enough such that

\gamma>\frac{\tilde{q}}{\tilde{q}-1}>1,

we obtain from the resulting inequality, Lemma
4.7
, and the Young inequality that

\displaystyle|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}^{\tilde{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)(1+|v|_{\infty})\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}+C(T)\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\frac{\tilde{q}^{2}}{\tilde{q}-1}}

\displaystyle\quad+C(T)\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\frac{\tilde{q}}{2}}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}+\frac{1}{2}|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}^{\tilde{q}},

which, along with the fact that
\tilde{q}\geq 2
, Lemma
4.6
, and the Young inequality, leads to

\displaystyle|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(\big(1+|v|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}\big)\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\frac{\tilde{q}}{\tilde{q}-1}}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+|v|_{\infty}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{\frac{2}{2\tilde{q}-1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+|v|_{\infty}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{\frac{2}{2\tilde{q}-1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+|v|_{\infty}+|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{2-m}|_{\infty}\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(T)\Big(|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-m}|\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{\frac{2}{2\tilde{q}-1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+|v|_{\infty}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{\frac{2}{2\tilde{q}-1}}\Big).

Finally, integrating above over
[0,t]
, we obtain from Lemmas
4.6
and
5.1
,
and the Hölder and Young inequalities that, for all
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\int_{0}^{t}\Big(1+|v|_{\infty}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{\frac{2}{2\tilde{q}-1}}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}|v|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)+C(T)\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2\tilde{q}-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}|v|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)+C(T)\Big(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|v|_{\infty}\Big)^{\frac{2}{2\tilde{q}-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\epsilon,T)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}|v|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)+\epsilon\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|v|_{\infty}.

The proof of Lemma
5.2
is completed.
∎

Now, the uniform
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate of the effective velocity can be derived as follows:

Lemma 5.3
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

|v(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for any $t\in[0,T]$}.

Proof.

First, define the flow map
y=\eta(t,r):[0,T]\times I\to I
as

\eta_{t}(t,r)=u(t,\eta(t,r))\qquad\text{with $\eta(0,r)=r$}.

(5.9)

Then (
4.15
), together with (
5.9
), implies the following ODE:

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}v(t,\eta(t,r))+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}(\rho^{\gamma-1}v)(t,\eta(t,r))=\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}(\rho^{\gamma-1}u)(t,\eta(t,r)),

which, along with the characteristic method, yields that

\displaystyle v(t,\eta(t,r))

\displaystyle=v_{0}(r)\exp\Big(-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\rho^{\gamma-1}(\tau,\eta(\tau,r))\,\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)

(5.10)

\displaystyle+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\int_{0}^{t}(\rho^{\gamma-1}u)(s,\eta(s,r))\,\exp\Big(-\int_{s}^{t}\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\rho^{\gamma-1}(\tau,\eta(\tau,r))\,\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s.

Since
\rho\geq 0
, it follows from (
5.10
) and Lemma
5.2
that, for all
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|v|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Big(|v_{0}|_{\infty}+\int_{0}^{t}|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\Big)

(5.11)

\displaystyle\leq C(\epsilon,T)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}|v|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)+C_{0}\epsilon\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|v|_{\infty},

where the
L^{\infty}(I)
-norm of
v_{0}
can be derived from Lemmas
A.1
,
A.11
,
and
B.1
as

|v_{0}|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}|(u_{0},(\log\rho_{0})_{r})|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}\|(\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\nabla\log\rho_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{0}.

Finally, letting
\epsilon=\frac{1}{2C_{0}}
in (
5.11
)
and applying the Grönwall inequality to (
5.11
) lead to
the desired conclusion.
∎

6.
Non-Formation of Cavitation
inside the Fluids in Finite Time

This section is devoted to showing that the cavitation does not form inside the fluids in finite
time for the case that
\bar{\rho}=0
, and establishing the lower bound estimates of the density.
Let
T>0
be any fixed time, and let
(\rho,u)(t,r)
be the
s
-order
(s=2,3)
regular solution of
problem (
4.2
) in
[0,T]\times I
obtained in Theorems
4.1
–
4.2
.
Moreover, throughout this section, we always assume that (
5.1
) holds.

We first show the following
L^{2}(I)
-estimate of
u
.

Lemma 6.1
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

|(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)(t)|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}+|u|^{2}_{\infty}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T)\qquad\mbox{for any $t\in[0,T]$}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1.

First, multiplying
\eqref{e1.5}_{2}
by
u
, along with (
4.8
), gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(u^{2})_{t}+2\alpha|u_{r}|^{2}+\alpha m\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}

(6.1)

\displaystyle=\Big(\underline{2\alpha u_{r}u+\alpha m\frac{u^{2}}{r}-\frac{2}{3}u^{3}}_{:=\mathcal{B}_{5}}\Big)_{r}-\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\rho^{\gamma-1}(v-u)u+vu_{r}u.

We need to show that
\mathcal{B}_{5}\in W^{1,1}(I)
and
\mathcal{B}_{5}|_{r=0}=0
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, which allows us to apply Lemma
A.4
to obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}(\mathcal{B}_{5})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\mathcal{B}_{5}|_{r=0}=0.

(6.2)

On one hand,
\mathcal{B}_{5}|_{r=0}=0
follows from
u|_{r=0}=0

and the fact that
(u,u_{r})\in C(\bar{I})
for each
t\in(0,T]
due to (
4.4
) or (
4.6
).
On the other hand, it follows from (
4.3
)–(
4.4
) or (
4.5
)–(
4.6
) that

(u,u_{r})\in L^{\infty}(I),\quad r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u,u_{r},u_{rr},u_{rrr})\in L^{2}(I)\qquad\,\mbox{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$}.

Then we obtain from Lemma
A.3
that

\displaystyle|(u,u_{r},u_{rr})|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}(u,u_{r},u_{rr})\big|_{2}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}(u,u_{r},u_{rr})\big|_{2}

(6.3)

\displaystyle\leq\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r(u,u_{r},u_{rr},u_{rrr})\big|_{2}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}(u,u_{r},u_{rr})\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{2-m}{2}}|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u,u_{r},u_{rr},u_{rrr})\big|_{2}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u,u_{r},u_{rr})\big|_{2}<\infty,

which, along with the Hölder’s inequality, leads to

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{5}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|u|_{2}\big(|u_{r}|_{2}+|u|_{2}+|u|_{2}|u|_{\infty}\big)<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{B}_{5})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|\big((u_{r})^{2},u_{rr}u,uu_{r},u^{2}u_{r}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(|u_{r}|_{2}^{2}+|u|_{2}|u_{rr}|_{2}+|u|_{2}|u_{r}|_{2}+|u|_{\infty}|u|_{2}|u_{r}|_{2}\big)<\infty.

Then integrating (
6.1
) over
I
, together with (
6.2
), yields

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|u|_{2}^{2}+2\alpha|u_{r}|_{2}^{2}+\alpha m\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{2}^{2}=-\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{\gamma-1}(v-u)u\,\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{\infty}vu_{r}u\,\mathrm{d}r:=\sum_{i=4}^{5}\mathcal{G}_{i}.

(6.4)

It follows from Lemmas
4.7
and
5.3
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{4}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(|\rho^{\gamma-1}v|_{2}|u|_{2}+|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|u|_{2}^{2}\big)\leq C(T)|(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)|_{2}^{2},

(6.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{5}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|v|_{\infty}|u|_{2}|u_{r}|_{2}\leq\frac{\alpha}{8}|u_{r}|_{2}^{2}+C(T)|u|_{2}^{2}.

Combining with (
6.4
)–(
6.5
) yields

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|u|_{2}^{2}+\alpha\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\leq C(T)|(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)|_{2}^{2}.

(6.6)

2.
For the
L^{2}(I)
-estimate of
\rho^{\gamma-1}v
,
we first multiply (
4.15
) by
\rho^{2\gamma-2}v
and then obtain from
\eqref{e1.5}_{1}
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2})_{t}+\frac{1}{2}(\underline{u\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2}}_{:=\mathcal{B}_{6}})_{r}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\rho^{3\gamma-3}v^{2}

(6.7)

\displaystyle=\big(\frac{3}{2}-\gamma\big)\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2}u_{r}-(\gamma-1)m\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2}\frac{u}{r}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\rho^{3\gamma-3}vu.

We need to show that
\mathcal{B}_{6}\in W^{1,1}(I)
and
\mathcal{B}_{6}|_{r=0}=0

for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, which allows us to apply Lemma
A.4
to obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}(\mathcal{B}_{6})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\mathcal{B}_{6}|_{r=0}=0.

(6.8)

On one hand, to obtain
\mathcal{B}_{6}|_{r=0}=0
, we first note that
v=u+2\alpha(\log\rho)_{r}
and

(u,(\log\rho)_{r})\in L^{\infty}(I)\qquad\mbox{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$}

due to (
4.3
)–(
4.4
) or (
4.5
)–(
4.6
), which implies that

v\in L^{\infty}(I)\qquad\text{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$}.

(6.9)

Then it follows from
u|_{r=0}
and
(\rho,u)\in C(\bar{I})
for each
t\in(0,T]
(due to (
4.4
) or (
4.6
)) that
\mathcal{B}_{6}|_{r=0}=0
.
On the other hand, it follows from (
4.3
)–(
4.4
) (or (
4.5
)–(
4.6
))
and (
6.3
) that

\displaystyle(u,u_{r},u_{rr})\in L^{2}(I),\quad(\rho,u,u_{r},(\log\rho)_{r})\in L^{\infty}(I),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u,u_{r},(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r},\frac{1}{r}(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r},(\log\rho)_{rr}\Big)\in L^{2}(I)

for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
. Thus, we obtain from Lemma
A.3
that

\displaystyle|(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}\big|_{2}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{2-m}{2}}|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}\big|_{2}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}\big|_{2}<\infty,

which, along with (
4.8
) and the Hölder and Young inequalities, yields that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{6}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|u|_{\infty}\big(|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}|u|_{2}^{2}+|(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}|_{2}^{2}\big)\leq C_{0}|(u,(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r})|_{2}^{2}<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{B}_{6})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|\big(u_{r}\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2},u\rho^{\gamma-1}(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}v^{2},u\rho^{2\gamma-2}vv_{r}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|u_{r}|_{\infty}\big(|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}|u|_{2}^{2}+|(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}|_{2}^{2}\big)+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}|_{2}|u|_{2}|(u,(\log\rho)_{r})|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{2}|(u,(\log\rho)_{r})|_{\infty}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r(u_{r},(\log\rho)_{rr})|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}|u|_{2}|(u,(\log\rho)_{r})|_{\infty}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}(u_{r},(\log\rho)_{rr})|_{2})

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|u_{r}|_{\infty}\big(|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}|u|_{2}^{2}+|(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}|_{2}^{2}\big)+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}|_{2}|u|_{2}|(u,(\log\rho)_{r})|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}|u_{r}|_{2}|(u,(\log\rho)_{r})|_{\infty}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{2-m}{2}}|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{r},(\log\rho)_{rr})\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}|u|_{2}|(u,(\log\rho)_{r})|_{\infty}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{r},(\log\rho)_{rr})\big|_{2}<\infty.

Thus, integrating (
6.7
) over
I
, we obtain from (
6.8
), Lemmas
4.7
and
5.3
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|\rho^{\gamma-1}v|_{2}^{2}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\big|\rho^{\frac{3\gamma-3}{2}}v\big|_{2}^{2}

(6.10)

\displaystyle=\big(\frac{3}{2}-\gamma\big)\int\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2}u_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r-(\gamma-1)m\int\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2}\frac{u}{r}\,\mathrm{d}r+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\int\rho^{3\gamma-3}vu\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|v|_{\infty}|\rho^{\gamma-1}v|_{2}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}|\rho^{\gamma-1}v|_{2}|u|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)|(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{8}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}.

Combining (
6.6
) with (
6.10
) gives

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\leq C(T)|(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)|_{2}^{2}.

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, yields that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

|(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)(t)|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

(6.11)

We still needs to check the
L^{2}(I)
-boundedness of
(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}v_{0},u_{0})
.
Indeed, it follows from Lemmas
A.1
,
A.11
, and
B.1
that

\displaystyle|u_{0}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\chi_{1}^{\flat}u_{0}|_{2}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}u_{0}|_{2}\leq|u_{0}|_{\infty}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{0}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\leq C_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{H^{2}}\leq C_{0},

\displaystyle|\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}v_{0}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}(u_{0},(\log\rho_{0})_{r})|_{2}\leq C_{0}\big(|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|u_{0}|_{2}+|(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{r}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}+C_{0}\big(|\chi_{1}^{\flat}(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{r}|_{2}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{r}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}+C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{2-m}{2}}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{r}|_{2}+C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{r}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Big(|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}+\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{r}}{r},(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\gamma-1}+\|\nabla(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})\|_{H^{1}}\big)\leq C_{0}.

Finally, it follows from (
6.11
) and Lemma
A.1
that

\int_{0}^{t}|u|^{2}_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{t}|(u,u_{r})|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C_{0}\Big(t\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|u|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}|u_{r}|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\leq C(T).

The proof of Lemma
6.1
is completed.
∎

Now, with the help of Lemmas
5.3
and
6.1
, we can show the pointwise estimates of
\rho

in the domain containing the origin.

Lemma 6.2
.

Suppose that

\inf_{z\in[0,r]}\rho_{0}(z)=\underline{\rho}(r)>0\qquad\text{for $r>0$},

(6.12)

with
\underline{\rho}(r)
, defined on
I
, satisfying
\underline{\rho}(r)\to 0
as
r\to\infty
.
Then, for any
R>0
, there exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

\begin{gathered}\inf_{(t,r)\in[0,T]\times[0,R]}\rho(t,r)\geq\min\big\{C(T)^{-1},\big(e^{-1}\underline{\rho}(R)\big)^{C(T)(\sqrt{R}+1)}\big\}.\end{gathered}

In particular, the cavitation does not form in
[0,T]\times\{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\,|\boldsymbol{x}|\leq R\}

for any
R>0
.

Proof.

First, it follows from (
4.8
) and Lemmas
5.3
and
6.1
that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

|\chi_{R}^{\flat}(\log\rho)_{r}(t)|_{2}\leq C_{0}|\chi_{R}^{\flat}(v,u)(t)|_{2}\leq C_{0}\big(\sqrt{R}|v(t)|_{\infty}+|u(t)|_{2}\big)\leq C(T)(\sqrt{R}+1).

(6.13)

Next, multiplying
\eqref{e1.5}_{1}
by
\chi_{R}^{\flat}\rho^{-1}\log\rho
and integrating over
I
, we obtain from (
6.13
) and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|\chi_{R}^{\flat}\log\rho|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{\infty}\chi_{R}^{\flat}u(\log\rho)_{r}\log\rho\,\mathrm{d}r-\int_{0}^{\infty}\chi_{R}^{\flat}\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)\log\rho\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq|u|_{\infty}|\chi_{R}^{\flat}(\log\rho)_{r}|_{2}|\chi_{R}^{\flat}\log\rho|_{2}+C_{0}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}|\chi_{R}^{\flat}\log\rho|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big((R+1)|u|_{\infty}^{2}+\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}+|\chi_{R}^{\flat}\log\rho|_{2}^{2}\Big),

which, along with (
6.12
), Lemmas
6.1
, and the Grönwall inequality, leads to

\displaystyle|\chi_{R}^{\flat}\log\rho(t)|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(|\chi_{R}^{\flat}\log\rho_{0}|_{2}^{2}+R+1\big)\leq C(T)\big(R|\chi_{R}^{\flat}\log\rho_{0}|_{\infty}^{2}+R+1\big).

(6.14)

Combining (
6.13
) with (
6.14
) and setting
R_{0}\geq 1
large enough
such that

\underline{\rho}(R)\leq\min\{1,|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}^{-1}\}\qquad\text{for $R\geq R_{0}$},

together with Lemma
A.1
, yield that, for all
t\in[0,T]
and
R\geq R_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\chi_{R}^{\flat}\log\rho(t)|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{1+R}{R}}|\chi_{R}^{\flat}\log\rho(t)|_{2}+|\chi_{R}^{\flat}(\log\rho)_{r}(t)|_{2}\Big)

(6.15)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)(\sqrt{R}+1)\big(|\chi_{R}^{\flat}\log\rho_{0}|_{\infty}+1\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)(\sqrt{R}+1)\Big(\max\big\{\log\big(|\chi_{R}^{\flat}\rho_{0}|_{\infty}\big),\log\big(|\chi_{R}^{\flat}\rho_{0}^{-1}|_{\infty}\big)\big\}+1\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)(\sqrt{R}+1)\Big(\max\big\{\log(|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}),-\log\underline{\rho}(R)\big\}+1\Big)

\displaystyle=-C(T)(\sqrt{R}+1)\big(\log(\underline{\rho}(R))-1\big)=-C(T)(\sqrt{R}+1)\,\log\big(e^{-1}\underline{\rho}(R)\big),

which, along with
e^{-1}\underline{\rho}(R)<1
, implies that,
for all
(t,r)\in[0,T]\times[0,R]
and
R\geq R_{0}
,

\rho(t,r)\geq\big(e^{-1}\underline{\rho}(R)\big)^{C(T)(\sqrt{R}+1)}.

Finally, for
R\leq R_{0}
, it follows from Lemma
A.1
and (
6.13
)–(
6.14
) that

\displaystyle|\chi_{R}^{\flat}\log\rho(t)|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq|\chi_{R_{0}}^{\flat}\log\rho(t)|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{1+R_{0}}{R_{0}}}|\chi_{R_{0}}^{\flat}\log\rho(t)|_{2}+|\chi_{R_{0}}^{\flat}(\log\rho)_{r}(t)|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(|\chi_{R_{0}}^{\flat}\log\rho_{0}|_{\infty}+1\big)\leq C(T),

which implies that
\rho(t,r)\geq C(T)^{-1}
for all
(t,r)\in[0,T]\times[0,R]
and
R\leq R_{0}
.
∎

7.
Global Estimates for the
2
-Order
Regular Solutions with Far-Field Vacuum

The goal of this section is to establish the global-in-time uniform estimates for the
2
-order regular solutions
when
\bar{\rho}=0
.
Let
T>0
be any fixed time, and let
(\rho,u)(t,r)
be the
2
-order regular solution of problem (
4.2
) in
[0,T]\times I

obtained in Theorems
4.1
.
Moreover, throughout this section, we always assume that
(
5.1
) holds.

Next, we consider the enlarged system (
3.2
) in spherical coordinates.
Specifically, we introduce the following two important quantities:

\phi=\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\rho^{\gamma-1},\qquad\psi=\frac{1}{\gamma-1}(\log\phi)_{r}=(\log\rho)_{r}.

(7.1)

Then (
4.2
) can be rewritten as the problem of
(\phi,u,\psi)
in
[0,T]\times I
:

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\phi_{t}+u\phi_{r}+(\gamma-1)\phi\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)=0,\\[6.0pt]
\displaystyle u_{t}+uu_{r}+\phi_{r}=2\alpha\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)_{r}+2\alpha\psi u_{r},\\[6.0pt]
\displaystyle\psi_{t}+(\psi u)_{r}+\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)_{r}=0,\\[6.0pt]
\displaystyle(\phi,u,\psi)|_{t=0}=(\phi_{0},u_{0},\psi_{0})=(\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1},u_{0},(\log\rho_{0})_{r})\qquad\text{for $r\in I$},\\[6.0pt]
\displaystyle u|_{r=0}=0\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\text{for $t\in[0,T]$},\\[6.0pt]
\displaystyle(\phi,u)\to(0,0)\ \ \text{as $r\to\infty$}\qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T]$}.\end{cases}

(7.2)

Clearly, the effective velocity
v=u+2\alpha(\log\rho)_{r}
and its equation (
4.15
) take the following forms, respectively:

\displaystyle v=u+2\alpha\psi=u+\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma-1}(\log\phi)_{r},

(7.3)

\displaystyle v_{t}+uv_{r}+\frac{\gamma-1}{2\alpha}\phi(v-u)=0.

(7.4)

First, the following lemma will be frequently used in the later analysis, which can be seen as a consequence of the div-curl estimates for spherically symmetric functions in spherical coordinates.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
p\in(1,\infty)
and
\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{x})=f(r)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{r}\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
Then

\displaystyle\|\nabla\boldsymbol{f}\|_{L^{p}}\sim\Big|r^{\frac{m}{p}}\big(f_{r}+\frac{m}{r}f\big)\Big|_{p},\quad\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{L^{p}}\sim\Big|r^{\frac{m}{p}}\big(f_{r}+\frac{m}{r}f\big)_{r}\Big|_{p},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{L^{p}}\sim\Big|r^{\frac{m}{p}}\Big(\big(f_{r}+\frac{m}{r}f\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(f_{r}+\frac{m}{r}f\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{p},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{L^{p}}\sim\Big|r^{\frac{m}{p}}\Big(\big(f_{r}+\frac{m}{r}f\big)_{rrr},\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(f_{r}+\frac{m}{r}f\big)_{r}\Big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{p},

where
F_{1}\sim F_{2}
denotes that there exists a constant
C\geq 1
depending only on
(n,p)

such that
C^{-1}F_{1}\leq F_{2}\leq CF_{1}
. As a consequence, along with
Lemma
B.1
,
the following estimates hold
:

\displaystyle\Big|r^{\frac{m}{p}}\big(f_{r},\frac{f}{r}\big)\Big|_{p}\sim\Big|r^{\frac{m}{p}}\big(f_{r}+\frac{m}{r}f\big)\Big|_{p},\quad\Big|r^{\frac{m}{p}}\Big(f_{rr},\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{p}\sim\Big|r^{\frac{m}{p}}\big(f_{r}+\frac{m}{r}f\big)_{r}\Big|_{p},
