\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}u_{tt}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=-2\alpha\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\big(u_{tr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{t}\big)\big(\varphi_{r}+\frac{m}{r}\varphi\big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\big((uu_{r})_{t}+\phi_{tr}-2\alpha(\psi u_{r})_{t}\big)\varphi\,\mathrm{d}r.

Here, the process of integration by parts can be justified by following the similar discussions in (
7.33
)–(
7.38
).

Next, differentiating the above with respect to
t
leads to

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}u_{tt}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=-2\alpha\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\big(u_{ttr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{tt}\big)\big(\varphi_{r}+\frac{m}{r}\varphi\big)\,\mathrm{d}r

(8.37)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\big((uu_{r})_{tt}+\phi_{ttr}-2\alpha(\psi u_{r})_{tt}\big)\varphi\,\mathrm{d}r.

Based on the
a priori
assumption
r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{ttr},\frac{u_{tt}}{r},\phi_{ttr},\psi_{tt})\in L^{2}([\tau,T];L^{2}(I))
for
\tau\in(0,T)
,
and the calculations of
\mathcal{G}_{21}
–
\mathcal{G}_{23}
in Step 2, we obtain

\frac{1}{\omega_{n}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\cdot\boldsymbol{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}u_{tt}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}r\leq F(t)\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\varphi,\varphi_{r},\frac{\varphi}{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}

(8.38)

for some positive function
F(t)\in L^{2}(\tau,T)
, where
\omega_{n}
denotes the surface area of the
n
-sphere. Thus, it follows from Lemma
B.1
and Lemma 1.1 on
[
77
, page 250]
that
\boldsymbol{u}_{ttt}\in L^{2}([\tau,T];H^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
. Consequently, the energy equality follows easily from the spherical coordinates transformation and the following identity, due to Lemma
A.10
:

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}|u_{tt}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r=\frac{1}{\omega_{n}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\frac{2}{\omega_{n}}\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{ttt},\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\rangle_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

The proof of Lemma
8.4
is completed.
∎

Lemma 8.5
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{trr},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r},u_{rrrr},\big(\frac{u_{rr}}{r}\big)_{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rrr},\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)_{r}\Big)(t)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}s\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{trrr},\frac{u_{trr}}{r},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u_{tr}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1.
It follows from (
7.65
) and Lemma
8.4
that

\sqrt{t}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{trr},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)(t)\Big|_{2}\leq C(T)\big(\sqrt{t}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}(t)|_{2}+1\big)\leq C(T).

(8.39)

Then, according to (
8.20
)–(
8.15
), (
8.22
)–(
8.23
), (
8.39
),
and Lemma
7.1
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{rrrr},\big(\frac{u_{rr}}{r}\big)_{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rrr},\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)_{r}\Big)(t)\Big|_{2}

(8.40)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sqrt{t}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{trr},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)(t)\Big|_{2}+C(T)\leq C(T).

2.
Apply
r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}\partial_{t}
and
r^{\frac{m}{2}}\partial_{t}\partial_{r}
to
\eqref{e2.2}_{2}
, respectively. Then, taking the
L^{2}(I)
-norm of these two resulting equality,
we obtain from (
8.27
), and Lemmas
7.5
–
7.6
and
8.1
–
8.3
that

\displaystyle\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(u_{tr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{t}\big)_{r},\big(u_{tr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{t}\big)_{rr}\Big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{u_{tt}}{r},u_{ttr}\big)\Big|_{2}+\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{r}(uu_{r})_{t},(uu_{r})_{tr}\Big)\Big|_{2}+\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{\phi_{tr}}{r},\phi_{trr}\big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle+2\alpha\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{r}(\psi u_{r})_{t},(\psi u_{r})_{tr}\Big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{u_{tt}}{r},u_{ttr}\big)\Big|_{2}+C_{0}|(u,\psi)|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{trr}|_{2}

\displaystyle+C_{0}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\frac{u_{t}}{r},u_{tr},\frac{\psi_{t}}{r},\psi_{tr}\Big)\Big|_{2}|u_{r}|_{\infty}+C_{0}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tr}|_{2}\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tr}|_{\infty}\Big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\big(\frac{\psi}{r},\psi_{r}\big)\Big|_{2}+C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}u_{tr}|_{\infty}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{\psi}{r},\psi_{r}\big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle+C_{0}|u_{t}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}|_{2}+C_{0}|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}\psi_{t}|_{2}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}ru_{rr}|_{\infty}+C_{0}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\psi_{t}|_{2}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}u_{rr}|_{\infty}+C(T)

\displaystyle\leq\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{u_{tt}}{r},u_{ttr}\big)\Big|_{2}+C(T)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{trr}|_{2}+C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tr}|_{\infty}\Big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\big(\frac{\psi}{r},\psi_{r}\big)\Big|_{2}+C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}u_{tr}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+C(T)\big(|\chi_{1}^{\flat}ru_{rr}|_{\infty}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}u_{rr}|_{\infty}+1\big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{u_{tt}}{r},u_{ttr}\big)\Big|_{2}+C(T)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{trr}|_{2}

\displaystyle+C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{m+1}{2}}(u_{tr},u_{trr})|_{2}\Big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r\Big(\frac{\psi}{r},\big(\frac{\psi}{r}\big)_{r},\psi_{r},\psi_{rr}\Big)\Big|_{2}+C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}(u_{tr},u_{trr})|_{2}

\displaystyle+C(T)\big(|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{3}{2}}(u_{rr},u_{rrr})|_{2}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}(u_{rr},u_{rrr})|_{2}+1\big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{u_{tt}}{r},u_{ttr}\big)\Big|_{2}+C(T)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{trr}|_{2}

\displaystyle+C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{1}{2}}|_{\infty}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{2-m}{2}}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{tr},u_{trr})|_{2}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\frac{\psi}{r},\big(\frac{\psi}{r}\big)_{r},\psi_{r},\psi_{rr}\Big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle+C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{tr},u_{trr})|_{2}

\displaystyle+C(T)\big(|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{3-m}{2}}|_{\infty}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}|_{\infty}\big)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{rr},u_{rrr})|_{2}+C(T)

\displaystyle\leq\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\frac{u_{tt}}{r},u_{ttr})\big|_{2}+C(T)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{trr}|_{2}+C(T).

It follows from the above estimate, (
8.39
), and Lemmas
7.1
and
8.4
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}s\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{trrr},\frac{u_{trr}}{r},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u_{tr}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{t}s\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(u_{tr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{t}\big)_{r},\big(u_{tr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{t}\big)_{rr}\Big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{t}s\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{u_{tt}}{r},u_{ttr}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+C(T)t\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{trr}|_{2}^{2}+C(T)t\leq C(T).

This completes the proof.
∎

9.
Global Well-Posedness of Regular Solutions with Far-Field Vacuum

This section is devoted to proving Theorems
2.1
–
2.2
for the flow with far-field vacuum.

9.1.
Proof of Theorem
2.1

Based on the local well-posedness and the corresponding global uniform estimates obtained above, now we can give the proof of Theorem
2.1
. We divide the proof into four steps.

1. Global well-posedness of
2
-order regular solutions.
First, according to Theorem
3.3
, there exists a
2
-order
regular solution
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
of the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
for some
T_{*}>0
, which takes form (
2.8
).

Second, let
\overline{T}_{*}>0
be the life span of
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
,
and let
T
be any fixed time satisfying
T\in(0,\overline{T}_{*})
.
Collecting the uniform
a priori
bounds obtained in Lemmas
4.2
,
4.7
,
and
7.2
–
7.9
, and then using (
7.1
) and Lemma
B.1
yield that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\|\rho(t)\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}}+\|(\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1},(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{t})(t)\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T),

(9.1)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\log\rho(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\big\|\big(\nabla^{2}\log\rho,(\nabla\log\rho)_{t}\big)(t)\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T),

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{H^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(T),

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t})(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}s\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\boldsymbol{u}_{tt})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(T).

Clearly,
\overline{T}_{*}\geq T_{*}
. Next, we show that
\overline{T}_{*}=\infty
. Otherwise, if
\overline{T}_{*}<\infty
,
according to the uniform
a priori
estimates (
9.1
) and the standard weak convergence arguments, for any sequence
\{t_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
satisfying
0<t_{k}<\overline{T}_{*}
and
t_{k}\to\overline{T}_{*}
as
k\to\infty
, there exist a subsequence (still denoted by)
\{t_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
and limits
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u},\bar{\boldsymbol{f}},\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}},\bar{\mathcal{K}})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
satisfying

\displaystyle\rho(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\ \ \text{for any $p\in(1,\infty]$},\quad\,\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{f}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\quad\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\quad\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

and, as
k\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\rho(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\rho(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle\text{weakly\ \, in }L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

(9.2)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle\text{weakly\ \, in }H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

\displaystyle(\rho,\nabla\log\rho)(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to(\rho,\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle\text{weakly* in }L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

\displaystyle\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\bar{\boldsymbol{f}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle\text{weakly \, in }H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\log\rho(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\bar{\mathcal{K}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle\text{weakly \, in }L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}).

Then we claim that

(\bar{\boldsymbol{f}},\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}},\bar{\mathcal{K}})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=(\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1},\nabla\log\rho,\nabla^{2}\log\rho)(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\qquad\mbox{for {\it a.e.} $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$}.

(9.3)

For simplicity, we prove that
\bar{\boldsymbol{f}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
for
a.e.

\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, since the rest of (
9.3
) can be derived analogously.

First, due to
\eqref{global-unifrom}_{1}
, there exists a subsequence such that

\rho^{\gamma-1}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\bar{\phi}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\qquad\text{weakly* in $L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})$}

(9.4)

for some limit
\phi(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
On the other hand, it follows from
\eqref{global-unifrom}_{1}
and Lemma
6.2
that,
for any fixed
R>0
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\rho^{\gamma-1}(t)\|_{H^{2}(B_{R})}\leq C(R,T),\quad\quad\inf_{(t,\boldsymbol{x})\in[0,T]\times B_{R}}\rho(t,\boldsymbol{x})\geq C^{-1}(R,T)

(9.5)

for some constant
C(R,T)>0
depending only on
(C_{0},R,T)
,
where
B_{R}=\{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\,|\boldsymbol{x}|<R\}
.

Then, since
H^{2}(B_{R})
is compactly embedded in
C(\overline{B_{R}})
, by extracting a subsequence,
there exists a limit
0<\bar{\bar{\phi}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in C(\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that,
for each
R>0
,

\rho^{\gamma-1}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\bar{\bar{\phi}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\qquad\text{uniformly on $B_{R}$}\,\,\,\text{as $k\to\infty$}.

(9.6)

Clearly,
0<\bar{\phi}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\bar{\bar{\phi}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{n})
due to the uniqueness of limits in (
9.4
) and (
9.6
), which, together with
\eqref{global-unifrom}_{1}
and (
9.5
)–(
9.6
), yields that

\rho(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\bar{\phi}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\qquad\text{for any $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$}\,\,\,\text{as $k\to\infty$}.

(9.7)

Then it follows from the uniqueness of the limits in
\eqref{f2}_{1}
and (
9.7
) that
\bar{\phi}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\rho(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
,
i.e.
,
\bar{\phi}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\rho^{\gamma-1}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
.

Next, it follows from
\eqref{f2}_{4}
, (
9.4
),
\bar{\phi}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\rho^{\gamma-1}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
, and the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem that, for any
\zeta(\boldsymbol{x})\in C^{\infty}_{\rm c}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
i=1,\cdots\!,n
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\gamma-1}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\zeta_{x_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\gamma-1}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\zeta_{x_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=-\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{x_{i}}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\zeta(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bar{f}_{i}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\zeta(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}.

This implies that
\rho^{\gamma-1}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
admits the weak derivatives
(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{x_{i}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\bar{f}_{i}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for
i=1,\cdots\!,n
, so that
\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\bar{\boldsymbol{f}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
for
a.e.

\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

We now continue to prove
\overline{T}_{*}=\infty
. We aim to show that functions

(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
satisfy all the initial assumptions
given in Theorem
3.3
, which consist of showing that

(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
are spherically symmetric
and satisfy (
2.5
)–(
2.6
).

1.1.
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
are spherically symmetric.

It suffices to show that
\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
is spherically symmetric,
since the proof for
\rho(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
can be derived analogously.
To achieve this, it suffices to show that

\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})
for any
\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
.
Indeed, since
\boldsymbol{u}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})
is spherically symmetric for each
t_{k}
and, by (
9.2
),
\boldsymbol{u}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})
converges to
\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
weakly in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
as
k\to\infty
, it follows from the coordinate transformation that,
for any vector function
\boldsymbol{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\cdot\boldsymbol{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\cdot\boldsymbol{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u})(t_{k},\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})\cdot\boldsymbol{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\cdot(\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{\zeta})(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\cdot(\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{\zeta})(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})\cdot\boldsymbol{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x},

which implies that

\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})
for any
\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
.

1.2.
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
satisfies (
2.5
)–(
2.6
).
Clearly, by (
9.2
), it remains to show that
\rho(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Since
\bar{\phi}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\rho(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
in (
9.7
) and
\rho(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\geq 0
, it follows from (
9.1
), (
9.7
), and Lemma
A.5
that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\liminf_{k\to\infty}\rho(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq\liminf_{k\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C(T),

which implies that
\rho(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

1.3.
\overline{T}_{*}=\infty
.

To sum up, we have shown that
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
satisfies all the initial assumptions on the initial data of Theorem
3.3
.
As a consequence, according to Theorem
3.3
, there exists a constant
T_{**}>0
such that the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) admits a unique
2
-order regular solution

(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
in

[\overline{T}_{*},\overline{T}_{*}+T_{**}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Thus, by setting

(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})=(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
in
[\overline{T}_{*},\overline{T}_{*}+T_{**}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
, it can be shown that
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
is actually the
2
-order regular solution in
[0,\overline{T}_{*}+T_{**}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
due to their time-continuities, which contradicts to the maximality of
\overline{T}_{*}<\infty
. Therefore
\overline{T}_{*}=\infty
.

2. Proof of Theorem
2.1
(i).

2.1. Time-spatial continuity of
(\rho,\nabla\rho)
.

First, it follows from (
9.1
) and the relation:

\nabla\rho=\rho\nabla\log\rho
that, for any finite
T>0
,

\displaystyle\|\rho\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\rho\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(T),

(9.8)

\displaystyle\|\rho\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\rho\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\nabla\log\rho\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(T),

\displaystyle\|\rho\|_{D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\rho\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\nabla\log\rho\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\nabla\log\rho\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(T),

which implies that

\rho\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
.
Since
\rho
is the
2
-order regular solution, this implies that
\rho\in C([0,T];L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
and
\nabla\log\rho\in C([0,T];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
. Then, for any
t,t_{0}\in[0,T]
,
repeating the calculations in (
9.8
) with
\rho(t)
replaced by
(\rho(t)-\rho(t_{0}))
,
along with (
9.1
), gives

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to t_{0}}\|\rho(t)-\rho(t_{0})\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

(9.9)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\lim_{t\to t_{0}}\|\rho(t)-\rho(t_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}+C(T)\lim_{t\to t_{0}}\big\|\nabla\log\rho(t)-\nabla\log\rho(t_{0})\big\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=0,\qquad

so that
\rho\in C([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
. Then it follows from Lemma
C.6
in Appendix
C
that
\rho\in C([0,T];C^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{2}}))
.

2.2. Time-spatial continuity of
(\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{u},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{u}_{t})
.

First, since
\boldsymbol{u}
is the
2
-order regular solution,
\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
. Hence, Lemma
A.1
yields
\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{2}}))
.

Next, due to (
9.1
), then
t\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
and
(t\boldsymbol{u}_{t})_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
.
It follows from Lemmas
A.10
and
C.4
that

t\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))\implies\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\in C((0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{2}})).

(9.10)

Finally, to obtain
\boldsymbol{u}\in C((0,T];C^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{2}}))
,
we show that
t\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
. Rewrite (
2.3
) as the following elliptic system:

L\boldsymbol{u}=\underline{-\boldsymbol{u}_{t}-\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}-\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1}}_{:=\boldsymbol{F}_{1}}+\underline{\nabla\log\rho\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u})}_{:=\boldsymbol{F}_{2}}.

(9.11)

Then, by the classical regularity theory for elliptic equations in Lemma
A.13
, we have

\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C_{0}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|t\boldsymbol{F}_{i}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{F}_{i}(t_{0})\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

(9.12)

For
\boldsymbol{F}_{1}
, by (
9.10
) and
\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1}\in C([0,T];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
, then
(t\boldsymbol{u}_{t},t\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1})\in C([0,T];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
.
Moreover, it follows from (
9.1
) and Lemma
A.1
that

t\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})),\qquad(t\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u})_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})).

This, along with Lemma
A.10
, implies that
t\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
, so that

t\boldsymbol{F}_{1}\in C([0,T];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})).

(9.13)

For
\boldsymbol{F}_{2}
, it follows from (
9.1
), Lemmas
A.1
–
A.2

and
C.4
, and the Young inequality that, for all
0\leq t,t_{0}\leq T
and
\omega\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\|t\boldsymbol{F}_{2}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{F}_{2}(t_{0})\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

(9.14)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big\|\nabla\log\rho(t)-\nabla\log\rho(t_{0})\big\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{D^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle+C_{0}\big\|\nabla\log\rho(t_{0})\big\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{0}\big\|\nabla\log\rho(t)-\nabla\log\rho(t_{0})\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle+C_{0}\big\|\nabla\log\rho(t_{0})\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big\|\nabla\log\rho(t)-\nabla\log\rho(t_{0})\big\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+C(\omega,T)\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)-\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle+C(\omega,T)|t-t_{0}|+\omega\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Consequently, collecting (
9.12
)–(
9.14
) and setting
\omega
small enough, we see
from the time-continuity of
(\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\log\rho)
that, as
t\to t_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

(9.15)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|t\boldsymbol{F}_{1}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{F}_{1}(t_{0})\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+C(T)\big\|\nabla\log\rho(t)-\nabla\log\rho(t_{0})\big\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle+C(T)\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)-\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+C(T)|t-t_{0}|\to 0.

This, together with
t\boldsymbol{u}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
and
\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
, implies that
t\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
. Therefore, it follows from Lemma
C.5
that
\boldsymbol{u}\in C((0,T];C^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{2}}))
.

2.3. Time-spatial continuity of
\rho_{t}
.
It follows from the relation:

\rho_{t}=-\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\rho-\rho\diver\boldsymbol{u},

(9.16)

and the conclusions obtained in Steps 2.1–2.2 that
\rho_{t}\in C((0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{2}}))
, which implies Theorem
2.1
(i).

3. Proof of Theorem
2.1
(ii).
Let
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{*}
be defined in Theorem
2.1
(ii). First, since
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})
is the
2
-order regular solution, then
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
, which, along with Lemma
A.1
, implies that
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{3}}))
.

Next, we show that
\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\in C((0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{3}}))
.
To obtain this, we claim that

t\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})),\qquad t\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})).

(9.17)

\eqref{707}_{1}
follows from the same argument as in Step 2.2 above, with
\mathbb{R}^{2}
replaced by
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
For
\eqref{707}_{2}
, based on the discussions in (
9.11
)–(
9.15
) and
\eqref{707}_{1}
,
we see that

t\boldsymbol{F}_{1}\in C([0,T];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})),

(9.18)

so it suffices to derive the estimates on
\boldsymbol{F}_{2}
.
To this end, it follows from (
9.1
), Lemmas
A.1
–
A.2
,
and
C.2
that, for all
0\leq t,t_{0}\leq T
and
\omega\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\|t\boldsymbol{F}_{2}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{F}_{2}(t_{0})\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(9.19)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big\|\nabla\log\rho(t)-\nabla\log\rho(t_{0})\big\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{D^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+C_{0}\big\|\nabla\log\rho(t_{0})\big\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\frac{2}{3}}\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{3}}

\displaystyle+C_{0}\big\|\nabla\log\rho(t)-\nabla\log\rho(t_{0})\big\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{D^{2,3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+C_{0}\big\|\nabla\log\rho(t_{0})\big\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\frac{2}{3}}\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{3}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big\|\nabla\log\rho(t)-\nabla\log\rho(t_{0})\big\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C(\omega,T)\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)-\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+C(\omega,T)|t-t_{0}|+\omega\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Consequently, collecting (
9.12
) (with
\mathbb{R}^{2}
replaced by
\mathbb{R}^{3}
) and (
9.18
)–(
9.19
) and setting
\omega
small enough, it follows from the time-continuity of
(\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\log\rho)
that, as
t\to t_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|t\boldsymbol{u}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\|t\boldsymbol{F}_{2}(t)-t_{0}\boldsymbol{F}_{2}(t_{0})\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\quad+C(T)\big\|\nabla\log\rho(t)-\nabla\log\rho(t_{0})\big\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\quad+C(T)\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)-\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0})\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C(T)|t-t_{0}|\to 0.

This, together with
t\boldsymbol{u}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
and
\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
, implies that
t\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
. Therefore, it follows from Lemma
A.1
that
\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\in C((0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{3}}))
.

Now, it remains to show that

(\nabla\rho,\rho_{t},\nabla\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{*}),\qquad(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{u}_{t})\in C((0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{*}).

(9.20)

To this end, it follows from (
9.17
),
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
,
and Lemma
B.1
that

\displaystyle r\Big(\rho_{r},\rho_{rr},\frac{\rho_{r}}{r},u_{r},\frac{u}{r},u_{rr},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\in C([0,T];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle r\Big(u_{rrr},\frac{u_{rr}}{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r},u_{t},u_{tr},\frac{u_{t}}{r}\Big)\in C((0,T];L^{2}(I)),

which, along with Lemma
A.1
, leads to

\displaystyle\Big(\rho_{r},u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\Big)\in C([0,T]\times[\sigma,\infty)),\qquad\Big(u_{rr},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r},u_{t}\Big)\in C((0,T]\times[\sigma,\infty)),

for any
\sigma>0
. Hence,
(\rho_{r},u_{r},\frac{u}{r})\in C([0,T]\times(0,\infty))
and
(u_{rr},(\frac{u}{r})_{r},u_{t})\in C((0,T]\times(0,\infty))
, that is,
(\nabla\rho,\nabla\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{*})
and
(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{u}_{t})\in C((0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{*})
due to Lemma
B.1
. Finally,
\rho_{t}\in C([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{*})
can be derived directly via (
9.16
) and the fact that
(\rho,\nabla\rho,\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{*})
. The proof of Theorem
2.1
(ii) is completed.

4. Proof of Theorem
2.1
(iii)–(iv).
We first show that
\mathcal{P}(t)\equiv\boldsymbol{0}
for
t\in[0,T]
. For simplicity, we only give the proof for the 3-D case, since the 2-D case follows analogously. Since
(\sqrt{\rho},\sqrt{\rho}\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
, then

\rho\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
, which implies that
\mathcal{P}(t)
is bounded
and continuous on
[0,T]
and
r^{2}\rho u\in C([0,T];L^{1}(I))
.
Next, according to the spherical coordinate transformations:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{x}=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})^{\top}=(r\cos\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2},r\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2},r\cos\theta_{2})^{\top},

\displaystyle r\in I,\qquad\theta_{1}\in[0,2\pi],\qquad\theta_{2}\in[0,\pi],

we obtain from the spherical symmetry of
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\rho\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})^{\top}}{r}(\rho u)(t,r)r^{2}\sin\theta_{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\theta_{1}\mathrm{d}\theta_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{p}(t)\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(\cos\theta_{1}\sin^{2}\theta_{2},\sin\theta_{1}\sin^{2}\theta_{2},\cos\theta_{2}\sin\theta_{2})^{\top}\,\mathrm{d}\theta_{1}\mathrm{d}\theta_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{p}(t)\int_{0}^{\pi}(0,0,\cos\theta_{2}\sin\theta_{2})^{\top}\,\mathrm{d}\theta_{2}=\boldsymbol{0},\qquad\text{with }\mathfrak{p}(t):=\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2}(\rho u)(t,r)\,\mathrm{d}r,

which yields that
\mathcal{P}(t)\equiv\boldsymbol{0}
for
t\in[0,T]
.

The conservation of total mass can be simply derived by integrating the mass equation
\eqref{eq:1.1benwen}_{1}
over
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
and using the fact that
\rho\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];W^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
. Finally, Theorem
2.1
(iv) is a directly consequence of Lemmas
4.2
,
4.7
, and
6.2
.

The proof of Theorem
2.1
is completed.

9.2.
Proof of Theorem
2.2

Now we are ready to provide the proof of Theorem
2.2
. We divide the proof into three steps.

1. Global well-posedness of
3
-order regular solutions.

First, according to Theorem
3.4
, there exists a
3
-order
regular solution
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
of the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
for some
T_{*}>0
, which takes form (
2.8
).

Second, let
\overline{T}_{*}>0
be the life span of
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
,
and let
T
be any fixed time satisfying
0<T<\overline{T}_{*}
.
Then collecting the uniform
a priori
bounds obtained in Lemmas
4.2
,
4.7
,
7.2
–
7.9
, and
8.1
–
8.5
, together with (
7.1
) and Lemma
B.1
, yields that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\|\rho(t)\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}}+\big\|(\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1},(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{t})(t)\big\|_{H^{2}}\leq C(T),

(9.21)

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla\log\rho(t)\big\|_{L^{\infty}}+\big\|\big(\nabla^{2}\log\rho,(\nabla\log\rho)_{t}\big)(t)\big\|_{H^{1}}\leq C(T),

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{H^{3}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{H^{1}}+\int_{0}^{t}\big\|(\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\boldsymbol{u}_{tt})\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T),

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\big\|(\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\boldsymbol{u}_{tt})(t)\big\|_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}s\big\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{tt})\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

The remaining proof here is basically the same as that in Step 1 of §
9.1
. We omit it here.

2. Proof of Theorem
2.2
(i).

According to Step 2 in the proof for Theorem
2.1
(see §
9.1
), it suffices to
show that

(\rho_{t},\nabla\boldsymbol{u},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{u}_{t})\in C([0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{2}})).

(9.22)

Since
\boldsymbol{u}
is the
3
-order regular solution, we have

\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})),\qquad\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})),

which, along with Lemma
C.4
and (
9.16
), yields that

(\nabla\boldsymbol{u},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\rho_{t})\in C([0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{2}}))
.

3. Proof of Theorem
2.2
(ii).
According to Step 3 in the proof for Theorem
2.1
(see §
9.1
), it only remains to show that

(\nabla\rho,\rho_{t},\nabla\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{3}})),\qquad(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{u}_{t})\in C((0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{3}})).

3.1. Time-spatial continuity of
(\nabla\rho,\rho_{t},\nabla\boldsymbol{u})
.

First, it follows from the fact that
\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
and Lemma
A.1
that
\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];C^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{3}}))
. Next, following a calculation similar to (
9.8
), with
\mathbb{R}^{2}
replaced by
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we obtain
\rho\in C([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
and

\displaystyle\|\rho(t)\|_{D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\rho(t)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\rho(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\log\rho(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{3}

\displaystyle+\|\rho(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\log\rho(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\log\rho(t)\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\|\rho(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\log\rho(t)\|_{D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C(T),

which implies that
\rho\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
.
Since
\rho
is the
3
-order regular solution, then
\rho\in C([0,T];L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
and
\nabla^{2}\log\rho\in C([0,T];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
.
For any
t_{0}\in[0,T]
, repeating the above calculation with
\rho(t)
replaced by
\rho(t)-\rho(t_{0})
,
together with (
9.21
), gives

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to t_{0}}\|\rho(t)-\rho(t_{0})\|_{D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\lim_{t\to t_{0}}\|\rho(t)-\rho(t_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C(T)\lim_{t\to t_{0}}\|\nabla^{2}\log\rho(t)-\nabla^{2}\log\rho(t_{0})\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}=0,

which implies
\rho\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
.
Hence, by Lemma
A.1
,
\rho\in C([0,T];C^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{3}}))
.
Finally,
\rho_{t}\in C([0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{3}}))
due to (
9.16
) and the facts that
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T];C^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{3}}))
.

3.2. Time-spatial continuity of
(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{u}_{t})
.

Thanks to (
9.21
), we have

\displaystyle(t\boldsymbol{u}_{t},t\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})),\qquad((t\boldsymbol{u}_{t})_{t},(t\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})_{t})\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})),

which, along with Lemma
A.10
, yields

(t\boldsymbol{u}_{t},t\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T];W^{1,q}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))\qquad\text{for all $q\in[2,6)$}.

(9.23)

Therefore, it follows from (
9.23
) and Lemma
A.1
that
(\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})\in C((0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

The proof of Theorem
2.2
is completed.

9.3.
Proof of Corollary
2.1

Corollary
2.1
is a direct consequence of Lemma
A.11
in Appendix
A
by taking
(f,\nu)=(\rho_{0},\gamma-1)
.

10.
Global Well-Posedness of Regular Solutions with Strictly Positive Initial Density

This section is devoted to establishing the global well-posedness of spherically symmetric classical solutions of the Cauchy problem of system (
1.1
) with general smooth initial data and
strictly positive initial density,
i.e.
,
\inf_{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})>0
. Certainly, in this case, under the spherical coordinates, the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
for some
T>0
can be written as the following initial-boundary value problem in
[0,T]\times I
:

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\rho_{t}+(\rho u)_{r}+\frac{m\rho u}{r}=0,\\[3.0pt]
\displaystyle\rho u_{t}+\rho uu_{r}+A(\rho^{\gamma})_{r}=2\alpha\big(\rho u_{r}+\frac{m\rho u}{r}\big)_{r}-\frac{2\alpha m\rho_{r}u}{r},\\[5.0pt]
\displaystyle(\rho,u)|_{t=0}=(\rho_{0},u_{0})\quad\,\,\,\text{for $r\in I$},\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle u|_{r=0}=0\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T]$},\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle(\rho,u)\to\left(\bar{\rho}>0,0\right)\qquad\text{as $r\to\infty\,$ \, for $t\in[0,T]$}.\end{cases}

(10.1)

10.1.
Some notations and conventions

Denote by
\chi_{E}
the characteristic function with respect of a set
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}

(
i.e.
,
\chi_{E}=1
on
E
and
\chi_{E}=0
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash E
).

For
\sigma\geq 0
and
t\geq 0
, define

\displaystyle E_{\sigma}:=E_{\sigma}(t)=\{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\,|\rho(t,\boldsymbol{x})-\bar{\rho}|\leq\sigma\},

\displaystyle E^{\sigma}:=E^{\sigma}(t)=\{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\,|\rho(t,\boldsymbol{x})-\bar{\rho}|>\sigma\}.

Clearly,
\mathbb{R}^{n}=E_{\sigma}\cup E^{\sigma}
for each
\sigma\geq 0
and
t\geq 0
. Moreover, since the solution
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})
we considered is spherically symmetric, we will still denote by
(E_{\sigma},E^{\sigma})
the subsets of
I
under the spherical coordinates when no confusion arises, namely,

\displaystyle E_{\sigma}=E_{\sigma}(t)=\{r\in I:\,|\rho(t,r)-\bar{\rho}|\leq\sigma\},

\displaystyle E^{\sigma}=E^{\sigma}(t)=\{r\in I:\,|\rho(t,r)-\bar{\rho}|>\sigma\}.

In this case,
I=E_{\sigma}\cup E^{\sigma}
for each
\sigma\geq 0
and
t\geq 0
.

Besides, we adapt the notations
(\chi_{\sigma}^{\flat},\chi_{\sigma}^{\sharp})
defined in (
4.7
) and the notation
j_{\gamma}(z)
in
[
58
, Chapter 5]
as

j_{\gamma}(z)=(z^{\gamma}-\bar{\rho}^{\gamma})-\gamma\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}(z-\bar{\rho})\qquad\mbox{for $z\geq 0$}.

In the rest of this section,
C_{0}\in[1,\infty)
denotes a generic constant depending only on
(\rho_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})
and fixed constants
(n,\alpha,\gamma,A,\bar{\rho})
;
C(\nu_{1},\cdots\!,\nu_{k})\in[1,\infty)
denotes a generic constant depending only on
C_{0}
and parameters
(\nu_{1},\cdots\!,\nu_{k})
,
which may be different at each occurrence.

10.2.
Some equivalent norms on the initial data

In order to obtain the desired global uniform estimates on the regular solutions
by employing analogous arguments
shown in §
4
–§
8
,
we first give some equivalent norms on the initial density
\rho_{0}
,
which will be frequently used in the subsequent analysis.

Lemma 10.1
.

Let the initial condition (
2.20
) or (
2.23
) hold. Then

\mathrm{(i)}

0\leq j_{\gamma}(\rho_{0})\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
;

\mathrm{(ii)}

\nabla\sqrt{\rho_{0}}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
;

\mathrm{(iii)}

if (
2.20
) holds, then
\nabla\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\nabla\log\rho_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
;

\mathrm{(iv)}

if (
2.23
) holds, then
\nabla\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\nabla\log\rho_{0}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1. Proof of (i).

A direct calculation gives

\displaystyle j_{\gamma}^{\prime}(z)=\gamma(z^{\gamma-1}-\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}),\quad j_{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}(z)=\gamma(\gamma-1)z^{\gamma-2}>0\qquad\,\,\text{for $z>0$}.

with

j_{\gamma}(\bar{\rho})=j_{\gamma}^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})=0
.
Thus,
j_{\gamma}(z)
is a convex function and
j_{\gamma}(z)\geq 0
for
z\geq 0
.
Moreover, by the Taylor expansion, we have

j_{\gamma}(z)=\frac{1}{2!}j_{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}(z_{\vartheta})(z-\bar{\rho})^{2}=\frac{\gamma(\gamma-1)}{2}z_{\vartheta}^{\gamma-2}(z-\bar{\rho})^{2},

(10.2)

where
z_{\vartheta}=\vartheta z+(1-\vartheta)\bar{\rho}
for some
\vartheta\in[0,1]
. Setting
\rho_{0*}=\inf_{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})
and taking
z=\rho_{0}
imply that

\displaystyle 0\leq j_{\gamma}(\rho_{0})\leq C_{0}\big(\min\{\rho_{0*},\bar{\rho}\}\big)^{\gamma-2}(\rho_{0}-\bar{\rho})^{2}\qquad\quad\,\text{if $\gamma\in(1,2)$},

\displaystyle 0\leq j_{\gamma}(\rho_{0})\leq C_{0}\big(\max\{|\rho_{0}|_{\infty},\bar{\rho}\}\big)^{\gamma-2}(\rho_{0}-\bar{\rho})^{2}\qquad\text{if $\gamma\in[2,\infty)$},

which, along with
\rho_{0}-\bar{\rho}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, yields (i).

2. Proof of (ii)–(iv).

(ii)–(iv) follows easily from the facts that
\rho_{0}\geq\rho_{0*}>0
,

\rho_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
and
\nabla\rho_{0}\in H^{l-1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
(
l=2
or
3
). For example, if (
2.23
) holds, it follows from Lemma
A.1
and

\nabla\log\rho=\frac{\nabla\rho}{\rho}
that

\displaystyle\|\nabla\log\rho\|_{D^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0*}}+\frac{1}{\rho_{0*}^{2}}\|\nabla\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\frac{1}{\rho_{0*}^{3}}\|\nabla\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\Big)\|\nabla\rho_{0}\|_{H^{2}}\leq C_{0}.

The rest of (ii)–(iv) can be proved in the same manner, we omit the details here.
∎

10.3.
Local-in-time well-posedness with strictly positive initial density

10.3.1.
Local-in-time well-posedness in M-D coordinates

When
\inf_{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})>0
,
we consider the following reformulated system in the M-D cases:

\begin{cases}\rho_{t}+\diver(\rho\boldsymbol{u})=0,\\
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}+\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1}+L\boldsymbol{u}=\nabla\log\rho\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u}),\end{cases}

(10.3)

where the operators
(L,Q)
are defined in (
2.4
).
Since the positive lower bound of
\rho
can be obtained via its transport
equation
\eqref{re}_{1}
in short time for strong solutions,

\eqref{re}_{2}
becomes a standard parabolic system
for
\boldsymbol{u}
without degeneracy or singularity.
Then, starting from
(
10.3
)
,
by adaptation of the classical methods for the well-posedness
of the transport equation and the parabolic equations,
such as the ones used in Matsumura-Nishida
[
60
]

and Sundbye
[
75
]
,
one can establish the following local well-posedness results of
the
s
-order (
s=2,3
) regular solutions (as defined in Definition
2.2
)
when
\inf_{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})>0
.
For brevity, we omit the proof here.

Theorem 10.1
.

Let
n=2
or
3
,
\bar{\rho}>0
in (
1.3
), and (
3.7
) hold.
If the initial data
(\rho_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})(\boldsymbol{x})

are spherically symmetric and satisfy (
2.20
)–(
2.21
),
then there exists
T_{*}>0
such that the Cauchy
problem (
1.1
)
–
(
1.3
) admits a unique
2
-order regular solution
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})

in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfying (
2.22
) with
T

replaced by
T_{*}
.
Moreover,
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})
is spherically symmetric with form (
2.8
), and (
2.9
)
–
(
2.10
)
hold with
T
replaced by
T_{*}
.

Theorem 10.2
.

Let
n=2
or
3
,
\bar{\rho}>0
in (
1.3
), and (
3.7
) hold. If the initial data
(\rho_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})(\boldsymbol{x})
are spherically symmetric and satisfy (
2.23
),
then there exists
T_{*}>0
such that the Cauchy
problem (
1.1
)
–
(
1.3
) admits a unique
3
-order regular solution
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfying (
2.22
) with
T
replaced by
T_{*}
and satisfying

\rho_{tt}\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T_{*}];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})).

(10.4)

Moreover,
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})
is spherically symmetric with form (
2.8
),
and (
2.15
)
–
(
2.16
) hold with
T
replaced by
T_{*}
.

10.3.2.
Local-in-time well-posedness in spherical coordinates

Based on relation (
2.8
),
we say that
(\rho,u)(t,r)
is the
s
-order
(s=2,3)
regular solution of problem (
10.1
) in
[0,T_{*}]\times I
if the vector function
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
is the
s
-order
(s=2,3)
regular solution (as defined in Definition
2.2
)
of the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
(
n=2,3
)
with
\inf_{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})>0
.

Now, based on Lemma
B.1
in Appendix
B
,
Theorems
10.1
–
10.2
in spherical coordinates
can be stated as follows:

Theorem 10.3
.

Let (
3.7
) hold. Assume the initial data
(\rho_{0},u_{0})(r)
satisfy

\inf_{r\in I}\rho_{0}(r)>0,\quad\ r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\rho_{0}-\bar{\rho},(\rho_{0})_{r},\frac{(\rho_{0})_{r}}{r},(\rho_{0})_{rr},u_{0},\frac{u_{0}}{r},(u_{0})_{r},\big(\frac{u_{0}}{r}\big)_{r},(u_{0})_{rr}\Big)\in L^{2}(I),

and, in addition,

(\rho_{0})_{r}\in L^{\infty}(I)\qquad\text{when $n=3$}.

Then there exists
T_{*}>0
such that problem (
10.1
)
admits a unique
2
-order regular solution
(\rho,u)(t,r)
in
[0,T_{*}]\times I
satisfying

\displaystyle\inf_{(t,r)\in[0,T_{*}]\times I}\rho(t,r)>0,\qquad(\rho,\rho_{r})\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}]\times I),

(10.5)

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\rho-\bar{\rho},\rho_{r},\frac{\rho_{r}}{r},\rho_{rr},\rho_{t},\rho_{tr}\Big)\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r},u_{rr},u_{t}\Big)\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{u_{rr}}{r},u_{rrr},\frac{u_{t}}{r},u_{tr}\Big)\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{u_{rr}}{r},u_{rrr},\frac{u_{t}}{r},u_{tr}\Big)\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{tt},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r},u_{trr}\Big)\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

and

\displaystyle\Big(\rho,\rho_{r},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r}\Big)\in C((0,T_{*}];C(\bar{I})).

(10.6)

Theorem 10.4
.

Let (
3.7
) hold. Assume the initial data
(\rho_{0},u_{0})(r)
satisfy

\displaystyle\inf_{r\in I}\rho_{0}(r)>0,\quad r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\rho_{0}-\bar{\rho},(\rho_{0})_{r},\frac{(\rho_{0})_{r}}{r},(\rho_{0})_{rr},\big(\frac{(\rho_{0})_{r}}{r}\big)_{r},(\rho_{0})_{rrr}\Big)\in L^{2}(I),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{0},\frac{u_{0}}{r},(u_{0})_{r},\big(\frac{u_{0}}{r}\big)_{r},(u_{0})_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u_{0}}{r}\big)_{r},\big(\frac{u_{0}}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{(u_{0})_{rr}}{r},(u_{0})_{rrr}\Big)\in L^{2}(I).

Then there exists
T_{*}>0
such that problem (
10.1
)
admits a unique
3
-order regular solution
(\rho,u)(t,r)
in
[0,T_{*}]\times I
satisfying

\displaystyle\inf_{(t,r)\in[0,T_{*}]\times I}\rho(t,r)>0,\qquad(\rho,\rho_{r})\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}]\times I),

(10.7)

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\rho-\bar{\rho},\rho_{r},\frac{\rho_{r}}{r},\rho_{rr},\big(\frac{\rho_{r}}{r}\big)_{r},\rho_{rrr},\rho_{t},\rho_{tr},\frac{\rho_{tr}}{r},\rho_{trr}\Big)\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{tt}\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),\quad r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{ttr}\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r},u_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{u_{rr}}{r},u_{rrr},u_{t},\frac{u_{t}}{r},u_{tr}\Big)\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\big(\frac{1}{r}(\frac{u}{r})_{r}\big)_{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rrr},\big(\frac{u_{rr}}{r}\big)_{r},u_{rrrr},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r},u_{trr}\Big)\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\big(\frac{1}{r}(\frac{u}{r})_{r}\big)_{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rrr},\big(\frac{u_{rr}}{r}\big)_{r},u_{rrrr},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r},u_{trr},u_{tt}\Big)\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\frac{u_{tt}}{r},u_{ttr},u_{trrr},\frac{u_{trr}}{r},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u_{tr}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(I)),

and

\Big(\rho,\rho_{r},\rho_{t},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r}\Big)\in C([0,T_{*}];C(\bar{I})),\quad\,\Big(u_{t},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r},u_{rr}\Big)\in C((0,T_{*}];C(\bar{I})).

(10.8)

10.4.
Outline of the proof

Our following proof is organized as follows:
First, in §
10.5
, we establish the global
uniform upper bound of
\rho
. Second, in §
10.6
, we establish the global uniform

L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate for the effective velocity.
Next, in §
10.7
, we establish the global uniform lower bound of
\rho
.
Finally, in §
10.8
–§
10.10
, we make the global uniform estimates
for the
2
- and
3
-order regular solutions and establish the global well-posedness of regular solutions for general smooth data with strictly positive initial density.

10.5.
Global uniform upper bound of the density

In §
10.5
–§
10.7
, let
T>0
be any fixed time, and
let
(\rho,u)(t,r)
be the
s
-order (
s=2,3
) regular solution of problem (
10.1
)
in
[0,T]\times I
obtained in Theorems
10.3
–
10.4
.
This subsection is devoted to proving the uniform upper bound of
\rho
.

First, we define the effective velocity, which is identical to
Definition
4.1
in §
4
;
we present it here for subsequent development.

Definition 10.1
.

Let
(\rho,u,\alpha)
be defined as in
§
1
.
Define the effective velocity
v
as

v:=u+2\alpha(\log\rho)_{r}.

(10.9)

Besides, define
v_{0}:=v|_{t=0}=u_{0}+2\alpha(\log\rho_{0})_{r}
.

Next, we establish the fundamental energy estimate and the BD entropy estimate.

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
j_{\gamma}(\rho)=j_{\gamma}(\rho)(t,r):=j_{\gamma}(\rho(t,r))
. There exists a constant
C_{0}>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(r^{m}\rho u^{2}+r^{m}j_{\gamma}(\rho))(t,\cdot)\,\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\Big(\rho|u_{r}|^{2}+\rho\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s\leq C_{0},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(r^{m}\rho v^{2}+r^{m}|(\sqrt{\rho})_{r}|^{2}+r^{m}j_{\gamma}(\rho))(t,\cdot)\,\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma-2}|\rho_{r}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s\leq C_{0}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1.
First, a direct calculation yields that

\frac{A}{\gamma-1}(r^{m}j_{\gamma}(\rho))_{t}+\Big(\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}r^{m}(\rho^{\gamma-1}-\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1})\rho u\Big)_{r}=A(\rho^{\gamma})_{r}r^{m}u.

(10.10)

Then multiplying
\eqref{e1.5hh}_{2}
by
r^{m}u
, together with (
10.10
), gives

\begin{split}&\Big(\frac{r^{m}}{2}\rho u^{2}+\frac{A}{\gamma-1}r^{m}j_{\gamma}(\rho)\Big)_{t}+2\alpha r^{m}\Big(\rho|u_{r}|^{2}+m\rho\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)\\
&=\Big(\underline{-\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}r^{m}(\rho^{\gamma-1}-\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1})\rho u+2\alpha r^{m}\rho uu_{r}-\frac{r^{m}}{2}\rho u^{3}}_{:=\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}\Big)_{r}.\end{split}

(10.11)

Next, we need to show that
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{1}\in W^{1,1}(I)
and
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{1}|_{r=0}=0
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, which allows us to apply Lemma
A.4
to obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{1})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{1}|_{r=0}=0.

(10.12)

On one hand, it follows from (
10.5
)–(
10.6
) (or (
10.7
)–(
10.8
)) that

\rho_{r}\in L^{\infty}(I),\qquad\Big(\rho,u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r}\Big)\in C(\bar{I}),\qquad r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\rho-\bar{\rho},\rho_{r},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r},u_{rr}\Big)\in L^{2}(I)

for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, which yields
that
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{1}|_{r=0}=0
.
On the other hand, it follows from (
10.5
)–(
10.6
) (or (
10.7
)–(
10.8
))
and the Taylor expansion that

z^{\gamma-1}-\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}=(\gamma-1)z_{\vartheta}^{\gamma-2}(z-\bar{\rho})\qquad\text{with $z_{\vartheta}=\vartheta z+(1-\vartheta)\bar{\rho}\,\,$ for some $\vartheta\in[0,1]$}.

Then this, together with the Hölder inequality, yields that
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{1}\in W^{1,1}(I)
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
:

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{1}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|r^{m}\big((\rho^{\gamma-1}-\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1})\rho u,\rho uu_{r},\rho u^{3}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\rho_{\vartheta}^{\gamma-2}\rho|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho-\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+|u|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}\big)<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{1})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|r^{m-1}\big((\rho^{\gamma-1}-\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1})\rho u,\rho uu_{r},\rho u^{3}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}\big|r^{m}\big(\rho^{\gamma-1}\rho_{r}u,\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}\rho_{r}u,(\rho^{\gamma-1}-\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1})\rho u_{r}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}\big|r^{m}\big(\rho_{r}uu_{r},\rho(u_{r})^{2},\rho uu_{rr},\rho_{r}u^{3},\rho u^{2}u_{r}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\rho_{\vartheta}^{\gamma-2}\rho|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho-\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}\big|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}u\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}\Big(|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}(|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}+1)\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}\big|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}+C_{0}|\rho_{\vartheta}^{\gamma-2}\rho|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho-\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}^{2}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}|u_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}<\infty,

where
\rho_{\vartheta}=\vartheta\rho+(1-\vartheta)\bar{\rho}
. This, along with Lemma
A.4
, yields (
10.12
).

Integrating above over
[0,t]\times I
, we obtain from (
10.12
) and Lemma
10.1
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}r^{m}\rho u^{2}+\frac{A}{\gamma-1}r^{m}j_{\gamma}(\rho)\Big)(t,r)\,\mathrm{d}r+2\alpha\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\Big(\rho|u_{r}|^{2}+m\rho\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

(10.13)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}r^{m}\rho_{0}|u_{0}|^{2}+\frac{A}{\gamma-1}r^{m}j_{\gamma}(\rho_{0})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C_{0}.

2.
By Lemmas
10.1
and
B.1
and Definition
10.1
,
(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}v_{0}\in L^{2}(I)
. Thus, the BD estimate can be obtained via an analogous argument used
in Step 2 of the proof of Lemma
4.1
.
First, we obtain the equation of effective velocity:

\rho(v_{t}+uv_{r})+P_{r}=0.

(10.14)

Next, multiplying (
10.14
) by
r^{m}v
and
integrating the resulting equality over
[0,t]\times I
, we
obtain from
\eqref{e1.5hh}_{1}
and Lemma
10.1
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}r^{m}\rho v^{2}+\frac{A}{\gamma-1}r^{m}j_{\gamma}(\rho)\Big)(t,r)\,\mathrm{d}r+2A\alpha\gamma\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma-2}|\rho_{r}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s

(10.15)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}r^{m}\rho_{0}|v_{0}|^{2}+\frac{A}{\gamma-1}r^{m}j_{\gamma}(\rho_{0})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C_{0}.

Finally, (
10.9
), together with the energy estimates and (
10.15
), yields

|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho})_{r}|_{2}=\frac{1}{2}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\log\rho)_{r}\big|_{2}\leq C_{0}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}(v,u)\big|_{2}\leq C_{0}.

∎

Clearly, in this case, Corollary
4.1
in §
4
still holds. We present it here for later use.

Corollary 10.1
.

The effective velocity
v
satisfies the following equation
:

v_{t}+uv_{r}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\rho^{\gamma-1}(v-u)=0.

(10.16)

Besides, we can also obtain the following estimates of
\rho
:

Lemma 10.3
.

j_{\gamma}(\rho)(t,r)\geq 0
for all
(t,r)\in[0,T]\times I
. Moreover, for any
\sigma\in(0,\bar{\rho})
,
there exists a constant
C(\sigma)>0
such that,
for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\big|\chi_{E_{\sigma}}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho(t)-\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}+\big|\chi_{E^{\sigma}}r^{\frac{m}{\gamma}}(\rho(t)-\bar{\rho})\big|_{\gamma}\leq C(\sigma).

Proof.

Since
j_{\gamma}(\rho)\geq 0
, it follows from Lemmas
10.2
and
B.1
that
\|j_{\gamma}(\rho)(t)\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{0}
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Then, following the proof of Lemma 5.3 on

[
58
, Page 43]
, we obtain

\big\|\chi_{E_{\sigma}}(\rho(t)-\bar{\rho})\big\|_{L^{2}}+\big\|\chi_{E^{\sigma}}(\rho(t)-\bar{\rho})\big\|_{L^{\gamma}}\leq C(\sigma),

(10.17)

for
\sigma\in(0,\bar{\rho})
, which, along with Lemma
B.1
, yields the desired estimates.

For the reader’s convenience, we still provide a brief proof of (
10.17
).
Let
\sigma\in(0,\bar{\rho})
. First, on the set
E_{\sigma}
,
\bar{\rho}-\sigma\leq\rho\leq 2\bar{\rho}
. Then it follows from (
10.2
) that

j_{\gamma}(\rho)=\frac{\gamma(\gamma-1)}{2}\rho_{\vartheta}^{\gamma-2}(\rho-\bar{\rho})^{2}\geq C(\sigma)^{-1}(\rho-\bar{\rho})^{2},

where
\rho_{\vartheta}=\vartheta\rho+(1-\vartheta)\bar{\rho}
. This implies that
\big\|\chi_{E_{\sigma}}(\rho(t)-\bar{\rho})\big\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\sigma)
.

Next, on the set
E^{\sigma}
, we can show that

j_{\gamma}(\rho)\geq C_{0}^{-1}|\rho-\bar{\rho}|^{\gamma}.

(10.18)

Indeed, define the function

J_{\gamma}(z):=\frac{j_{\gamma}(z)}{|z-\bar{\rho}|^{\gamma}}=\frac{(z^{\gamma}-\bar{\rho}^{\gamma})-\gamma\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}(z-\bar{\rho})}{|z-\bar{\rho}|^{\gamma}}\qquad\,\,\text{for $|z-\bar{\rho}|>\sigma$ and $z\geq 0$}.

A direct calculation gives that

\displaystyle J_{\gamma}^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{z-\bar{\rho}}{|z-\bar{\rho}|}\frac{H_{\gamma}(z)}{|z-\bar{\rho}|^{\gamma+1}}\qquad\text{with $H_{\gamma}(z):=j_{\gamma}^{\prime}(z)(z-\bar{\rho})-\gamma j_{\gamma}(z)$},

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{\gamma}(z)

\displaystyle=j_{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}(z)(z-\bar{\rho})-(\gamma-1)j_{\gamma}^{\prime}(z)=\gamma(\gamma-1)\bar{\rho}(\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-2}-z^{\gamma-2}).

This yields that
J^{\prime}_{\gamma}(z)<0
on
(\bar{\rho}+\sigma,\infty)
,

J^{\prime}_{\gamma}(z)>0
on
(0,\bar{\rho}-\sigma)
, and
J_{\gamma}(z)
is strictly decreasing on
(\bar{\rho}+\sigma,\infty)
and strictly increasing on
(0,\bar{\rho}-\sigma)
. Hence, we see that

\displaystyle\frac{j_{\gamma}(z)}{|z-\bar{\rho}|^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=J_{\gamma}(z)\geq\lim_{z\to\infty}J_{\gamma}(z)=1

\displaystyle\text{for all $z\in(\bar{\rho}+\sigma,\infty)$},

\displaystyle\frac{j_{\gamma}(z)}{|z-\bar{\rho}|^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=J_{\gamma}(z)\geq\lim_{z\to 0}J_{\gamma}(z)=\gamma-1

\displaystyle\text{for all $z\in(0,\bar{\rho}-\sigma)$}.

Setting
z=\rho
above yields claim (
10.18
).
Therefore, it follows from (
10.18
) and Lemma
10.1
that
\big\|\chi_{E^{\sigma}}(\rho(t)-\bar{\rho})\big\|_{L^{\gamma}}\leq C_{0}
.
∎

The next lemma concerns the weighted
L^{p}(0,\omega)
-estimates of
\rho

for
\omega>0
and
p\in[1,\infty]
.

Lemma 10.4
.

For any
t\in[0,T]
and
\omega>0
,

\mathrm{(i)}

When
n=2
, there exist two positive constants
C(p,\nu,\omega)
and
C(\nu,\omega)

such that

\displaystyle\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r^{\nu}\rho(t)\big|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(p,\nu,\omega)

\displaystyle\text{for any }\nu>-\frac{1}{p}\text{ and }p\in[1,\infty),

(10.19)

\displaystyle\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r^{\nu}\rho(t)\big|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu,\omega)

\displaystyle\text{for any }\nu>0;

\mathrm{(ii)}

When
n=3
, there exist two positive constants
C(p,\omega)
and
C(\omega)
such that

\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\rho(t)\big|_{p}\leq C(p,\omega)\ \ \text{for any $p\in[1,\infty)$},\qquad\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r\rho(t)\big|_{\infty}\leq C(\omega).

(10.20)

Proof.

Since we focus only on the estimates of
\rho
on
[0,\omega]
for
\omega>0
,
we perform the argument as in the proof of Lemma
4.3
and use Lemmas
10.2
–
10.3
and
A.3
to obtain the desired conclusions.
Here, for brevity, we only sketch the proof for the 3-D case.

First, it follows from Lemma
10.3
and the Hölder inequality that, for fixed
\sigma\in(0,\bar{\rho})
,

\displaystyle|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r\sqrt{\rho}|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r^{2}\rho|_{1}\leq\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}\chi_{E_{\sigma}}r^{2}\rho\big|_{1}+\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}\chi_{E^{\sigma}}r^{2}\rho\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}\chi_{E_{\sigma}}r^{2}(\rho-\bar{\rho})\big|_{1}+\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}\chi_{E^{\sigma}}r^{2}(\rho-\bar{\rho})\big|_{1}+C(\omega)

\displaystyle\leq|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r|_{2}\big|\chi_{E_{\sigma}}r(\rho-\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}+|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r|_{2}^{\frac{2\gamma-2}{\gamma}}\big|\chi_{E^{\sigma}}r^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}(\rho-\bar{\rho})\big|_{\gamma}+C(\omega)\leq C(\omega).

Next, let
p\in[1,\infty)
.
According to the above estimate and Lemmas
10.2
and
A.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\rho\big|_{p}

\displaystyle=\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2p}}\sqrt{\rho}\big|_{2p}^{2}\leq C(p,\omega)\big(\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r\sqrt{\rho}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r(\sqrt{\rho})_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}\big)\leq C(p,\omega),

\displaystyle\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r\rho\big|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\rho}\big|_{\infty}^{2}\leq C(\omega)\big(\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r\sqrt{\rho}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\flat}r(\sqrt{\rho})_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}\big)\leq C(\omega),

which thus leads to (
10.20
).
∎

Now, based on Lemmas
10.3
–
10.4
, we can obtain the global uniform upper bound of
\rho
in the exterior domain.

Lemma 10.5
.

There exist a constant
C_{0}>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,
p\in[2,\infty]
,
and
\omega\in(0,\infty)
,

\big|\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}(t)-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{p}\leq C_{0}.

(10.21)

In particular, for any
\omega\in(0,\infty)
, there exist a constant
C(\omega)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}\rho(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(\omega).

(10.22)

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1.
We first show that Lemma
10.5
holds for all
\omega\in[\omega_{0},\infty)
with some constant
\omega_{0}>0
depending only on
(n,\alpha,\gamma,A,\bar{\rho})
. Let
\omega>0
and
\sigma\in(0,\bar{\rho})
. It follows from Lemma
10.3
and the Hölder and Chebyshev inequality (Lemma
A.9
) that

\displaystyle\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}\chi_{E^{\sigma}}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}^{2}

(10.23)

\displaystyle\leq\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{\infty}\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}\Big(\int_{\omega}^{\infty}\chi_{E^{\sigma}}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\omega^{-\frac{m}{2}}\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{\infty}\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}\Big(\int_{0}^{\infty}\chi_{E^{\sigma}}r^{m}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\omega^{-\frac{m}{2}}\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{\infty}\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}\frac{\big|r^{\frac{m}{\gamma}}(\rho(t)-\bar{\rho})\chi_{E^{\sigma}}\big|_{\gamma}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}}{\sigma^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\sigma)\omega^{-\frac{m}{2}}\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{\infty}\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}.

On the other hand, it follows from Lemmas
10.2
and
A.4
,
and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\omega}^{\infty}\big|\big(r^{m}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})^{2}\big)_{r}\big|\,\mathrm{d}r

(10.24)

\displaystyle\leq\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho})_{r}\big|_{2}+m\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m-1}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}+m\omega^{-1}\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}^{2}.

Substituting the above in to (
10.23
) leads to

\displaystyle\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}\chi_{E^{\sigma}}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\sigma)\omega^{-\frac{m}{4}}\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}^{\frac{3}{4}}

(10.25)

\displaystyle+C(\sigma)\omega^{-\frac{m+1}{4}}\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}.

Next, it follows from the Taylor expansion that

\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\rho_{\vartheta}}}(\rho-\bar{\rho}),

where
\rho_{\vartheta}=\vartheta\rho+(1-\vartheta)\bar{\rho}
for some
\vartheta\in[0,1]
.
If
r\in E_{\sigma}
and
\sigma\in(0,\frac{\bar{\rho}}{2})
, then
\rho_{\vartheta}\in[\frac{\bar{\rho}}{2},\frac{3\bar{\rho}}{2}]
so that, for all
(t,r)\in[0,T]\times I
,

C_{0}^{-1}\big(\chi_{E_{\sigma}}|\rho-\bar{\rho}|\big)\leq\chi_{E_{\sigma}}|\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}}|\leq C_{0}\big(\chi_{E_{\sigma}}|\rho-\bar{\rho}|\big),

which, along with Lemma
10.3
, implies that

\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}\chi_{E_{\sigma}}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}\leq C_{0}\big|\chi_{E_{\sigma}}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho(t)-\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}\leq C(\sigma).

(10.26)

Therefore, choosing suitable fixed
\sigma\in(0,\frac{\bar{\rho}}{2})

and collecting (
10.25
)–(
10.26
),
we obtain from the Young inequality that

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}(t)-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}

(10.27)

\displaystyle\leq\big|\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega}\chi_{E^{\sigma}}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}(t)-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega}\chi_{E_{\sigma}}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}(t)-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\omega^{-\frac{m}{4}}\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}^{\frac{3}{4}}+C_{0}\omega^{-\frac{m+1}{4}}\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}+C_{0}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(\omega^{-\frac{m}{3}}+\omega^{-\frac{m+1}{4}}\big)\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}+C_{0}.

Choosing
\omega_{0}>0
sufficiently large such that

\omega_{0}^{-\frac{m}{3}}+\omega_{0}^{-\frac{m+1}{4}}=(2C_{0})^{-1},

(10.28)

we obtain from (
10.27
) that, for all
t\in[0,T]
and
\omega\in[\omega_{0},\infty)
,

\big|\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}(t)-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}\leq C_{0}.

(10.29)

Of course, we also obtain from the above and (
10.24
) that,
for all
t\in[0,T]
and
\omega\in[\omega_{0},\infty)
,

\big|\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}(t)-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}.

(10.30)

The
L^{p}(I)
-estimates (
p\in(2,\infty)
) of
\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}(t)-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})
follow from (
10.29
)–(
10.30
). Finally, it follows from (
10.30
) that, for all
t\in[0,T]
and
\omega\in[\omega_{0},\infty)
,

\displaystyle|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}\rho|_{\infty}=|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}\sqrt{\rho}|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})|_{\infty}^{2}+C_{0}\bar{\rho}

(10.31)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\omega_{0}^{-m}\big|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{\infty}^{2}+C_{0}\bar{\rho}\leq C_{0}.

To sum up, we have shown that Lemma
10.5
holds for all
\omega\in[\omega_{0},\infty)
with some constant
\omega_{0}>0
, depending only on
(n,\alpha,\gamma,A,\bar{\rho})

2.
Now, based on Lemma
10.4
, we can show that Lemma
10.5
holds for arbitrary constant
\omega>0
. For brevity, we take
n=3

(m=2)
as an example, since the 2-D case can be derived analogously. Let
\omega_{0}
be defined in (
10.28
).
Due to Lemma
10.4
and the fact that (
10.21
) holds for
\omega\in[\omega_{0},\infty)
,
then, for arbitrary
\omega\in(0,\omega_{0})
,

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega}r(\sqrt{\rho}(t)-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\chi_{[\omega,\omega_{0}]}r(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{p}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega_{0}}r(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq|\chi_{\omega_{0}}^{\flat}\sqrt{r}|_{p}\big|\chi_{\omega_{0}}^{\flat}\sqrt{r}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{\infty}+C_{0}\leq C_{0}\big|\chi_{\omega_{0}}^{\flat}r\rho\big|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}+C_{0}\leq C_{0},

where
p\in[2,\infty]
. Similarly, for
\omega\in(0,\omega_{0})
, it follows from Lemma
10.4
and (
10.31
) that

|\chi_{\omega}^{\sharp}\rho|_{\infty}\leq\big|\chi_{[\omega,\omega_{0}]}\rho\big|_{\infty}+|\chi_{\omega_{0}}^{\sharp}\rho|_{\infty}\leq\omega^{-1}|\chi_{\omega_{0}}^{\flat}r\rho|_{\infty}+C_{0}\leq C(\omega),

which thus leads to (
10.22
).
∎

Due to Lemmas
10.4
–
10.5
, we can obtain the
L^{2}(I)
-estimate of
r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho-\bar{\rho})
.

Lemma 10.6
.

There exists a constant
C_{0}>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho(t)-\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}\leq C_{0}.

Proof.

This can be seen directly from Lemmas
10.4
–
10.5
that

\displaystyle\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho-\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\sqrt{\rho}-\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})\big|_{2}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}(\sqrt{\rho}+\sqrt{\bar{\rho}})|_{\infty}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho-\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}\rho|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho|_{2}+C_{0}\leq C_{0}.

∎

Next, we can show the
L^{p}(I)
-estimates
(p\in[4,\infty))
of
(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u
.

Lemma 10.7
.

Let
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
. For any
p\in[4,\infty)
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,
there exist two positive constants
C(p)
and
C(p,\epsilon)
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+p\alpha\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)

(10.32)

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p-2}}u\big|_{p-2}^{p-2}+C(p,\epsilon)+\epsilon\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1.
Let
p\in[4,\infty)
. Multiplying
\eqref{e1.5hh}_{2}
by
r^{m}|u|^{p-2}u
, along with
\eqref{e1.5hh}_{1}
, gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}(r^{m}\rho|u|^{p})_{t}+2\alpha(p-1)r^{m}\rho|u|^{p-2}|u_{r}|^{2}+2\alpha mr^{m-2}\rho|u|^{p}

(10.33)

\displaystyle=(p-1)Ar^{m}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u_{r}+mAr^{m-1}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u

\displaystyle+\Big(\underline{2\alpha r^{m}\rho|u|^{p-2}uu_{r}-Ar^{m}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u-\frac{1}{p}r^{m}\rho u|u|^{p}}_{:=\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{2}}\Big)_{r}.

Next, we need to show that
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{2}\in W^{1,1}(I)
and
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{2}|_{r=0}=0
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, which allows us to apply Lemma
A.4
to obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{2})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{2}|_{r=0}=0.

(10.34)

\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{2}|_{r=0}=0
follows easily from the fact that
(\rho,u,u_{r})\in C(\bar{I})
for each
t\in(0,T]
due to (
10.6
) (or (
10.8
)). On the other hand, based on (
10.5
)–(
10.6
) (or (
10.7
)–(
10.8
)), one has

\Big(\rho,\rho_{r},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r}\Big)\in L^{\infty}(I),\quad\,r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\rho_{r},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r}\Big)\in L^{2}(I)\qquad\,\mbox{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$}.

Then we obtain from
p\in[4,\infty)
and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{2}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|r^{m}\big(\rho|u|^{p-1}|u_{r}|,\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-1},\rho|u|^{p+1}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma}|u|_{\infty}^{p-3}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}+|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-1}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}\big)<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{2})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|r^{m-1}\big(\rho|u|^{p-1}|u_{r}|,\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-1},\rho|u|^{p+1}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big|r^{m}\big(\rho_{r}|u|^{p-1}u_{r},\rho|u|^{p-2}u_{r}^{2},\rho|u|^{p-1}u_{rr}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big|r^{m}\big(\rho^{\gamma-1}\rho_{r}|u|^{p-1},\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u_{r},\rho_{r}|u|^{p+1},\rho|u|^{p}u_{r}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\big|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}u\big|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma}|u|_{\infty}^{p-3}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}\big|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}u\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-1}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}\big|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}u\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\big(|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}\!+\!|\rho|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}^{2}\!+\!|\rho|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big(|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}\big|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}+|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma}|u|_{\infty}^{p-3}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}|u|^{p}_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}+|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-1}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}\big)<\infty.

Thus, integrating (
10.33
) over
I
, together with (
10.34
), leads to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+2\alpha(p-1)\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+2\alpha m\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}

(10.35)

\displaystyle=(p-1)A\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r+mA\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m-1}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u\,\mathrm{d}r:=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{I}_{i}.

2. Estimate of
\mathcal{I}_{1}
.
For
\mathcal{I}_{1}
, it follows from the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big|_{2}

(10.36)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{8}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}u^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+C(p)\underline{\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big|_{2}^{2}}_{:=\mathcal{I}_{1}^{\flat}}

\displaystyle+C(p)\underline{\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big|_{2}^{2}}_{:=\mathcal{I}_{1}^{\sharp}}.

The calculation of
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{\flat}
is the same as that of Step 2 in the proof of Lemma
4.4
. Indeed, for any
p\in[4,\infty)
, it follows from the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\mathcal{I}_{1}^{\flat}\leq\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{p+m-2}{p\gamma-p+1}}\rho\big|_{p\gamma-p+1}^{\frac{2p\gamma-2p+2}{p}}\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p-2}\!\leq\frac{\alpha}{8}\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\underline{\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{p+m-2}{p\gamma-p+1}}\rho\big|_{p\gamma-p+1}^{p\gamma-p+1}}_{:=\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{\flat}}\!\!.

Notice that the term
\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{\flat}
is nothing but
\mathcal{G}_{1,1}

in Step 2 of the proof of Lemma
4.4
.
Thus, it follows from Lemma
10.4
and Step 2 in the proof of Lemma
4.4
that, for all
p\in[4,\infty)
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

\mathcal{I}_{1}^{\flat}\leq\frac{\alpha}{8}\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p,\epsilon)+\epsilon\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}.

(10.37)

For the estimate of
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{\sharp}
, we employ Lemma
10.5
to obtain

\mathcal{I}_{1}^{\sharp}\leq|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p-2}}u\big|_{p-2}^{p-2}\leq C_{0}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p-2}}u\big|_{p-2}^{p-2}.

(10.38)

Thus, collecting (
10.36
)–(
10.38
), we obtain that,
for all
p\in[4,\infty)
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{8}\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}u^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)+C(p)\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p-2}}u\big|_{p-2}^{p-2}

(10.39)

\displaystyle+C(p,\epsilon)+\epsilon\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}.

3. Estimate of
\mathcal{I}_{2}
.

Now we deal with
\mathcal{I}_{2}
. It follows from (
10.37
)–(
10.38
) and the Hölder inequality that, for all
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{\frac{p}{2}}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big|_{2}

(10.40)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{8}\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)(\mathcal{I}_{1}^{\flat}+\mathcal{I}_{1}^{\sharp})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{4}\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p-2}}u\big|_{p-2}^{p-2}+C(p,\epsilon)+\epsilon\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}.

Substituting (
10.39
)–(
10.40
) into (
10.35
) gives the desired conclusion of this lemma.
∎

In addition, we can show the following
L^{p}(I)
-estimates of
(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}v

for
p\in[4,\infty)
:

Lemma 10.8
.

Let
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
.
Then, for any
p\in[4,\infty)
, there exists a constant
C(p)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{A\gamma p}{4\alpha}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}\leq C(p)\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big).

(10.41)

Proof.

First, multiplying (
10.16
) by
r^{m}\rho|v|^{p-2}v
with
p\in[4,\infty)
, along with
\eqref{e1.5hh}_{1}
, gives

\frac{1}{p}\big(r^{m}\rho|v|^{p}\big)_{t}+\frac{1}{p}\big(\underline{r^{m}\rho u|v|^{p}}_{:=\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{3}}\big)_{r}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}|v|^{p}=\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}uv|v|^{p-2}.

(10.42)

Next, we need to show that
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{3}\in W^{1,1}(I)
and
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{3}|_{r=0}=0
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, which allows us to apply Lemma
A.4
to obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{3})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{3}|_{r=0}=0.

(10.43)

On one hand, we obtain
\mathcal{B}_{3}|_{r=0}=0
from (
10.9
)
and the fact that
(\rho,\rho_{r},u)\in C(\bar{I})
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)

due to (
10.5
)–(
10.6
) (or (
10.7
)–(
10.8
)).
On the other hand, based on (
10.5
)–(
10.6
) (or (
10.7
)–(
10.8
)),
we have

r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\rho_{r},\frac{\rho_{r}}{r},\rho_{rr},u\Big)\in L^{2}(I),\quad\,\Big(\rho,\rho^{-1},\rho_{r},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r}\Big)\in L^{\infty}(I)\qquad\,\,\mbox{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$}.

Then we obtain from the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{3}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big(\big|r^{m}\rho|u|^{p+1}\big|_{1}+\big|r^{m}\rho u|(\log\rho)_{r}|^{p}\big|_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big(|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-1}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}+|\rho^{1-p}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}^{p-2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}^{2}\big)<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{3})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big(\big|r^{m-1}\rho|u|^{p+1}\big|_{1}+\big|r^{m-1}\rho u|(\log\rho)_{r}|^{p}\big|_{1}+\big|r^{m}\rho_{r}|u|^{p+1}\big|_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big(\big|r^{m}\rho_{r}u|(\log\rho)_{r}|^{p}\big|_{1}+\big|r^{m}\rho|u|^{p}u_{r}\big|_{1}+\big|r^{m}\rho u_{r}|(\log\rho)_{r}|^{p}\big|_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big|r^{m}\rho u|(\log\rho)_{r}|^{p-1}|(\log\rho)_{rr}|\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big(|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-1}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}\big|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}u\big|_{2}\!+\!|\rho^{1-p}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}^{p-2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}\big|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}\rho_{r}\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|u|_{\infty}^{p}+C(p)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|\rho^{-p}|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}^{p}
