\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}\big(|u|_{\infty}^{p-1}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}+|\rho^{-p}|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}^{p-1}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|\rho^{2-p}|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}^{p-1}\big(|\rho^{-2}|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}\!+\!|\rho^{-1}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{rr}|_{2}\big)<\infty.

Integrating (
10.42
) over
I
, then we obtain from (
10.43
), the fact that
\frac{2}{\gamma-1}>1
whenever
\gamma\in(1,3)
, Lemmas
10.4
–
10.5
, and the Young inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}

(10.44)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}uv|v|^{p-2}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq\frac{A\gamma}{4\alpha}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A\gamma}{4\alpha}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\Big(\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A\gamma}{4\alpha}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{2}{\gamma-1}}\rho\big|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A\gamma}{4\alpha}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p)\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big),

which implies the desired estimates of this lemma.
∎

Based on Lemmas
10.7
–
10.8
, we can derive the following important estimates on
(u,v)
:

Lemma 10.9
.

Let
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
. Then, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
,
there exists a constant
C(p,T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u(t)\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}v(t)\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(p,T).

Proof.

First, multiplying (
10.41
) by
\frac{8\alpha\epsilon}{A\gamma p}
with
\epsilon\in(0,1)
leads to

\frac{8\alpha\epsilon}{A\gamma p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+2\epsilon\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}\leq C(p)\epsilon\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big).

Then summing the above inequality with (
10.32
) yields that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{8\alpha\epsilon}{A\gamma p}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)

\displaystyle+p\alpha\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)+\epsilon\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p-2}}u\big|_{p-2}^{p-2}\Big)+C(p,\epsilon)+C(p)\epsilon\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}.

As a consequence, setting

\epsilon=\min\big\{\frac{\alpha}{C(p)},\frac{1}{2}\big\},

and then applying the Grönwall inequality imply that, for all
t\in[0,T]
and
p\in[4,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)

(10.45)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(p,T)\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p-2}}u\big|_{p-2}^{p-2}+C(p,T).

Here, we still need to check the
L^{p}(I)
-boundedness of
(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}(u_{0},v_{0})
.
Indeed, it follows from Lemmas
10.1
,
A.1
, and
B.1
that

\displaystyle\big|(r^{m}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}(u_{0},v_{0})\big|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{p}}\big|r^{\frac{m}{p}}(u_{0},(\log\rho_{0})_{r})\big|_{p}\leq C_{0}\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{p}}\|(\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\nabla\log\rho_{0})\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(\|(\rho_{0}-\bar{\rho})\|_{H^{2}}+1\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|(\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\nabla\log\rho_{0})\|_{H^{1}}\leq C(p).

Next, setting
p=4
in (
10.45
), together with Lemma
10.2
, yields

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{4}}u\big|_{4}^{4}+\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{4}}v\big|_{4}^{4}\Big)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{4}}u\big|_{4}^{4}+\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{4}}v\big|_{4}^{4}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C(T).

Hence, taking
p=2N
with
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
N\geq 3
in (
10.45
),
we can iteratively obtain from the above
and Lemma
10.2
that, for all
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2N}}u\big|_{2N}^{2N}+\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2N}}v\big|_{2N}^{2N}\Big)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{N-1}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2N}}u\big|_{2N}^{2N}+\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma})^{\frac{1}{2N}}v\big|_{2N}^{2N}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C(N,T),

which, along with the interpolation, leads to the desired result.
∎

Now, based on Lemmas
10.5
and
10.9
, using the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
4.7
, we can obtain the global uniform upper bound of
\rho
in
[0,T]\times I
.

Lemma 10.10
.

Let
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
. Then there exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

|\rho(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for any $t\in[0,T]$}.

10.6.
Global uniform
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate of
v

In §
10.6
, we always assume that
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
.
This subsection is devoted to providing the uniform boundedness of
v
.
To get this, we first derive the
L^{p}(I)
-estimates of
\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u
.

Lemma 10.11
.

For any
p\in[4,\infty)
,
there exists a constant
C(p,T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u(t)\big|_{p}^{p}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(p,T)\Big(\big(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|v|_{\infty}\big)^{2}+1\Big).

Proof.

First, multiplying both sides of
\eqref{e1.5hh}_{2}
by
|u|^{p-2}u
with
p\in[4,\infty)
, together with
\eqref{e1.5hh}_{1}
and (
10.9
), leads to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\big(\rho|u|^{p}\big)_{t}+2\alpha(p-1)\rho|u|^{p-2}|u_{r}|^{2}+\frac{2\alpha m(p-1)}{p}\frac{\rho|u|^{p}}{r^{2}}-A(p-1)\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u_{r}

(10.46)

\displaystyle=\Big(2\alpha\rho u_{r}|u|^{p-2}u+\frac{2\alpha m}{p}\frac{\rho|u|^{p}}{r}-\frac{1}{p}\rho u|u|^{p}-A\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u\Big)_{r}-\frac{m}{p}\frac{\rho v|u|^{p}}{r}.

Next, we need to show that
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{4}\in W^{1,1}(I)
and
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{4}|_{r=0}=0
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, which allows us to apply Lemma
A.4
to obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{4})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{4}|_{r=0}=0.

(10.47)

On one hand,
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{4}|_{r=0}=0
follows directly from
p\geq 4
,
u|_{r=0}=0
and the fact that
(\rho,u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r})\in C(\bar{I})
for each
t\in(0,T]
due to (
10.6
) (or (
10.8
)).

On the other hand, when
n=2

(m=1)
, (
10.5
)–(
10.6
) (or (
10.7
)–(
10.8
)) imply that

\sqrt{r}\Big(u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r},u_{rr}\Big)\in L^{2}(I),\quad\,\,\Big(\rho,\rho^{-1},\rho_{r},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r}\Big)\in L^{\infty}(I)\qquad\,\,\mbox{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$},

which, along with the Hölder inequality, yields

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{4}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\Big(\big|\rho|u|^{p-1}|u_{r}|\big|_{1}+\Big|\frac{1}{r}\rho|u|^{p}\Big|_{1}+\big|\rho|u|^{p+1}\big|_{1}+\big|\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-1}\big|_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\Big(\Big|\frac{u}{\sqrt{r}}\Big|_{2}|\sqrt{r}u_{r}|_{2}+\Big|\frac{u}{\sqrt{r}}\Big|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-1}|\sqrt{r}u|_{2}\Big|\frac{u}{\sqrt{r}}\Big|_{2}+C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}|\sqrt{r}u|_{2}\Big|\frac{u}{\sqrt{r}}\Big|_{2}<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{4})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|\big(|\rho_{r}||u_{r}||u|^{p-1},\rho|u_{rr}||u|^{p-1},\rho u_{r}^{2}|u|^{p-2}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\Big|\Big(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\rho|u|^{p},\frac{1}{r}\rho_{r}|u|^{p},\frac{1}{r}\rho|u|^{p-1}|u_{r}|\Big)\Big|_{1}\!\!+\!C(p)\big|\big(|\rho_{r}||u|^{p+1},\rho|u|^{p}|u_{r}|\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big|\big(\rho^{\gamma-1}|\rho_{r}||u|^{p-1},\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}|u_{r}|\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}\Big|\frac{u}{\sqrt{r}}\Big|_{2}\big(|\rho^{-1}|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|\sqrt{r}u_{r}|_{2}+|\sqrt{r}u_{rr}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-4}|\sqrt{r}u|_{2}\Big|\frac{u}{\sqrt{r}}\Big|_{2}\Big(|u_{r}|_{\infty}^{2}+\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|u|_{\infty}^{p-4}\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}|\sqrt{r}u|_{2}\Big|\frac{u}{\sqrt{r}}\Big|_{2}\big(|u|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}+|\rho|_{\infty}|u_{r}|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}|\sqrt{r}u|_{2}\Big|\frac{u}{\sqrt{r}}\Big|_{2}\big(|u|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}+|\rho|_{\infty}|u_{r}|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|u|_{\infty}^{p-4}|\sqrt{r}u|_{2}\Big|\frac{u}{\sqrt{r}}\Big|_{2}\big(|u|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}+|\rho|_{\infty}|u_{r}|_{\infty}\big)<\infty.

When
n=3

(m=2)
, by (
10.5
)–(
10.6
) (or (
10.7
)–(
10.8
)), we have

(u,ru_{r},ru_{rr})\in L^{2}(I),\quad\,\,\Big(\rho,\rho^{-1},\rho_{r},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r}\Big)\in L^{\infty}(I)\qquad\,\,\text{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$},

which yields from the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{4}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big(\big|\rho|u|^{p-1}|u_{r}|\big|_{1}+\Big|\frac{1}{r}\rho|u|^{p}\Big|_{1}+\big|\rho|u|^{p+1}\big|_{1}+\big|\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-1}\big|_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}^{p-3}|u|_{2}^{2}\Big(\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{\infty}+|u|_{\infty}^{2}+|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\Big)<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{4})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|\big(|\rho_{r}||u_{r}||u|^{p-1},\rho|u_{rr}||u|^{p-1},\rho u_{r}^{2}|u|^{p-2}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\Big|\Big(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\rho|u|^{p},\frac{1}{r}\rho_{r}|u|^{p},\frac{1}{r}\rho|u|^{p-1}|u_{r}|\Big)\Big|_{1}\!\!+\!C(p)\big|\big(|\rho_{r}||u|^{p+1},\rho|u|^{p}|u_{r}|\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\big|\big(\rho^{\gamma-1}|\rho_{r}||u|^{p-1},\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}|u_{r}|\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}\!|u|_{\infty}^{p-4}|u|_{2}\big(|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}|ru_{r}|_{2}\!+\!|\rho|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}|ru_{rr}|_{2}\!+\!|\rho|_{\infty}|u_{r}|_{\infty}|ru_{r}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|u|_{\infty}^{p-4}\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}|u|_{2}^{2}\Big(\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}|\rho|_{\infty}+|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}+|\rho|_{\infty}|u_{r}|_{\infty}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|u|_{\infty}^{p-2}|u|_{2}\Big(|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}|u|_{2}+|\rho|_{\infty}\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{\infty}|ru_{r}|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|u|_{\infty}^{p-4}|u|_{2}^{2}\big(|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}+|\rho|_{\infty}|u_{r}|_{\infty}\big)<\infty.

As a consequence, integrating (
10.46
) over
I
, we obtain from (
10.47
) that, for
p\in[4,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+2\alpha(p-1)\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\frac{2\alpha m(p-1)}{p}\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}

(10.48)

\displaystyle=A(p-1)\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r-\frac{m}{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\rho v|u|^{p}}{r}\,\mathrm{d}r:=\sum_{i=3}^{4}\mathcal{I}_{i}.

For
\mathcal{I}_{3}
, it follows from Lemma
10.10
and the Hölder and Young inequality that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u_{r}\big|_{1}+C(p)\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u_{r}\big|_{1}

(10.49)

\displaystyle\leq C(p)|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-4}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}\Big(|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{2-m}{2}}|_{\infty}\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{\frac{p}{2}}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}|_{\infty}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{\frac{p}{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(p,T)\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-4}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)+\frac{\alpha}{8}\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}.

For
\mathcal{I}_{4}
, we obtain from the same calculation of
\mathcal{G}_{3}
in the proof
of Lemma
5.1
that

\mathcal{I}_{4}\leq C(p)(|v|_{\infty}^{2}+1)\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\frac{\alpha}{8}\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}.

(10.50)

Thus, collecting (
10.48
)–(
10.50
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}

(10.51)

\displaystyle\leq C(p,T)\Big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-4}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)+C(p)(|v|_{\infty}^{2}+1)\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p},

which, along with the Grönwall inequality and Lemma
10.9
, yields that, for all
t\in[0,T]
and
p\in[4,\infty)
,

\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{p}}u(t)\big|_{p}^{p}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}\Big)\mathrm{d}s\leq C(p,T)\Big(\big(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|v|_{\infty}\big)^{2}+1\Big).

Here, we still need to check the
L^{p}(I)
-boundedness of
\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p}}u_{0}
. Indeed, it follows from Lemmas
A.1
and
B.1
that

\displaystyle\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p}}u_{0}\big|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p}}u_{0}\big|_{p}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p}}u_{0}\big|_{p}\leq|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{p}}|u_{0}|_{\infty}+|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{p}}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{p}}|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m}{p}}u_{0}\big|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{p}}\big)\leq C(p)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{H^{2}}\leq C(p).

This completes the proof.
∎

Next, based on Lemma
10.11
, we can obtain the
L^{1}([0,T];L^{\infty}(I))
-estimate of
\rho^{\gamma-1}u
.

Lemma 10.12
.

For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,
there exists a constant
C(\epsilon,T)>0
such that

\int_{0}^{t}|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(\epsilon,T)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}|v|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)+\epsilon\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|v|_{\infty}\qquad\text{for any $\,t\in[0,T]$}.

Proof.

Let
q\geq 4
be a constant to be determined later. First, it follows from (
10.9
), Lemma
A.1
,
and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}^{q}

\displaystyle=\big||\rho^{\gamma-1}u|^{q}\big|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{q\gamma-q}|u|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}r+C_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}|(\rho^{q\gamma-q}|u|^{q})_{r}|\,\mathrm{d}r

(10.52)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{q\gamma-q}|u|^{q}\mathrm{d}r\!+\!C(q)\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{q\gamma-q}\Big((|v|+|u|)|u|^{q}+|u|^{q-1}|u_{r}|\Big)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(q)(1+|v|_{\infty})\big|\rho^{q\gamma-q}u^{q}\big|_{1}+C(q)\big|\rho^{q\gamma-q}u^{q+1}\big|_{1}

\displaystyle+C(q)\big|\rho^{2q\gamma-2q-1}u^{q}\big|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{q-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(q)(1+|v|_{\infty})|\rho|_{\infty}^{q\gamma-q-1}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{q}}u\big|_{q}^{q}+C(q)|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}|\rho|_{\infty}^{(q-1)\gamma-q}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{q}}u\big|_{q}^{q}

\displaystyle+C(q)|\rho|_{\infty}^{q\gamma-q-1}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{q}}u\big|_{q}^{\frac{q}{2}}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{q-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}.

Next, setting
q=\tilde{q}\geq 4
in (
10.52
) large enough such that

\gamma>\frac{\tilde{q}}{\tilde{q}-1}>1,

then we obtain from the resulting inequality, Lemma
10.10
, and the Young inequality that

\displaystyle|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}^{\tilde{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)(1+|v|_{\infty})\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}+C(T)\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\frac{\tilde{q}^{2}}{\tilde{q}-1}}

\displaystyle\quad+C(T)\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\frac{\tilde{q}}{2}}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}+\frac{1}{2}|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}^{\tilde{q}},

which, along with the fact that
\tilde{q}\geq 4
, Lemma
10.9
,
and the Young inequality, leads to

\displaystyle|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(\big(1+|v|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}\big)\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\frac{\tilde{q}}{\tilde{q}-1}}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+|v|_{\infty}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{\frac{2}{2\tilde{q}-1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+|v|_{\infty}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}\rho^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{\frac{2}{2\tilde{q}-1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+|v|_{\infty}+|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{2-m}|_{\infty}\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(T)\Big(|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-m}|\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{\frac{2}{2\tilde{q}-1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+|v|_{\infty}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{\frac{2}{2\tilde{q}-1}}\Big).

Finally, integrating the above over
[0,t]
, we see from Lemmas
10.9
and
10.11
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that, for all
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|\rho^{\gamma-1}u|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\int_{0}^{t}\Big(1+|v|_{\infty}+\big|(r^{m-2}\rho)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}u\big|_{\tilde{q}}^{\tilde{q}}+\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{\frac{2}{2\tilde{q}-1}}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}|v|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)+C(T)\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\big|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\tilde{q}-2}{2}}u_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2\tilde{q}-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}|v|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)+C(T)\Big(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|v|_{\infty}\Big)^{\frac{2}{2\tilde{q}-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\epsilon,T)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}|v|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)+\epsilon\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|v|_{\infty}.

The proof of Lemma
10.12
is completed.
∎

Now, with the help of Lemma
10.12
, we obtain the
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate of
v
. The proof is identical to that of Lemma
5.3
, we omit it here for brevity.

Lemma 10.13
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that,

|v(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for any $t\in[0,T]$}.

10.7.
Global uniform lower bound of the density

In §
10.7
, we always assume that
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
.
By the
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimates of
(\rho,v)
, we are able to obtain the uniform lower bound of
\rho
in
[0,T]\times I
. Similar to the arguments presented in §
6
, we first show the following
L^{2}(I)
-estimate of
u
.

Lemma 10.14
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

|(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)(t)|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}+|u|^{2}_{\infty}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for any $t\in[0,T]$}.

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is similar to that of Lemma
6.1
, with merely minor modifications to the justifications of integration by parts. We divide the proof into two steps.

1.
First, multiplying
\eqref{e1.5hh}_{2}
by
u
, along with (
10.9
), gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(u^{2})_{t}+2\alpha|u_{r}|^{2}+\alpha m\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}

(10.53)

\displaystyle=\Big(\underline{2\alpha u_{r}u+\alpha m\frac{u^{2}}{r}-\frac{2}{3}u^{3}}_{:=\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{5}}\Big)_{r}-\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\rho^{\gamma-1}(v-u)u+vu_{r}u.

Here we need to show that
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{5}\in W^{1,1}(I)
and
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{5}|_{r=0}=0
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, which allows us to apply Lemma
A.4
to obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{5})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{5}|_{r=0}=0.

(10.54)

Since
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{5}
consists of
u
only, the proof of (
10.54
) is the same as that of (
6.2
) in the proof for Lemma
6.1
.

Thus, integrating (
10.53
) over
I
, together with (
10.54
), yields

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|u|_{2}^{2}+2\alpha|u_{r}|_{2}^{2}+\alpha m\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{2}^{2}=\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{\gamma-1}(v-u)u\,\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{\infty}vu_{r}u\,\mathrm{d}r:=\sum_{i=5}^{6}\mathcal{I}_{i}.

(10.55)

Then it follows from Lemmas
10.10
and
10.13
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{5}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(|\rho^{\gamma-1}v|_{2}|u|_{2}+|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|u|_{2}^{2}\big)\leq C(T)|(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)|_{2}^{2},

(10.56)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{6}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|v|_{\infty}|u|_{2}|u_{r}|_{2}\leq\frac{\alpha}{8}|u_{r}|_{2}^{2}+C(T)|u|_{2}^{2}.

Combining with (
10.55
)–(
10.56
) yields

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|u|_{2}^{2}+\alpha\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\leq C(T)|(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)|_{2}^{2}.

(10.57)

2.
For
|\rho^{\gamma-1}v|_{2}
, multiplying (
10.16
) by
\rho^{2\gamma-2}v
, together with
\eqref{e1.5hh}_{1}
, yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2})_{t}+\frac{1}{2}(\underline{u\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2}}_{:=\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{6}})_{r}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\rho^{3\gamma-3}v^{2}

(10.58)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{3}{2}-\gamma\Big)\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2}u_{r}-(\gamma-1)m\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2}\frac{u}{r}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\rho^{3\gamma-3}vu.

Next, we need to show that
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{6}\in W^{1,1}(I)
and
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{6}|_{r=0}=0

for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, which allows us to apply Lemma
A.4
to obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{6})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{6}|_{r=0}=0.

(10.59)

To show
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{6}|_{r=0}=0
, we first note that
v=u+2\alpha(\log\rho)_{r}
and

(\rho,\rho^{-1},u,\rho_{r})\in L^{\infty}(I)\qquad\,\,\mbox{ for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$}

due to (
10.5
)–(
10.6
) (or (
10.7
)–(
10.8
)), which implies that
v\in L^{\infty}(I)
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
.
Then it follows from
u|_{r=0}
and
(\rho,u)\in C(\bar{I})

for each
t\in(0,T]
(due to (
10.6
) or (
10.8
)) that
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{6}|_{r=0}=0
.
On the other hand, based on (
10.5
)–(
10.6
) (or (
10.7
)–(
10.8
)),
we have

(\rho,\rho^{-1},\rho_{r},u,u_{r})\in L^{\infty}(I),\quad\,\,r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u,u_{r},\rho_{r},\rho_{rr})\in L^{2}(I)\qquad\,\,\mbox{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$}.

Thus, one can first obtains from Lemma
A.3
that

\displaystyle|(u,\rho_{r})|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}(u,\rho_{r})\big|_{2}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}(u,\rho_{r})\big|_{2}\leq C_{0}\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r\big(u,u_{r},\rho_{r},\rho_{rr}\big)\big|_{2}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}(u,\rho_{r})\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{2-m}{2}}\big|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u,u_{r},\rho_{r},\rho_{rr}\big)\big|_{2}+\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}\big|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u,\rho_{r})|_{2}<\infty.

Then it follows from the above, (
10.9
), and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{6}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|u|_{\infty}\big(|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}|u|_{2}^{2}+|\rho^{2\gamma-4}|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{2}^{2}\big)<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{6})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|\big(u_{r}\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2},u\rho^{2\gamma-3}\rho_{r}v^{2},u\rho^{2\gamma-2}vv_{r}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}(|u_{r}|_{\infty}|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}+|\rho^{2\gamma-3}|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty})(|u|_{2}^{2}+|\rho^{-2}|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{2}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}\,|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}\big(|u|_{\infty}+|\rho^{-1}|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}\big)\Big|\frac{u}{r}\Big|_{2}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{2-m}{2}}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\times\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+|\rho^{-2}|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}+|\rho^{-1}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{rr}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}\,|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}\big(|u|_{\infty}+|\rho^{-1}|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}\big)|u|_{2}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\times\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+|\rho^{-2}|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}+|\rho^{-1}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{rr}|_{2}\big)<\infty.

Thus, integrating (
10.58
) over
I
, then we obtain from (
10.59
),
Lemmas
10.10
and
10.13
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|\rho^{\gamma-1}v|_{2}^{2}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\big|\rho^{\frac{3\gamma-3}{2}}v\big|_{2}^{2}

(10.60)

\displaystyle=\big(\frac{3}{2}-\gamma\big)\int\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2}u_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r-(\gamma-1)m\int\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2}\frac{u}{r}\,\mathrm{d}r+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}\int\rho^{3\gamma-3}vu\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|v|_{\infty}|\rho^{\gamma-1}v|_{2}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}|\rho^{\gamma-1}v|_{2}|u|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)|(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{8}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}.

Combining with (
10.57
) and (
10.60
) gives

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\leq C(T)|(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)|_{2}^{2},

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, yields that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

|(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)(t)|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

(10.61)

Here, it is still required to check the
L^{2}(I)
-boundedness of
(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}v_{0},u_{0})
.
Indeed, according to Lemmas
10.1
,
A.1
, and
B.1
, we have

\displaystyle|u_{0}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\chi_{1}^{\flat}u_{0}|_{2}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}u_{0}|_{2}\leq|u_{0}|_{\infty}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{0}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\leq C_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{H^{2}}\leq C_{0},

\displaystyle|\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}v_{0}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}(u_{0},(\log\rho_{0})_{r})|_{2}\leq C_{0}|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|u_{0},(\log\rho_{0})_{r}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}\big(u_{0},(\log\rho_{0})_{r}\big)\big|_{2}+C_{0}\big|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}\big(u_{0},(\log\rho_{0})_{r}\big)\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{2-m}{2}}|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}\big(u_{0},(\log\rho_{0})_{r}\big)\big|_{2}+C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{0},(\log\rho_{0})_{r}\big)\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big\|\big(\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\nabla\log\rho\big)\big\|_{H^{1}}\leq C_{0}.

Finally, it follows from (
10.61
) and Lemma
A.1
that

\int_{0}^{t}|u|^{2}_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{t}|(u,u_{r})|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C_{0}\Big(t\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|u|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}|u_{r}|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\leq C(T).

The proof of Lemma
10.14
is completed.
∎

Now, based on Lemmas
10.13
–
10.14
, we obtain the global uniform lower bound of
\rho
.

Lemma 10.15
.

For any
(t,r)\in[0,T]\times I
,

\rho(t,r)\geq C(T)^{-1},

where
C(T)\geq 1
is a constant depending only on
(T,\bar{\rho},\rho_{0},u_{0},n,\alpha,\gamma,A)
.

Proof.

First, it follows from (
10.9
), and Lemmas
10.13
–
10.14
and
A.1
that

\displaystyle\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{\infty}^{3}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|^{3}\,\mathrm{d}r+C_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|^{2}\big|(\log\rho)_{r}\big|\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|^{3}\,\mathrm{d}r+C_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|^{2}\big(|u|+|v|\big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|\big(\log(\rho/\bar{\rho}),v\big)\big|_{\infty}\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}^{2}+C_{0}\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{\infty}\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}|u|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(\big(\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{\infty}+1\big)\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{\infty}\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}\Big),

which, along with the Young inequality, leads to

\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\big(\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}+1\big).

(10.62)

Next, rewrite
\eqref{e1.5hh}_{1}
as

\big(\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big)_{t}+u(\log\rho)_{r}+\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)=0.

(10.63)

Multiplying the above by
\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})
and integrating over
I
, we obtain from (
10.9
), Lemmas
10.13
–
10.14
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{\infty}u(\log\rho)_{r}\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\,\mathrm{d}r-\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq|u|_{2}|(\log\rho)_{r}|_{\infty}\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}+C_{0}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Big(|u|_{2}^{2}|(u,v)|_{\infty}^{2}+\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\Big)+\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+|u|_{\infty}^{2}+\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\Big)+\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}^{2},

which, along with Lemma
10.14
and the Grönwall inequality, leads to

\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}\leq C(T)\big(\big|\log(\rho_{0}/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}+1\big).

(10.64)

For the
L^{2}(I)
-boundedness of
\log(\rho_{0}/\bar{\rho})
, observing that
\rho_{0}(r)\to\bar{\rho}
as
r\to\infty
, one can find a sufficiently large
R_{0}>0
, depending only on
\bar{\rho}
, such that, for all
r\in[R_{0},\infty)
,

\chi_{R_{0}}^{\sharp}\big|\log(\rho_{0}/\bar{\rho})\big|=\chi_{R_{0}}^{\sharp}\big|\log\big(1+(\rho_{0}/\bar{\rho}-1)\big)\big|\leq 2\chi_{R_{0}}^{\sharp}|\rho_{0}/\bar{\rho}-1|\leq C_{0}\chi_{R_{0}}^{\sharp}|\rho_{0}-\bar{\rho}|.

(10.65)

For such
R_{0}>0
, Lemmas
10.1
and
B.1
, together with
0<\inf_{r\in I}\rho_{0}\leq\rho_{0}\leq|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}
, imply

\displaystyle\big|\log(\rho_{0}/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\chi_{R_{0}}^{\flat}\log(\rho_{0}/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}+\big|\chi_{R_{0}}^{\sharp}\log(\rho_{0}/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}

(10.66)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sqrt{R_{0}}\big(|\log\rho_{0}|_{\infty}+|\log\bar{\rho}|\big)+C_{0}\big|\chi_{R_{0}}^{\sharp}(\rho_{0}-\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}+C_{0}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{0}-\bar{\rho})|_{2}\leq C_{0}+C_{0}\|\rho_{0}-\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{0}.

Thus, substituting above into (
10.64
), together with (
10.62
), gives

\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\big(\big|\log(\rho/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}+1\big)\leq C(T)\big(\big|\log(\rho_{0}/\bar{\rho})\big|_{2}+1\big)\leq C(T).

(10.67)

This implies that, for all
(t,r)\in[0,T]\times I
,
\rho(t,r)\geq C(T)^{-1}
for some constant
C(T)\geq 1
depending only on
(T,\bar{\rho},\rho_{0},u_{0},n,\alpha,\gamma,A)
.
∎

10.8.
Global uniform
a priori
estimates for the 2-order regular solutions

In §
10.8
,
we always assume that
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
.
Let
T>0
be any fixed time, and let
(\rho,u)(t,r)
be the
2
-order regular solution
of problem (
10.1
) in
[0,T]\times I
obtained in Theorem
10.3
.
To establish the global-in-time uniform estimates for the
2
-order regular solutions
when
\bar{\rho}>0
, we consider system (
10.3
) in spherical coordinates:

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\rho_{t}+u\rho_{r}+\rho\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)=0,\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle u_{t}+uu_{r}+\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{r}=2\alpha\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)_{r}-2\alpha\frac{\rho_{r}}{\rho}u_{r},\\[6.0pt]
\displaystyle(\rho,u)|_{t=0}=(\rho_{0},u_{0})\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ \text{for $r\in I$},\\[5.0pt]
\displaystyle u|_{r=0}=0\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\text{for $t\in[0,T]$},\\[5.0pt]
\displaystyle(\rho,u)\to\left(\bar{\rho}>0,0\right)\ \ \text{as $r\to\infty$}\qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T]$}.\end{cases}

(10.68)

Besides, let
(\phi,\psi,\boldsymbol{\psi})
be defined as in (
3.1
) and (
7.1
):

\displaystyle\phi=\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\rho^{\gamma-1},\qquad\psi=(\log\rho)_{r},\qquad\boldsymbol{\psi}=\nabla\log\rho.

(10.69)

The first lemma is on the zeroth-order energy estimate of
u
.

Lemma 10.16
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u,\rho^{\gamma-1}v)(t)\big|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1.
Using the same arguments as in Step 1 of the proof for Lemma
7.2
, with
(\phi,\psi)
replaced by
(\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\rho^{\gamma-1},(\log\rho)_{r})
, we obtain (
7.14
):

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}+\alpha\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\leq C(T)(1+|u|_{\infty}^{2})|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho^{\gamma-1}v,u)|_{2}^{2}.

(10.70)

2.
For the
L^{2}(I)
-estimate of
r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho^{\gamma-1}v
, one can first multiply (
10.16
) by
r^{m}\rho^{2\gamma-2}v
and then obtain from
\eqref{re-r}_{1}
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(r^{m}\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2})_{t}+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}r^{m}\rho^{3\gamma-3}v^{2}

(10.71)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}(\underline{r^{m}u\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2}}_{:=\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{7}})_{r}+\big(\frac{3}{2}-\gamma\big)r^{m}\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2}\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)+\frac{A\gamma}{2\alpha}r^{m}\rho^{3\gamma-3}vu.

Next, we need to show that
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{7}\in W^{1,1}(I)
and
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{7}|_{r=0}=0
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, which allows us to apply Lemma
A.4
to obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{7})_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=-\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{7}|_{r=0}=0.

(10.72)

On one hand,
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{7}|_{r=0}=0
follows from
v\in L^{\infty}(I)
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
, and
u|_{r=0}=0
and
(\rho,u)\in C(\bar{I})
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T]
due to (
10.5
). On the other hand, thanks to (
10.5
)–(
10.6
),

\Big(\rho,\rho^{-1},\rho_{r},u,\frac{u}{r},u_{r}\Big)\in L^{\infty}(I),\quad\,\,r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{r},\rho_{rr},u,u_{r})\in L^{2}(I)\qquad\,\,\mbox{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$},

we obtain from (
10.9
) and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{7}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|u|_{\infty}\big(|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}+|\rho^{2\gamma-4}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}^{2}\big)<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{7})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big|\big(r^{m-1}u\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2},r^{m}u_{r}\rho^{2\gamma-2}v^{2},r^{m}u\rho^{2\gamma-3}\rho_{r}v^{2},r^{m}u\rho^{2\gamma-2}vv_{r}\big)\big|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Big|\big(\frac{u}{r},u_{r}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\big(|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}^{2}+|\rho^{2\gamma-4}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|\rho^{2\gamma-3}|_{\infty}|v|_{\infty}^{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}|v|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{2\gamma-2}|v|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u|_{2}\big(|\rho^{-2}|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}+|\rho^{-1}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{rr}|_{2}\big)<\infty.

Integrating (
10.71
) over
I
, then repeating the same calculations (
7.17
)–(
7.20
) in the proof of Lemma
7.2
, with
(\phi,\psi)
replaced by
(\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\rho^{\gamma-1},(\log\rho)_{r})
, and using Lemma
10.1
, we can obtain the desired estimate of this lemma.
∎

The second lemma concerns the first-order energy estimate for
u
.

Lemma 10.17
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)(t)\Big|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{t}|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

Proof.

Lemma
10.17
can be derived by the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
7.3
, with
(\phi,\psi)
replaced by
(\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\rho^{\gamma-1},(\log\rho)_{r})
. We omit the details here.
∎

The third lemma concerns the second-order energy estimate for
u
.

Lemma 10.18
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{t}(t)|^{2}_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{tr},\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

Proof.

Note that, due to Lemmas
10.10
and
10.15
, we can show that
(|\phi_{r}|,|\psi|)
and
|\phi_{t}|
are equivalent to
|\rho_{r}|
and
|\rho_{t}|
, respectively:

\begin{gathered}|\phi_{r}|\sim|\psi|\sim|\rho_{r}|,\quad\,|\phi_{t}|\sim|\rho_{t}|\qquad\,\,\,\mbox{for all $(t,r)\in[0,T]\times I$},\end{gathered}

(10.73)

where
E\sim F
denotes
C(T)^{-1}E\leq F\leq C(T)E
. Hence, based on (
10.73
) and Lemma
10.1
, following the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
7.4
, with
(\phi,\psi)
replaced by
(\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\rho^{\gamma-1},(\log\rho)_{r})
, we can complete the proof.

Thus, recalling (
7.33
), we find that the major modification is to justify the process of integration by parts for

\mathcal{B}_{10}:=2\alpha r^{m}u_{t}\big(u_{tr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{t}\big)-r^{m}u_{t}\phi_{t}-2\alpha r^{m}\psi uu_{t}u_{r}.

We first prove
\mathcal{B}_{10}|_{r=0}=0
. Using the same arguments as in Step 2 of the proof of Lemma
7.4
, we need to show that

r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi_{t}|_{r=0}=A\gamma r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho^{\gamma-2}\rho_{t}|_{t=0}<\infty\qquad\text{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$}.

(10.74)

Thanks to (
10.5
)–(
10.6
), one has

(\rho,\rho^{-1})\in C(\bar{I}),\quad\,\,r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{t},\rho_{tr})\in L^{2}(I)\qquad\,\,\text{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$}.

(10.75)

Then, if
n=2
(
m=1
), it follows from (
10.75
) and Lemma
A.3
that, for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
,

r(\rho_{t},\rho_{tr})\in L^{2}(0,1)\implies r^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho_{t}\in C([0,1]),

and hence
r^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{\gamma-2}\rho_{t}|_{r=0}<\infty
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
; while if
n=3
(
m=2
), one can deduce from (
10.75
)
and Lemmas
A.1
and
A.3
that, for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
,

r\rho_{t}\in H^{1}(0,1)\implies r\rho_{t}\in C([0,1]),

that is,

r\rho^{\gamma-2}\rho_{t}|_{r=0}<\infty\qquad\mbox{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$}.

Thus, we conclude (
10.74
).

Next, we show that
\mathcal{B}_{10}\in W^{1,1}(I)
for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
.
By (
10.5
)–(
10.6
), we have

(\rho_{r},u,u_{r})\in L^{\infty}(I),\quad\,\,\,r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\rho_{t},\rho_{tr},\rho_{rr},u_{r},u_{t},u_{rr},\frac{u_{t}}{r},u_{tr},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r},u_{trr}\Big)\in L^{2}(I)

for
a.e.

t\in(0,T)
.
Thus, following a calculation similar to (
7.37
) in the proof of Lemma
7.4
,
we obtain from the above, (
10.73
), and (
10.75
) that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{10}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{t}|_{2}\Big(\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{tr},\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi_{t}|_{2}+|\psi|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{t}|_{2}\Big(\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{tr},\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{t}|_{2}+|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}\Big)<\infty,

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{B}_{10})_{r}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{tr},\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}\Big(\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{tr},\frac{u_{t}}{r},\phi_{t}\big)\Big|_{2}+|\psi|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{t}|_{2}\Big(\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{trr},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r},\phi_{tr}\Big)\Big|_{2}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\psi_{r}|_{2}|u|_{\infty}|u_{r}|_{\infty}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{t}|_{2}\big(|\psi|_{\infty}|u_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+|\psi|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{tr},\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}\Big(\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{tr},\frac{u_{t}}{r},\rho_{t}\big)\Big|_{2}+|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{t}|_{2}\Big(\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{trr},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big|_{2}+|\rho^{\gamma-3}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{t}|_{2}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}+|\rho^{\gamma-2}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{tr}|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{t}|_{2}\Big(|\rho^{-2}|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}\big|_{2}+|\rho^{-1}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{rr}|_{2}\Big)|u|_{\infty}|u_{r}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad+C(T)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{t}|_{2}\big(|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|u_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|u|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}|_{2}\big)<\infty,

which implies the assertion.
∎

With the help of
10.18
, we can also obtain the following estimates:

Lemma 10.19
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

|(u,r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r})(t)|_{\infty}+\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\rho_{r},\rho_{t},u_{rr},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)(t)\Big|_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{\infty}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

Proof.

Based on (
10.73
), Lemma
10.19
follows directly from the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
7.5
, with
(\phi,\psi)
replaced by
(\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\rho^{\gamma-1},(\log\rho)_{r})
.
We omit the details. Of course, we can also obtain from (
7.44
) that

\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\Big(\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{tr},\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}+1\Big).

(10.76)

This, together with Lemma
10.18
, leads to the desired estimates of this lemma.
∎

The following lemma provides the high-order estimates of
(\rho,u)
.

Lemma 10.20
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

|\rho_{r}(t)|_{\infty}+\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\rho_{rr},\frac{\rho_{r}}{r},\rho_{tr}\Big)(t)\Big|^{2}_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{rrr},\frac{u_{rr}}{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1.

Estimates on
\rho
.
First, it follows from (
10.9
) and Lemmas
10.10
,
10.13
, and
10.19
that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

|\rho_{r}(t)|_{\infty}\leq|\rho(t)|_{\infty}|(\log\rho)_{r}(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(T)|(v,u)(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(T).

(10.77)

Then, by (
10.69
), Lemmas
10.10
and
10.15
, and the following identities: for
\partial=\partial_{r}
or
\partial_{t}
,

\displaystyle\partial\phi_{r}=A\gamma(\gamma-2)\rho^{\gamma-3}\rho_{r}\partial\rho+A\gamma\rho^{\gamma-2}\partial\rho_{r},\quad\partial\psi=-\rho^{-2}\rho_{r}\partial\rho+\rho^{-1}\partial\rho_{r},

(10.78)

\displaystyle\partial\rho_{r}=(2-\gamma)\rho^{-1}\rho_{r}\partial\rho+(A\gamma)^{-1}\rho^{2-\gamma}\partial\phi_{r}=\psi\partial\rho+\rho\partial\psi,

we obtain from (
10.77
) that the following mutual constraints between
(|\partial\phi_{r}|,|\partial\psi|)
and
(|\partial\rho|,|\partial\rho_{r}|)
hold:
For all
(t,r)\in[0,T]\times I
,

\displaystyle|\partial\phi_{r}|+|\partial\psi|\leq C(T)(|\partial\rho|+|\partial\rho_{r}|),

(10.79)

\displaystyle|\partial\rho_{r}|\leq C(T)(|\partial\rho|+\mathcal{Z}_{1})\qquad\text{with $\mathcal{Z}_{1}=|\partial\phi_{r}|$ or $|\partial\psi|$}.

Next, repeating the same calculations (
7.45
)–(
7.52
) in the proof of Lemma
7.6
, with
\phi
replaced by
\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\rho^{\gamma-1}
, we see from (
10.73
) and Lemma
10.1
that, for
t\in[0,T]
,

\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\psi_{r},\frac{\psi}{r}\big)(t)\Big|_{2}+\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\psi_{t}(t)\big|_{2}\leq C(T).

Thus, it follows from (
10.73
) and (
10.79
), and Lemma
10.19
that

\displaystyle\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\rho_{rr},\frac{\rho_{r}}{r}\big)(t)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\rho_{r},\psi_{r},\frac{\psi}{r}\big)(t)\Big|_{2}\leq C(T),

(10.80)

\displaystyle|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{tr}(t)|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{t},\psi_{t})|_{2}\leq C(T).

2.

Estimates on
u
.

To obtain the estimates for
u
, repeating the same calculations (
7.54
)–(
7.55
)
as in the proof of Lemma
7.7
, we obtain from Lemmas
10.10
and
10.19
,
(
10.73
), and (
10.79
)–(
10.80
) that

\displaystyle\Big|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)_{r}\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}u_{t}|_{2}+C_{0}|u_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}(u,\psi)|_{2}+C_{0}|\phi|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}\psi|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}u_{t}|_{2}+|u_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}(u,\rho_{r})|_{2}+|\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1}|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}u_{t}|_{2}+|u_{r}|_{\infty}+1\big),

\displaystyle\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)_{rr}\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tr}|_{2}+|u_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+C_{0}|(u,\psi)|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|\psi|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi_{r}|_{2}+C_{0}|(\phi,u_{r})|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\psi_{r}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tr}|_{2}+|u_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}+|(u,\rho_{r})|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(T)\big(|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{r}|_{2}+|(\rho^{\gamma-1},u_{r})|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{r},\rho_{rr})|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tr}|_{2}+|u_{r}|_{\infty}+1\big).

Thus, the above estimates, together with Lemmas
7.1
and
10.18
–
10.19
,
give the desired estimates of this lemma.
∎

Finally, the following two lemmas concern time-weighted estimates of the velocity.

Lemma 10.21
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\begin{split}&t\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{tr},\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)(t)\Big|^{2}_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}s|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|^{2}_{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).\end{split}

Proof.

First, following the same calculations (
7.56
)–(
7.59
) in the proof of Lemma
7.8
, we see from (
10.73
), (
10.76
) and (
10.79
), Lemmas
7.1
and
10.18
–
10.20
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\alpha\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{tr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{t}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\int r^{m}\big((uu_{r})_{t}+\phi_{tr}-2\alpha(\psi u_{r})_{t}\big)u_{tt}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{t},\psi_{t})|_{2}|u_{r}|_{\infty}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tr}|_{2}|(u,\psi)|_{\infty}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi_{tr}|_{2}\big)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{t},\rho_{t},\rho_{tr})|_{2}|u_{r}|_{\infty}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tr}|_{2}|(u,\rho_{r})|_{\infty}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{t},\rho_{tr})|_{2}\big)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{tr},\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}+1\Big)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{tr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{t}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}+1\Big)+\frac{1}{8}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|_{2}^{2}.

Finally, based on the above inequality, repeating the same calculations (
7.60
)–(
7.62
)
as in the proof of Lemma
7.8
, we obtain the desired estimate.
∎

Lemma 10.22
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

t\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{rrr},\frac{u_{rr}}{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)(t)\Big|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}s\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{trr},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

Proof.

First, following the same calculations (
7.63
)–(
7.64
) as in the proof
of Lemma
7.9
, we see that

\sqrt{t}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{\infty}+\sqrt{t}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{rrr},\frac{u_{rr}}{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}\leq C(T).

(10.81)

Next, repeating the same calculation (
7.65
) in the proof of Lemma
7.9
, together with (
10.73
), (
10.79
),
(
10.81
), and Lemmas
10.18
–
10.21
, yields

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{trr},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sqrt{t}\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|_{2}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{t},\psi_{t})|_{2}|u_{r}|_{\infty}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tr}|_{2}|(u,\psi)|_{\infty}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi_{tr}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\sqrt{t}\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|_{2}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{t},\rho_{t},\rho_{tr})|_{2}|u_{r}|_{\infty}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tr}|_{2}|(u,\rho_{r})|_{\infty}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{t},\rho_{tr})|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(\sqrt{t}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|_{2}+1\big).

Taking the square of the above and integrating the resulting inequality over
[0,t]
,
together with Lemma
10.21
, leads to the desired estimates.
∎

10.9.
Global uniform
a priori
estimates for 3-order regular solutions

In §
10.9
, we always assume that
\gamma\in(1,\infty)
if
n=2
and
\gamma\in(1,3)
if
n=3
. Let
T>0
be any fixed time, and let
(\rho,u)(t,r)
be the
3
-order regular solution of
problem (
10.1
) in
[0,T]\times I
obtained in Theorems
10.4
.
The
H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimates are the same as those presented
in Lemmas
10.16
–
10.20
, and thus we focus only on
the
D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimates and the time-weighted estimates.

We first give the
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate of
(u_{r},\frac{u}{r})
and the third-order estimates of
u
.

Lemma 10.23
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{tr},\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)(t)\Big|_{2}+\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{rrr},\frac{u_{rr}}{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)(t)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle+\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)(t)\Big|_{\infty}+\int_{0}^{t}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|^{2}_{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{trr},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

Proof.

This can be proved by the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
8.1
,
with
(\phi,\psi)
replaced by
(\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\rho^{\gamma-1},(\log\rho)_{r})
.
To achieve this, it suffices to show that (
8.3
) holds.

Indeed, taking the limit as
\tau\to 0
in (
8.2
),
we obtain from Lemmas
7.1
,
10.1
,
A.1
, and
B.1
,
and the time-continuity of
(\rho,u)
presented in Lemma
10.2

that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to 0}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{tr},\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)(\tau)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|(\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\nabla\log\rho_{0},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\nabla^{2}\log\rho_{0})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}\|(\nabla^{2}\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1},\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}_{0})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{0}.

The proof of Lemma
10.23
is completed.
∎

Next, we derive the higher-order estimates for
(\rho,u)
.

Lemma 10.24
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\!\Big(\rho_{rrr},\!\big(\frac{\rho_{r}}{r}\big)_{r},\rho_{trr},\!\frac{\rho_{tr}}{r}\Big)(t)\Big|_{2}\!+\!\int_{0}^{t}\!\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\!\Big(u_{rrrr},\!\big(\frac{u_{rr}}{r}\big)_{r},\!\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rrr},\!\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1.

Estimates on
\rho
.

First, it follows from
\eqref{re-r}_{1}
, (
10.77
), and Lemmas
10.19
and
10.23
that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

|\rho_{t}|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}\Big(|u|_{\infty}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}+|\rho|_{\infty}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\Big)\leq C(T).

(10.82)

Then, based on the above, (
10.73
) and (
10.77
), following an argument
similar to (
10.78
)–(
10.79
) in Step 1 of the proof of Lemma
10.20
,
we can obtain some mutual constraints for the high-order derivatives of
(\rho,\phi,\psi)
:
For all
(t,r)\in[0,T]\times I
,

\displaystyle\begin{cases}|\phi_{rrr}|+|\psi_{rr}|\leq C(T)(|\rho_{r}|+|\rho_{rr}|+|\rho_{rrr}|),\\[6.0pt]
|\rho_{rrr}|\leq C(T)(|\rho_{r}|+|\rho_{rr}|+\mathcal{Z}_{2})\qquad\text{with $\mathcal{Z}_{2}=|\phi_{rrr}|$ or $|\psi_{rr}|$},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\begin{cases}|\phi_{trr}|+|\psi_{tr}|\leq C(T)(|\rho_{t}|+|\rho_{tr}|+|\rho_{rr}|+|\rho_{trr}|),\\[6.0pt]
|\rho_{tr}|\leq C(T)(|\rho_{r}|+\mathcal{Z}_{3})\qquad\text{with $\mathcal{Z}_{3}=|\phi_{tr}|$ or $|\psi_{t}|$},\\[6.0pt]
|\rho_{trr}|\leq C(T)(|\rho_{t}|+|\rho_{tr}|+|\rho_{rr}|+\mathcal{Z}_{4})\qquad\text{with $\mathcal{Z}_{4}=|\phi_{trr}|$ or $|\psi_{tr}|$},\end{cases}

(10.83)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\Big|\big(\frac{\phi_{r}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big|+\Big|\big(\frac{\psi}{r}\big)_{r}\Big|\leq C(T)\Big(\Big|\frac{\rho_{r}}{r}\Big|+\Big|\big(\frac{\rho_{r}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big|\Big),\\[6.0pt]
\displaystyle\Big|\big(\frac{\rho_{r}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big|\leq C(T)\Big(\Big|\frac{\rho_{r}}{r}\Big|+\mathcal{Z}_{5}\Big)\qquad\text{with $\mathcal{Z}_{5}=\Big|\big(\frac{\phi_{r}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big|$ or $\Big|\big(\frac{\psi}{r}\big)_{r}\Big|$}.\end{cases}

As a consequence, following the calculations similar to (
8.5
)–(
8.13
) in the proof of Lemma
8.3
, together with (
10.83
), and Lemmas
10.1
and
10.19
–
10.20
, one arrives at

\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\rho_{rrr},\big(\frac{\rho_{r}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}\leq C(T)\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\rho_{r},\rho_{rr},\frac{\rho_{r}}{r},\psi_{rr},\big(\frac{\psi}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}\leq C(T).

(10.84)

Next, repeating the same calculation (
8.14
) in the proof of Lemma
8.3
, we see from (
10.73
), (
10.79
), (
10.83
)–(
10.84
), and Lemmas
10.19
–
10.20
and
10.23
that

\displaystyle\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\psi_{tr},\frac{\psi_{t}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|(u,\psi)|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{rr},\psi_{rr})|_{2}+C_{0}\Big|\big(u_{r},\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big|_{\infty}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\psi_{r},\frac{\psi}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{rrr},\frac{u_{rr}}{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)|(u,\rho_{r})|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{rr},\rho_{r},\rho_{rr},\rho_{rrr})|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C(T)\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\rho_{r},\rho_{rr},\frac{\rho_{r}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}+C(T)\leq C(T),

which, along with (
10.83
) and Lemmas
10.19
–
10.20
, leads to

\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\rho_{trr},\frac{\rho_{tr}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}\leq\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\rho_{t},\rho_{tr},\rho_{rr},\frac{\rho_{r}}{r},\psi_{tr},\frac{\psi_{t}}{r}\big)\Big|_{2}\leq C(T).

(10.85)

2.

Estimates on
u
.
Repeating the calculations (
8.20
)
and (
8.22
) in the proof of Lemma
8.3
,
we obtain from (
10.73
), (
10.79
), (
10.83
)–(
10.84
),
and Lemmas
10.19
–
10.20
and
10.23
that

\displaystyle\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)_{r}\Big)_{r}\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r},\big(\frac{\phi_{r}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}+C_{0}|(u,\psi)|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}u_{rr}|_{2}

(10.86)

\displaystyle+C_{0}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r},\big(\frac{\psi}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)_{r}\Big|_{2}|u_{r}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big|_{2}+C(T)\Big(|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}+\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\frac{\rho_{r}}{r},\big(\frac{\rho_{r}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}+1\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big|_{2}+C(T),

\displaystyle\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)_{rrr}\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{trr},\phi_{rrr})\big|_{2}+|(u,\psi)|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rrr}|_{2}+C_{0}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}\psi_{r}|_{2}

\displaystyle+C_{0}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{rr},\psi_{rr})|_{2}|u_{r}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{trr}|_{2}+C(T)|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}+C(T)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}|_{2}|\rho_{r}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+C(T)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}\rho_{rr}|_{2}+C(T)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{r},\rho_{rr},\rho_{rrr})|_{2}+C(T)

\displaystyle\leq|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{trr}|_{2}+C(T)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}\rho_{rr}|_{2}+C(T).

Then, for the estimates of
|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}\rho_{rr}|_{2}
on the right-hand side of
\eqref{lem612-1-po}_{2}
, it follows from (
10.84
), Lemmas
10.10
,
10.19
–
10.20
,
10.23
,
A.1
, and
A.3
that

\displaystyle|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}\rho_{rr}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}\rho_{rr}|_{2}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}\rho_{rr}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{m+2}{2}}u_{rr}\rho_{rr}|_{2}+C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{m+2}{2}}u_{rrr}\rho_{rr}|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{m+2}{2}}u_{rr}\rho_{rrr}|_{2}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}\rho_{rr}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{rr},u_{rrr})|_{2}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r\rho_{rr}|_{\infty}

(10.87)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{rrr}|_{2}|\chi_{1}^{\flat}ru_{rr}|_{\infty}+C_{0}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{rr}|_{2}|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}\rho_{rr}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{3}{2}}(\rho_{rr},\rho_{rrr},u_{rr},u_{rrr})|_{2}+C(T)|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}(\rho_{rr},\rho_{rrr})|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{3-m}{2}}|_{\infty}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}|_{\infty}\big)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{rr},\rho_{rrr},u_{rr},u_{rrr})|_{2}\leq C(T).

Combining with (
10.86
)–(
10.87
), together with Lemmas
7.1
and
10.23
, gives the desired estimates of this lemma.
∎

Remark 10.1
.

As shown by (
10.73
), (
10.79
), (
10.83
),
and
Lemmas
10.19
–
10.20
and
10.24
,
each of the following
L^{2}(I)
-norms that we use:

|r^{\frac{m}{2}}X|_{2},\ |r^{\frac{m}{2}}X_{r}|_{2},\ \big|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}X\big|_{2},\ |r^{\frac{m}{2}}X_{rr}|_{2},\ \Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(\frac{X}{r}\big)_{r}\Big|_{2},\ |r^{\frac{m}{2}}X_{t}|_{2},\ |r^{\frac{m}{2}}X_{tr}|_{2},\ \big|r^{\frac{m-2}{2}}X_{t}\big|_{2}

with
X=\phi_{r}
or
\psi
, can be controlled by the corresponding
L^{2}(I)
-norms
with
X
replaced by
\rho_{r}
, combined with some
L^{2}(I)
-norms of the
lower-order derivatives of
\rho
, for instance,

|r^{\frac{m}{2}}X_{tr}|_{2}\leq C(T)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho_{trr}|_{2}+C(T)\underbrace{\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{t},\rho_{tr},\rho_{rr})\big|_{2}}_{\text{lower-order norms}}\leq C(T).

Now, we establish the time-weighted estimates for
u
. The following two lemmas are similar to Lemmas
8.4
–
8.5
.

Lemma 10.25
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\begin{split}&t|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}(t)|^{2}_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}s\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\big(u_{ttr},\frac{u_{tt}}{r}\big)\Big|^{2}_{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).\end{split}

Proof.

First, it follows from the same calculation (
8.27
) in the proof of Lemma
8.4
that

|u_{t}|_{\infty}+|\chi_{1}^{\flat}ru_{tr}|_{\infty}+|\chi_{1}^{\sharp}u_{tr}|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{trr}|_{2}+1\big).

(10.88)

Next, based on Remark
10.1
, repeating the same calculations (
8.28
)–(
8.30
) in the proof of Lemma
8.4
, one obtains that

\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\phi_{ttr},\psi_{tt})\big|_{2}\leq C(T)\Big(\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{trr},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}+1\Big).

(10.89)

As a consequence, with the help of (
10.88
)–(
10.89
), the expected estimate follows directly from the same arguments as in Steps 2–3 of the proof of Lemma
8.4
.
∎

Lemma 10.26
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{trr},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{r},u_{rrrr},\big(\frac{u_{rr}}{r}\big)_{r},\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{rrr},\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)_{r}\Big)(t)\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}s\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big(u_{trrr},\frac{u_{trr}}{r},\big(\frac{u_{t}}{r}\big)_{rr},\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{u_{tr}}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

Proof.

Using Remark
10.1
and the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
8.5
, we can obtain the desired estimates of this lemma. We omit the details.
∎

10.10.
Global well-posedness of the M-D regular solutions with strictly positive initial density

This part can be proved via the same argument as in §
9
.

11.
Local Well-Posedness of Regular Solutions with Far-Field Vacuum

This section is devoted to establishing the local existence of the unique regular solution of the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) in the M-D space variables when
\bar{\rho}=0
. Moreover, we show that,
if the initial data are spherically symmetric, so is the corresponding M-D regular solution of this problem in late time.
In the rest of §
11
,
C\in[1,\infty)
denotes a generic constant depending only on
(n,\alpha,A,\gamma)
and may be different at each occurrence.

11.1.
Local well-posedness of the 2-order regular solutions
with far-field vacuum

We prove Theorem
3.1
in §
11.1.1
–§
11.1.3
.
Moreover, at the end of §
11.1
, we show that
this theorem indeed implies Theorem
3.3
.

11.1.1.
Linearization

We start with the proof of Theorem
3.1
by considering the following linearized problem of
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
:

\begin{cases}\phi_{t}+\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\phi+(\gamma-1)\phi\diver\boldsymbol{w}=0,\\[5.0pt]
\boldsymbol{u}_{t}+\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{w}+\nabla\phi+L\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{w}),\\
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}+\sum\limits_{l=1}^{n}A_{l}(\boldsymbol{w})\partial_{l}\boldsymbol{\psi}+B(\boldsymbol{w})\boldsymbol{\psi}+\nabla\diver\boldsymbol{w}=\boldsymbol{0},\\
\displaystyle(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})|_{t=0}=\Big(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}=\frac{1}{\gamma-1}\nabla\log\phi_{0}\Big)\ \ \text{for}\ \ \boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\\[6.0pt]
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u})\to(0,\boldsymbol{0})\ \ \text{as}\ \ |\boldsymbol{x}|\to\infty\ \ \text{for}\ \ t\in[0,T],\end{cases}

(11.1)

where the operators
(L,Q)
are defined in (
2.4
),
A_{l}(\boldsymbol{w})
(
l=1,\cdots\!,n
) and
B(\boldsymbol{w})
are defined in §
3.2
.
Moreover, we assume that the initial data
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0})
are spherically symmetric, taking form (
3.4
), and satisfy

\begin{gathered}\phi_{0}>0,\quad\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\quad\nabla\phi_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\quad\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\in D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\quad\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\end{gathered}

(11.2)

and
\boldsymbol{w}=(w_{1},\cdots\!,w_{n})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a given spherically symmetric vector function satisfying
\boldsymbol{w}(0,\boldsymbol{x})=\boldsymbol{u}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})
and

\boldsymbol{w}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=w(t,|\boldsymbol{x}|)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{|\boldsymbol{x}|},

(11.3)

and, for any
T>0
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{l}\boldsymbol{w}\in C([0,T];H^{2-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{3-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad l=0,1,

(11.4)

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\partial_{t}^{l}\boldsymbol{w}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{3-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad\sqrt{t}\partial_{t}^{l+1}\boldsymbol{w}\in L^{2}([0,T];D^{2-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad l=0,1.

Then we have the following global well-posedness for the linearized problem (
11.1
), which can be obtained by classical arguments shown in
[
17
,
24
]
.

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
n=2
or
3
, and (
3.7
) hold. Let the initial data
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0})(\boldsymbol{x})
be spherically symmetric and satisfy (
11.2
).
Then, for any
T>0
, there exists a unique solution
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
of the linearized problem (
11.1
) in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that (
3.10
) holds, and

\displaystyle\mathrm{(i)}

\displaystyle\ (\phi>0,\boldsymbol{u})\ \text{satisfies this problem in the sense of distributions};

\displaystyle\mathrm{(ii)}

\displaystyle\ \phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\in C([0,T];L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\ \ \nabla\phi\in C([0,T];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\ \ \phi_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle\ \boldsymbol{\psi}\in C([0,T];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\in C([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}));

\displaystyle\mathrm{(iii)}

\displaystyle\ \boldsymbol{u}(t,\boldsymbol{x})|_{|\boldsymbol{x}|=0}=\boldsymbol{0}\ \ \text{for}\ \ t\in[0,T],

\displaystyle\ \partial_{t}^{l}\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];H^{2-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{3-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad l=0,1;

\displaystyle\mathrm{(iv)}

\displaystyle\ \sqrt{t}\partial_{t}^{l}\boldsymbol{u}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{3-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\ \ \sqrt{t}\partial_{t}^{l+1}\boldsymbol{u}\in L^{2}([0,T];D^{2-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\ \ l=0,1.

Moreover,
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
is spherically symmetric with form (
3.3
).

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1.
We can first solve for
\phi
from
\eqref{li4}_{1}
via the standard theory of transport equations. Next, the well-posedness of
\boldsymbol{\psi}
follows from the standard theory for the symmetric hyperbolic systems. Finally, rewrite
\eqref{li4}_{2}
as

{u}_{t}+L\boldsymbol{u}=-\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{w}-\nabla\phi+\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{w}).

Then we can solve for
u
from the above via the regularity properties of
(\phi,\boldsymbol{\psi})
and the standard theory of linear parabolic equations.

2.
Next, we give a brief proof for (
3.10
). Let
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\psi}}:=\frac{1}{\gamma-1}\nabla\log\phi
. Then multiplying
\eqref{li4}_{1}
by
\frac{1}{(\gamma-1)\phi}
and applying
\nabla
to the resulting equality yield

\tilde{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{t}+\sum_{l=1}^{n}A_{l}(\boldsymbol{w})\partial_{l}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\psi}}+B(\boldsymbol{w})\tilde{\boldsymbol{\psi}}+\nabla\diver\boldsymbol{w}=0.

(11.5)

This implies that
\boldsymbol{\psi}
and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\psi}}
satisfy the same equation and take the same initial data.
Therefore, the uniqueness of the solution yields (
3.10
).

3.
Finally, we show that the solution
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
is spherically symmetric and takes form (
3.3
).
Indeed, since
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0})
is spherically symmetric, for any
\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
,

\begin{gathered}\phi_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})=\phi_{0}(\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}),\ \ \boldsymbol{u}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})=\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{0}(\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}),\ \ \boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})=\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}(\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}).\end{gathered}

(11.6)

Thus, if denoting

\begin{gathered}\hat{\phi}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\phi(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}),\quad\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}),\quad\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\psi}(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}),\end{gathered}

one obtains from
|\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}|=|\boldsymbol{x}|
, (
3.5
), and (
11.6
) that

\displaystyle(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})|_{t=0}=(\hat{\phi}_{0},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{0},\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{0})=(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}),

\displaystyle\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}(t,\boldsymbol{x})|_{|\boldsymbol{x}|=0}=\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})|_{|\boldsymbol{x}|=0}=\boldsymbol{0}\qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T]$},

\displaystyle(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}})\to(0,\boldsymbol{0})\qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T]$}.

Next, we show that
(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})
is also a solution of (
11.1
). For any
\mathcal{O}=(\mathcal{O}_{lk})_{n\times n}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
, let
\boldsymbol{x}=(x_{1},\cdots\!,x_{n})^{\top}
and
\boldsymbol{y}=(y_{1},\cdots\!,y_{n})^{\top}
satisfy

\boldsymbol{y}=\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}\qquad\text{{\it i.e.}, $\,\,y_{l}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{O}_{lk}x_{k}$}.

(11.7)

Clearly,
\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{O}_{ki}\mathcal{O}_{kj}=\delta_{ij}
and
\boldsymbol{w}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{w}(t,\boldsymbol{y})
. For convenience, in Steps 3.1–3.3 below,
We adopt the Einstein summation convention: any index that appears exactly twice in a term is summed over.

3.1.
We first show that
\hat{\phi}
satisfies
\eqref{li4}_{1}

by the following direct calculations:

\displaystyle\hat{\phi}_{t}(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=\phi_{t}(t,\boldsymbol{y}),

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\hat{\phi})(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{ki}\mathcal{O}_{li}w_{l}\partial_{y_{k}}\phi)(t,\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=(\delta_{kl}w_{l}\partial_{y_{k}}\phi)(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla_{y}\phi)(t,\boldsymbol{y}),

\displaystyle(\hat{\phi}\diver\boldsymbol{w})(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\phi\mathcal{O}_{kj}\mathcal{O}_{lj}\partial_{y_{k}}w_{l})(t,\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=(\phi\delta_{kl}\partial_{y_{k}}w_{l})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\phi\diver_{y}\boldsymbol{w})(t,\boldsymbol{y}).

These identities imply that
\hat{\phi}(t,\boldsymbol{x})
satisfies
\eqref{li4}_{1}
indeed.

3.2.
Next, we show that
(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})
satisfies
\eqref{li4}_{2}
.
Notice that

\displaystyle\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{t}(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{qi}\partial_{t}u_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{t})(t,\boldsymbol{y}),

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{w})(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{lj}\mathcal{O}_{kj}\mathcal{O}_{qi}w_{l}\partial_{y_{k}}w_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\delta_{lk}\mathcal{O}_{qi}w_{l}\partial_{y_{k}}w_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{qi}w_{k}\partial_{y_{k}}w_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla_{y}\boldsymbol{w}))(t,\boldsymbol{y}),

\displaystyle\nabla\hat{\phi}(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{ki}\partial_{y_{k}}\phi)(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\nabla_{y}\phi)(t,\boldsymbol{y}),

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{lj}\mathcal{O}_{kj}\mathcal{O}_{qi}\partial_{y_{l}y_{k}}u_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=(\delta_{lk}\mathcal{O}_{qi}\partial_{y_{l}y_{k}}u_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\Delta_{y}\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{y}),

\displaystyle\nabla\diver\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{li}\mathcal{O}_{kj}\mathcal{O}_{qj}\partial_{y_{l}y_{k}}u_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{li}\delta_{kq}\partial_{y_{l}y_{k}}u_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\nabla_{y}\diver_{y}\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{y}),

\displaystyle(\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\cdot D(\boldsymbol{w}))(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{kj}\psi_{k}(\mathcal{O}_{lj}\mathcal{O}_{pi}\partial_{y_{l}}w_{p}+\mathcal{O}_{li}\mathcal{O}_{pj}\partial_{y_{l}}w_{p}))(t,\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=(\psi_{k}(\delta_{kl}\mathcal{O}_{pi}\partial_{y_{l}}w_{p}+\delta_{kp}\mathcal{O}_{li}\partial_{y_{l}}w_{p}))(t,\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{li}\psi_{k}(\partial_{y_{k}}w_{l}+\partial_{y_{l}}w_{k}))(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot D_{y}(\boldsymbol{w})))(t,\boldsymbol{y}).

The above identities imply that
(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})
satisfies
\eqref{li4}_{2}
.

3.3.
Finally, we show that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}
satisfies
\eqref{li4}_{3}
.
Since

\displaystyle\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{t}(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{qi}\partial_{t}\psi_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t})(t,\boldsymbol{y}),

\displaystyle(A_{j}(\boldsymbol{w})\partial_{j}\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{lj}\mathcal{O}_{kj}\mathcal{O}_{qi}w_{l}\partial_{y_{k}}\psi_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\delta_{lk}\mathcal{O}_{qi}w_{l}\partial_{y_{k}}\psi_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{qi}w_{k}\partial_{y_{k}}\psi_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}(A_{k}(\boldsymbol{w})\partial_{y_{k}}\boldsymbol{\psi}))(t,\boldsymbol{y}),

\displaystyle(B(\boldsymbol{w})\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{ki}\mathcal{O}_{lj}\mathcal{O}_{qj}\partial_{y_{k}}w_{l}\psi_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}_{ki}\delta_{lq}\partial_{y_{k}}w_{l}\psi_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{ki}\partial_{y_{k}}w_{q}\psi_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}(B_{y}(\boldsymbol{w})\boldsymbol{\psi}))(t,\boldsymbol{y}),

\displaystyle\nabla\diver\boldsymbol{w}(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{li}\mathcal{O}_{kj}\mathcal{O}_{qj}\partial_{y_{l}y_{k}}w_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{li}\delta_{kq}\partial_{y_{l}y_{k}}w_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\nabla_{y}\diver_{y}\boldsymbol{w})(t,\boldsymbol{y}),

with
B_{y}(\boldsymbol{w})=(\nabla_{y}\boldsymbol{w})^{\top}
, we see that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}
satisfies
\eqref{li4}_{3}
.

3.4.
In Steps 3.1–3.3, we have shown that
(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})
is also a solution of
the linearized problem (
11.1
), which takes the initial value
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0})
. Consequently, the uniqueness of the solution implies that

\phi(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})=\phi(t,\boldsymbol{x}),\qquad(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u})(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})=\boldsymbol{u}(t,\boldsymbol{x}),\qquad(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\psi})(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})=\boldsymbol{\psi}(t,\boldsymbol{x}).

(11.8)

Clearly, it follows from
\eqref{cls}_{1}
that
\phi(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\phi(t,|\boldsymbol{x}|)
with some function
\phi(t,r)
defined on
[0,T]\times I
.
It remains to show that

(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})(t,\boldsymbol{x})=(u(t,|\boldsymbol{x}|)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{|\boldsymbol{x}|},\psi(t,|\boldsymbol{x}|)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{|\boldsymbol{x}|})

with some function
(u,\psi)(t,r)
defined on
[0,T]\times I
.
Here, we take the 3-D case and
\boldsymbol{u}(t,\boldsymbol{x})
as an example, since
the proof for the 2-D case and
\boldsymbol{\psi}(t,\boldsymbol{x})
can be derived similarly.
Let
t_{0}\in[0,T]
be any fixed time and
\boldsymbol{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
be any fixed displacement vector. Let
\boldsymbol{e}_{1}=\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{0}}{|\boldsymbol{x}_{0}|}
and
\mathcal{O}_{1}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
be a rotation by 180 degrees about an axis parallel to
\boldsymbol{x}_{0}
. Then
\eqref{cls}_{2}
yields

\mathcal{O}_{1}\boldsymbol{x}_{0}=\boldsymbol{x}_{0},\qquad(\mathcal{O}_{1}\boldsymbol{u})(t_{0},\boldsymbol{x}_{0})=\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0},\boldsymbol{x}_{0}).

(11.9)

Next, let
\{\boldsymbol{e}_{2},\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\}
be two unit vectors such that
\{\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2},\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\}
becomes an orthonormal basis in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
Then there exist some constants
a_{i}=a_{i}(t_{0},\boldsymbol{x}_{0})\in\mathbb{R}

(i=1,2,3)
depending only on
(t_{0},\boldsymbol{x}_{0})
such that

\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0},\boldsymbol{x}_{0})=a_{1}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}+a_{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{2}+a_{3}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}.

This, together with (
11.9
), gives

\displaystyle a_{1}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}+a_{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{2}+a_{3}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}=a_{1}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}-a_{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{2}-a_{3}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}

\displaystyle\implies a_{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{2}+a_{3}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}=\boldsymbol{0}

\displaystyle\implies a_{2}=a_{3}=0\ \ \text{due to the linear independence of }\{\boldsymbol{e}_{2},\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\},

and hence, for any fixed
(t_{0},\boldsymbol{x}_{0})\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\boldsymbol{u}(t_{0},\boldsymbol{x}_{0})=a_{1}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}=a_{1}(t_{0},\boldsymbol{x}_{0})\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{0}}{|\boldsymbol{x}_{0}|}.

Based on
\eqref{cls}_{2}
, we see that
a_{1}(t_{0},\boldsymbol{x}_{0})=a_{1}(t_{0},|\boldsymbol{x}_{0}|)
, which yields
\boldsymbol{u}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=u(t,|\boldsymbol{x}|)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{|\boldsymbol{x}|}
with some function
u(t,r)
defined on
[0,T]\times I
. Finally,
\boldsymbol{u}(t,\boldsymbol{x})|_{|\boldsymbol{x}|=0}=\boldsymbol{0}
follows from Lemma
A.7
in Appendix
A
.

The proof of Lemma
11.1
is completed.
∎

11.1.2.
The uniform
a priori
estimates

Let
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
be a solution in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
obtained in Lemma
11.1
. We now establish the corresponding
a priori
estimates.

We first choose a positive constant
c_{0}
such that

2+\big\|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\big\|_{L^{1}}+\|\nabla\phi_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{H^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{D^{1}}\leq c_{0}.

(11.10)

Assume there exist
T^{*}\in(0,T)
and constants
(c_{1},c_{2},c_{3})

such that
1<c_{0}\leq c_{1}\leq c_{2}\leq c_{3}
and

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\|\boldsymbol{w}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\int_{0}^{T^{*}}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c^{2}_{1},

(11.11)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{w}_{t})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T^{*}}\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w},\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c^{2}_{2},

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}t\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w},\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T^{*}}t\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t},\boldsymbol{w}_{tt})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c^{2}_{3}.

Here,
T^{*}
and
c_{i}
(
i=1,2,3
) will be determined later, which depend only on
c_{0}

and the fixed constants
(\alpha,\gamma,A,T)
.

We first give the estimates for
(\phi,\boldsymbol{\psi})
.

Lemma 11.2
.

For any
t\in[0,T_{1}]
with
T_{1}:=\min\{T^{*},(1+Cc_{3})^{-2}\}
,

\displaystyle\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}(t)\big\|_{L^{1}}\leq c_{0},\quad\|\phi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\phi(t)\|_{H^{1}}\leq Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-3},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k-1}\phi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-3}c_{k}\quad\text{for $k=1,2$},

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\psi}(t)\|_{D^{1}}\leq Cc_{0},\quad\,\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}c_{2}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into five steps.

1.
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate on
\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}
.

First, we multiply
\eqref{li4}_{1}
by
\phi^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma-1}}
to obtain

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}+\diver\big(\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\boldsymbol{w}\big)=0.

Then integrating the above over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, along with (
11.10
), yields

\displaystyle\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}(t)\big\|_{L^{1}}=\big\|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\big\|_{L^{1}}\leq c_{0}\qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T^{*}]$}.

(11.12)

2.
D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimates on
\boldsymbol{\psi}
.
Applying
\partial^{\varsigma}\boldsymbol{\psi}\partial^{\varsigma}
with the multi-index
|\varsigma|=1
to
\eqref{li4}_{3}
, then integrating the resulting equality over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we obtain
from integration by parts, (
11.11
), Lemmas
A.1
,
C.2
, and
C.4
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that
