\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{\varsigma}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}}+\delta_{2n}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\delta_{3n}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{1}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}}+\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}}\leq Cc_{2}^{2}\big(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}+1\big),

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, yields that, for
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

\|\boldsymbol{\psi}(t)\|_{D^{1}}^{2}\leq e^{Cc_{2}^{2}t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}+Cc_{2}^{2}t\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{2}.

(11.13)

3.
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate on
\phi
.

First, it follows from (
3.10
), (
11.12
)–(
11.13
), Lemma
A.2
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\big\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{4-n}{n+4}}\big\|\nabla^{2}(\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}})\big\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2n}{n+4}}

(11.14)

\displaystyle\leq C\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\big\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{4-n}{n+4}}\big(\underline{\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}|\boldsymbol{\psi}|^{2}\big\|_{L^{2}}}_{:=I_{1}}+\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\big\|_{L^{2}}\big)^{\frac{2n}{n+4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\big\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{4-n}{n+4}}\big(I_{1}+\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}\big)^{\frac{2n}{n+4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\big\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{4-n}{n+4}}I_{1}^{\frac{2n}{n+4}}+C\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\big\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{4-n}{n+4}}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{2n}{(\gamma-1)(n+4)}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2n}{n+4}}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{0}^{\frac{4-n}{n+4}}I_{1}^{\frac{2n}{n+4}}+Cc_{0}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{2n}{(\gamma-1)(n+4)}}.

For the bound of
I_{1}
, if
n=2
, it follows from (
11.12
)–(
11.13
) and Lemma
C.4
that

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\big\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\leq\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\big\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2(\gamma-1)}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}\leq Cc_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2(\gamma-1)}};

(11.15)

while, if
n=3
, it follows from (
11.13
), Lemma
C.2
,
and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\big\|_{L^{6}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\leq\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\big\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1}{6}}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{5}{6(\gamma-1)}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}\leq Cc_{0}^{\frac{13}{6}}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{5}{6(\gamma-1)}}.

(11.16)

Thus, collecting (
11.14
)–(
11.16
), together with the Young inequality, gives

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{2n}Cc_{0}^{2}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{3(\gamma-1)}}+\delta_{3n}Cc_{0}^{2}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{5}{7(\gamma-1)}}+Cc_{0}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{2n}{(\gamma-1)(n+4)}}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{0}^{3}+Cc_{0}^{7}+Cc_{0}^{\frac{n+4}{4-n}}+\frac{1}{8}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\leq Cc_{0}^{7}+\frac{1}{8}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}},

which implies that, for all
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

\|\phi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-7}.

(11.17)

4.
D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
- and
D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimates on
\phi
.

Applying
\partial^{\varsigma}\phi\partial^{\varsigma}
with the multi-index
|\varsigma|=1
to
\eqref{li4}_{1}
and then integrating the resulting equality over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we see
from integration by parts, Lemma
A.1
, and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{\varsigma}\phi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}\big(\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c_{0}^{7\gamma-7}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}}\big),

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, the Hölder inequality,
and (
11.11
), yields that, for all
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

\begin{split}\|\nabla\phi(t)\|_{L^{2}}&\leq\exp\Big(C\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\Big(\|\nabla\phi_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-7}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\\
&\leq e^{Cc_{2}\sqrt{t}}\big(c_{0}^{2}+Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-7}c_{2}\sqrt{t}\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-5}.\end{split}

(11.18)

For the
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate of
\nabla^{2}\phi
, it follows from (
3.10
), (
11.13
), (
11.17
), the Hölder inequality, Lemmas
A.2
,
C.2
, and
C.4
that

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\phi\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\delta_{2n}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\delta_{3n}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}}\big)

(11.19)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\delta_{2n}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}}+\delta_{3n}\|\nabla\phi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\phi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}+\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(c_{0}^{7\gamma-4}+\delta_{3n}c_{0}^{\frac{7\gamma-3}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\phi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\big),

which, along with the Young inequality, yields that

\|\nabla^{2}\phi(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-3}\qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T_{1}]$}.

(11.20)

5. Estimates on
(\phi_{t},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t})
.

First, it follows from
\eqref{li4}_{3}
, (
11.11
), (
11.13
), and Lemmas
A.1
,
C.2
, and
C.4

that, for all
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\delta_{2n}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\delta_{3n}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}}+\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\leq C(\|\psi\|_{D^{1}}+1)\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}c_{2}.

Next, it follows from
\eqref{li4}_{1}
, (
11.11
), (
11.17
)–(
11.20
), Lemma
A.1
, and the Hölder inequality that, for all
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\|\phi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{4}}+\|\phi\|_{\infty}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{1}}\big(\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{1}}+\|\phi\|_{\infty}\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-3}c_{1},

\displaystyle\|\nabla\phi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{4}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\phi\|_{L^{2}}+\|\phi\|_{\infty}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}\big(\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{1}}+\|\phi\|_{\infty}\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2}.

This completes the proof of Lemma
11.7
.
∎

Next, to derive all the energy estimates for
\boldsymbol{u}
systematically, we rewrite
\eqref{li4}_{2}
as

\boldsymbol{u}_{t}+L\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{w})-\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{w}-\nabla\phi:=Z(\boldsymbol{w}).

(11.21)

Then we make the following useful estimates for
Z(\boldsymbol{w})
.

Lemma 11.3
.

For any
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\|Z(\boldsymbol{w})\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{1}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2}^{\frac{n}{4}},\quad\,\|\nabla Z(\boldsymbol{w})\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{2}^{7\gamma-3}\big(\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{n}{4}}+1\big),

\displaystyle\|(Z(\boldsymbol{w}))_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\Big(c_{0}c_{2}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)+c_{0}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+c_{0}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2}\Big).

Proof.

It follows from (
11.11
), (
11.21
), Lemmas
11.2
,
A.1
–
A.2
,
C.2
, and
C.4
, and the Hölder inequality that, for all
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\|Z(\boldsymbol{w})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\delta_{2n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\delta_{3n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\delta_{2n}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}+\delta_{3n}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}}

\displaystyle+C\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{4-n}{4}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{n}{4}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\delta_{2n}c_{0}c_{1}+\delta_{3n}c_{0}c_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}c_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}+c_{1}^{\frac{8-n}{4}}c_{2}^{\frac{n}{4}}+c_{0}^{7\gamma-3}\big)\leq Cc_{1}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2}^{\frac{n}{4}},

\displaystyle\|\nabla Z(\boldsymbol{w})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\delta_{2n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\delta_{3n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\|(\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi},\nabla\boldsymbol{w})\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\phi\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\delta_{2n}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}+\delta_{3n}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}}

\displaystyle+C\big(\|(\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi},\nabla\boldsymbol{w})\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{4-n}{4}}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{n}{4}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}\phi\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\delta_{2n}c_{0}c_{2}+\delta_{3n}c_{0}c_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+c_{1}^{\frac{8-n}{4}}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{n}{4}}+c_{2}^{2}+c_{0}^{7\gamma-3}\big)

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{2}^{7\gamma-3}\big(\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{n}{4}}+1\big),

\displaystyle\|(Z(\boldsymbol{w}))_{t}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|(\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t},\boldsymbol{w}_{t})\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\delta_{2n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\delta_{3n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\phi_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|(\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t},\boldsymbol{w}_{t})\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}+\delta_{2n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\delta_{3n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\phi_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(c_{0}c_{2}+c_{2})\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}+C\delta_{2n}c_{0}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\delta_{3n}c_{0}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+Cc_{2}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(c_{0}c_{2}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)+c_{0}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+c_{0}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2}\Big).

∎

Now, we derive the uniform estimates for
\boldsymbol{u}
.

Lemma 11.4
.

For any
t\in[0,T_{2}]
with
T_{2}:=\min\big\{T_{1},(1+Cc_{3})^{-56\gamma}\big\}
,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{0}^{2},

\displaystyle\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{u}_{t})(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{1}^{14\gamma-6}c_{2}^{\frac{n}{2}},

\displaystyle t\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t})(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}s\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\boldsymbol{u}_{tt})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{0}^{2}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into four steps.

1.
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate on
\boldsymbol{u}
.
Multiplying
\eqref{li4}_{2}
by
\boldsymbol{u}
and integrating the resulting equality over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
we obtain from Lemma
11.3
and the Hölder inequality that

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\alpha\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\alpha\|\diver\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}Z(\boldsymbol{w})\cdot\boldsymbol{u}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\|Z(\boldsymbol{w})\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+Cc_{1}^{14\gamma-6}c_{2}^{\frac{n}{2}},\end{split}

(11.22)

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, yields that

\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc_{0}^{2},

(11.23)

for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
with
T_{2}:=\min\big\{T_{1},(1+Cc_{3})^{-56\gamma}\big\}
.

2.
D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate on
\boldsymbol{u}
.

Multiplying
\eqref{li4}_{2}
by
\boldsymbol{u}_{t}
and integrating the resulting equality over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we obtain from Lemma
11.3
, and
the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\alpha\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\alpha\|\diver\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}Z(\boldsymbol{w})\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq Cc_{1}^{14\gamma-6}c_{2}^{\frac{n}{2}}+\frac{1}{8}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, implies that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc_{0}^{2}.

(11.24)

Next, rewrite (
11.21
) as

L\boldsymbol{u}=-\boldsymbol{u}_{t}+Z(\boldsymbol{w}).

(11.25)

Then it follows from (
11.25
),
Lemma
11.3
, and the classical regularity theory for elliptic equations in Lemma
A.13
that

\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|(Z(\boldsymbol{w}),\boldsymbol{u}_{t})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{1}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2}^{\frac{n}{4}}\big).

(11.26)

Consequently, it follows from (
11.24
) and (
11.26
) that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+Cc_{1}^{14\gamma-6}c_{2}^{\frac{n}{2}}t\leq Cc_{0}^{2}.

3.
D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate on
\boldsymbol{u}
.

Differentiating
\eqref{li4}_{2}
with respect to
t
gives

\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}+L\boldsymbol{u}_{t}=(Z(\boldsymbol{w}))_{t}.

(11.27)

Then multiplying above by
\boldsymbol{u}_{t}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
we see from the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\alpha\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\alpha\|\diver\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(Z(\boldsymbol{w}))_{t}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\|Z(\boldsymbol{w})_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}},

which, along with Lemma
11.3
, implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}c_{2}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)+Cc_{0}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2}.

Integrating the above over
(\tau,t)
with
\tau\in(0,t)
, together with (
11.11
), and the Hölder and Young inequalities, yields that, for all
t\in[\tau,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}+Cc_{0}c_{2}\int_{\tau}^{t}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

(11.28)

\displaystyle+Cc_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2}t

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}+Cc_{0}c_{2}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+Cc_{0}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{s}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}s\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}+Cc_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}+Cc_{0}c_{2}^{2}t^{\frac{1}{2}}+Cc_{0}c_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}c_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{4}}+Cc_{0}\leq\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}+Cc_{0}.

It follows from
\eqref{li4}_{2}
, the time continuity of
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
, (
11.4
), (
11.10
), Lemmas
A.1
and
C.2
, and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\lim\sup_{\tau\to 0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\phi_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\big(\delta_{2n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\delta_{3n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\phi_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\big(\delta_{2n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\delta_{3n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\big)\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{H^{1}}\leq Cc_{0}^{2}.

Based on this, we let
\tau\to 0
in (
11.28
) and apply the Grönwall inequality to
obtain

\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc_{0}^{4}\qquad\,\,\text{for all $t\in[0,T_{2}]$}.

(11.29)

It thus follows from (
11.26
) that

\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{1}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2}^{\frac{n}{4}}\big)\leq Cc_{1}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2}^{\frac{n}{4}}.

(11.30)

Finally, it follows from (
11.25
), Lemma
11.3
,
and the classical regularity theory for elliptic equations in Lemma
A.13
that

\begin{split}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C\|(\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\nabla Z(\boldsymbol{w}))\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{2}^{7\gamma-3}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{n}{4}}+c_{2}^{7\gamma-3}\big),\end{split}

(11.31)

which, along with (
11.11
), (
11.29
), and the Hölder inequality,
implies that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+Cc_{2}^{14\gamma-6}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{n}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+Cc_{2}^{14\gamma-6}t

(11.32)

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{0}^{4}+Cc_{2}^{14\gamma-6}t^{\frac{4-n}{4}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{n}{4}}\leq Cc_{0}^{4}.

4. Time-weighted estimates on
\boldsymbol{u}
.

First, multiplying (
11.27
) by
\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we see from Lemma
11.3
and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\diver\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(Z(\boldsymbol{w}))_{t}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\mathrm{d}x

(11.33)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(c_{0}c_{2}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}+c_{0}c_{2}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{0}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+c_{0}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2}\Big)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}.

Then multiplying above by
t
, together with (
11.11
) and the Young inequality, gives

\displaystyle\alpha\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(t\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+t\|\diver\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)+t\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(11.34)

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{0}^{2}c_{2}^{2}c_{3}^{2}+Cc_{0}^{2}c_{3}\sqrt{t}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+Cc_{0}^{14\gamma-6}c_{2}^{2}t+\alpha\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{3}^{6}+Cc_{3}^{3}\sqrt{t}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+Cc_{3}^{14\gamma-4}t.

Next, integrating the above over
[\tau,t]
with
\tau\in(0,t)
, along with (
11.11
), (
11.29
), and the Hölder inequality, gives that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle t\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\tau}^{t}s\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

(11.35)

\displaystyle\leq C\tau\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+Cc_{3}^{3}\int_{\tau}^{t}\sqrt{s}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+Cc_{3}^{14\gamma-4}t^{2}+Cc_{3}^{6}t

\displaystyle\leq C\tau\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+Cc_{3}^{3}\sqrt{t}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}s\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+Cc_{0}^{2}\leq C\tau\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+Cc_{0}^{2}.

Due to (
11.29
) and Lemma
A.6
, there exists a sequence
\{\tau_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
such that

\tau_{k}\to 0,\quad\,\tau_{k}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau_{k})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\to 0\qquad\,\,\text{as $k\to\infty$}.

Thus, setting
\tau=\tau_{k}\to 0
in (
11.35
) yields that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

t\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\tau}^{t}s\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc_{0}^{2}.

(11.36)

Now, rewrite (
11.27
) as

L\boldsymbol{u}_{t}=-\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}+(Z(\boldsymbol{w}))_{t}.

(11.37)

Then it follows from (
11.37
),
Lemma
11.3
,
and the classical regularity theory for elliptic equations in Lemma
A.13
that

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big\|(\boldsymbol{u}_{tt},(Z(\boldsymbol{w}))_{t})\big\|_{L^{2}}

(11.38)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+Cc_{0}c_{2}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)+Cc_{0}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2},

which, along with (
11.11
), (
11.36
), and the Hölder inequality,
yields that, for
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}s\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}s\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+Cc_{2}^{4}\int_{0}^{t}s\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

(11.39)

\displaystyle+Cc_{0}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}s\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+Cc_{0}^{14\gamma-6}c_{2}^{2}t

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{0}^{2}+Cc_{2}^{4}c_{3}^{2}t+Cc_{0}^{2}c_{3}\sqrt{t}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}s\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq Cc_{0}^{2}.

Finally, (
11.31
), together with (
11.11
) and (
11.36
), gives that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\begin{split}\sqrt{t}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C\sqrt{t}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+Cc_{2}^{7\gamma-3}t^{\frac{4-n}{8}}\big(\sqrt{t}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}\big)^{\frac{n}{4}}+Cc_{2}^{7\gamma-3}\sqrt{t}\\
&\leq Cc_{0}+Cc_{2}^{7\gamma-3}\sqrt{t}+Cc_{2}^{7\gamma-3}c_{3}^{\frac{n}{4}}t^{\frac{4-n}{8}}\leq Cc_{0}.\end{split}

(11.40)

The proof of Lemma
11.4
is completed.
∎

Finally, choose
T^{*}

and constants
c_{i}

(i=1,2,3)
as:

T^{*}=T_{2},\qquad c_{1}=C^{\frac{1}{2}}c_{0},\qquad c_{2}=c_{3}=C^{\frac{2}{4-n}}c_{1}^{\frac{28\gamma-12}{4-n}}=C^{\frac{14\gamma-4}{4-n}}c_{0}^{\frac{28\gamma-12}{4-n}}.

It follows from Lemmas
11.2
and
11.4
that, for any
t\in[0,T^{*}]
,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}^{2},

(11.41)

\displaystyle\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{u}_{t})(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle t\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t})(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}s\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\boldsymbol{u}_{tt})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq c_{3}^{2},

\displaystyle\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}(t)\|_{L^{1}}+\|\phi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(\nabla\phi,\phi_{t})(t)\|_{H^{1}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t})(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{3}^{7\gamma-2}.

11.1.3.
Proof of Theorem
3.1

We give the proof of Theorem
3.1
, which is based on the classical iteration scheme and conclusions obtained in §
11.1.2
. Denote as in §
11.1.2
that

2+\big\|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\big\|_{L^{1}}+\|\nabla\phi_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{H^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq c_{0}.

Next, let
\boldsymbol{u}^{0}
be the unique spherically symmetric solution of the problem in
[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
:

\begin{cases}\boldsymbol{u}^{0}_{t}-\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{0}=0,\\
\boldsymbol{u}^{0}|_{t=0}=\boldsymbol{u}_{0}&\qquad\text{for $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\
\boldsymbol{u}^{0}(t,\boldsymbol{x})\to\boldsymbol{0}\ \ \text{as}\ \ \left|\boldsymbol{x}\right|\to\infty&\qquad\text{for $t\geq 0$}.\end{cases}

We can obtain the global well-posedness of
\boldsymbol{u}^{0}
via the standard theory of linear parabolic equations, and then prove that
\boldsymbol{u}^{0}
is spherically symmetric via an argument similar to that in the proof of Lemma
11.1
. Now we can choose
T^{\prime}\in(0,T^{*}]
small enough such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{\prime}]}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T^{\prime}}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{0}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}^{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{1}^{2},

(11.42)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{\prime}]}\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{t}^{0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T^{\prime}}\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}^{0},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}^{0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{\prime}]}t\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}^{0},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}^{0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T^{\prime}}s\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}^{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}^{0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{3}^{2}.

We divide the rest of the proof into four steps.

1. Existence.

Starting the iteration with
\boldsymbol{w}=\boldsymbol{u}^{0}
in (
11.1
),
we first obtain a solution
(\phi^{1},\boldsymbol{u}^{1},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{1})

of problem (
11.1
).
Then we inductively construct approximate sequences
(\phi^{k+1},\boldsymbol{u}^{k+1},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k+1})
as follows: Suppose that
(\phi^{k},\boldsymbol{u}^{k},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k})
for
k\geq 1
has been obtained. then we define
(\phi^{k+1},\boldsymbol{u}^{k+1},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k+1})
by solving the following problem in
[0,T^{\prime}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\phi^{k+1}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\cdot\nabla\phi^{k+1}+(\gamma-1)\phi^{k+1}\diver\boldsymbol{u}^{k}=0,\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}^{k+1}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k}+\nabla\phi^{k+1}+L\boldsymbol{u}^{k+1}=\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k+1}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u}^{k}),\\
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k+1}_{t}+\sum_{l=1}^{n}A_{l}(\boldsymbol{u}^{k})\partial_{l}\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k+1}+B(\boldsymbol{u}^{k})\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k+1}+\nabla\diver\boldsymbol{u}^{k}=0,\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle(\phi^{k+1},\boldsymbol{u}^{k+1},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k+1})|_{t=0}=(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0})\ \ \ \,\quad\text{for $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle(\phi^{k+1},\boldsymbol{u}^{k+1})\to(0,\boldsymbol{0})\ \ \text{as}\ \ |\boldsymbol{x}|\to\infty\qquad\quad\text{for $t\in(0,T^{\prime}]$}.\end{cases}

(11.43)

By replacing
\boldsymbol{w}
with
\boldsymbol{u}^{k}
in (
11.1
), we can solve problem (
11.43
). Clearly,
(\phi^{k},\boldsymbol{u}^{k},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k})
satisfies the uniform estimates (
11.41
): for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}^{k}(t)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{k}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}^{2},

(11.44)

\displaystyle\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{k},\boldsymbol{u}^{k}_{t})(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}^{k},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k}_{t})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle t\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}^{k},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k}_{t})(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}s\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{k}_{t},\boldsymbol{u}^{k}_{tt})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq c_{3}^{2},

\displaystyle\big\|(\phi^{k})^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}(t)\big\|_{L^{1}}+\|\phi^{k}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(\nabla\phi^{k},\phi_{t}^{k})(t)\|_{H^{1}}+\|(\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}_{t})(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{3}^{7\gamma-2}.

Next, we are going to prove that the whole sequence
(\phi^{k},\boldsymbol{u}^{k},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k})
converges strongly to a limit
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
in some Sobolev space. Let

\hat{\phi}^{k+1}=\phi^{k+1}-\phi^{k},\qquad\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}=\boldsymbol{u}^{k+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{k},\qquad\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}=\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k+1}-\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}.

Then, by (
11.43
), one can deduce that

\begin{cases}\hat{\phi}^{k+1}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\cdot\nabla\hat{\phi}^{k+1}+(\gamma-1)\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\diver\boldsymbol{u}^{k}=\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{k},\\[4.0pt]
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\cdot\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}+\nabla\hat{\phi}^{k+1}+L\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u}^{k})+\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{k},\\
\displaystyle\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}_{t}+\sum_{l=1}^{n}A_{l}(\boldsymbol{u}^{k})\partial_{l}\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}+B(\boldsymbol{u}^{k})\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}+\nabla\diver\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}=\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{k}_{3},\\
(\hat{\phi}^{k+1},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1},\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1})|_{t=0}=(0,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{0})\qquad\quad\ \ \,\text{for $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle(\hat{\phi}^{k+1},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1})\to(0,\boldsymbol{0})\ \ \text{as}\ \ |\boldsymbol{x}|\to\infty\qquad\quad\text{for $t\in(0,T^{\prime}]$},\end{cases}

(11.45)

where
\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{i}^{k}
(
i=1,2,3
) are defined by

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{k}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\cdot\nabla\phi^{k}-(\gamma-1)\phi^{k}\diver\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\qquad\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{k}_{2}=\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\cdot Q(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k})-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k-1},

(11.46)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{3}^{k}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{l=1}^{n}A_{l}(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k})\partial_{l}\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}-B(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k})\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}.

1.1. Estimates of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}
.
First, we prove the following axillary lemma, which implies that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\in L^{\infty}([0,T^{\prime}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Lemma 11.5
.

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\,\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\in L^{\infty}([0,T^{\prime}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
.

Proof.

Let
\zeta(\boldsymbol{x})\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be a test function satisfying

0\leq\zeta(\boldsymbol{x})\leq 1,\quad\,\,\zeta(\boldsymbol{x})=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }0\leq|\boldsymbol{x}|\leq 1,\\
0&\text{if }|\boldsymbol{x}|\geq 2.\end{cases}

(11.47)

Define
\zeta_{R}(\boldsymbol{x})=\zeta(\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{R})
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}_{(R)}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\zeta_{R}
for
R\geq 1
. Then it follows from

\eqref{eq:1.2w}_{3}
that

\big(\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}_{(R)}\big)_{t}+\sum_{l=1}^{n}A_{l}(\boldsymbol{u}^{k})\partial_{l}\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}_{(R)}+B(\boldsymbol{u}^{k})\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}_{(R)}+\zeta_{R}\nabla\diver\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}=\zeta_{R}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{k}_{3}+\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}(\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\cdot\nabla\zeta_{R}).

(11.48)

Multiplying (
11.48
) by
2\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}_{(R)}
and integrating the resulting equality over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we obtain from (
11.44
), Lemmas
A.1
,
C.2
, and
C.4
, and the Hölder inequality that

\begin{split}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}_{(R)}\big\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}_{(R)}\big\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\nabla^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\quad+C\delta_{2n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}+C\delta_{3n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\\
&\quad+C\delta_{2n}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+C\delta_{3n}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\\
&\leq C(c_{0})\big(\big\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}_{(R)}\big\|_{L^{2}}+1\big),\end{split}

(11.49)

where
C(c_{0})>0
is a constant depending only on
(C,c_{0})
. Then applying the Grönwall inequality to (
11.49
) gives that, for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
and
R\geq 1
,

\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}_{(R)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(c_{0})T^{\prime}\exp\big(C(c_{0})T^{\prime}\big),

which, along with Lemma
A.5
, yields that

\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\in L^{\infty}([0,T^{\prime}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})).

(11.50)

The proof of Lemma
11.5
is completed.
∎

Now we turn to derive the uniform estimate of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}
. Multiplying
(\ref {eq:1.2w})_{3}
by
2\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}
and integrating the resulting equality over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we see from (
11.44
), Lemmas
A.1
,
C.2
, and
C.4
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\!+\!\|\nabla^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\!+\!\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

(11.51)

\displaystyle+C\delta_{2n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\delta_{3n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(c_{0})\big(\omega^{-1}+t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\omega\big(\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big)

for
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
, where
C(c_{0})
is a constant depending only on
(C,c_{0})
and
\omega\in(0,1)
is a constant to be determined later.

1.2. Estimates of
\hat{\phi}^{k+1}
.
First, multiplying
(\ref {eq:1.2w})_{1}
by
2\hat{\phi}^{k+1}
and then integrating the resulting equality over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
we obtain from (
11.44
), Lemma
A.1
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{6}}\|\nabla\phi^{k}\|_{L^{3}}\|\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

(11.52)

\displaystyle+C\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\phi^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(c_{0})\big(\omega^{-1}+t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\|\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\omega\big(\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big).

Next, applying
2\partial^{\varsigma}\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\partial^{\varsigma}
with the multi-index
|\varsigma|=1
to
(\ref {eq:1.2w})_{1}
and integrating the resulting equality over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we similarly obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{\varsigma}\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{6}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{3}}\big)\|\nabla\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

(11.53)

\displaystyle+C\big(\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\phi^{k}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\phi^{k}\|_{L^{6}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{3}}\big)\|\nabla\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\|\phi^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}},

which, along with (
11.44
), (
11.52
), Lemma
A.1
,
and the Young inequality, yields that, for
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
and
\omega\in(0,1)
,

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\leq C(c_{0})\big(\omega^{-1}+t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\|\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\omega\big(\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big).

(11.54)

1.3. Estimates of
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}
.
Multiplying
(\ref {eq:1.2w})_{2}
by
2\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}
and integrating the resulting equality over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we obtain from Lemmas
A.1
–
A.2
,
C.2
, and
C.4
, and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2\alpha\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2\alpha\|\diver\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{6}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k-1}\|_{L^{3}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{6}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{3}}+C\delta_{2n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\delta_{3n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{H^{2}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{H^{1}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k-1}\|_{H^{1}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{H^{1}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{6-n}{6}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{n}{6}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\delta_{2n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{D^{1}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\delta_{3n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{D^{1}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+C\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}},

which, along with (
11.44
) and the Young inequality, implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\alpha\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(11.55)

\displaystyle\leq C(c_{0})\omega^{-1}\big(\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)+\omega\big(\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big).

Similarly, applying
2\partial^{\varsigma}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\partial^{\varsigma}
with the multi-index
|\varsigma|=1
to
(\ref {eq:1.2w})_{2}
and then integrating the resulting equality over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{\varsigma}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\alpha\|\partial^{\varsigma}\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\alpha\|\partial^{\varsigma}\!\diver\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k-1},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k})\|_{L^{6}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{3}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{k-1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial^{\varsigma}\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\delta_{2n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\nabla^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}+C\delta_{3n}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\nabla^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{6}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{3}}+C\|\nabla\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial^{\varsigma}\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k-1},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k})\|_{H^{1}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{H^{1}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{H^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{H^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{k-1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\!+\!C\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{4-n}{4}}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{n}{4}}\|\partial^{\varsigma}\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{D^{1}}\|\nabla^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\big(\delta_{2n}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}+\delta_{3n}\|\nabla^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{H^{1}}\|\nabla^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{n}{6}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{6-n}{6}}+C\|\nabla\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial^{\varsigma}\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}},

which, along with (
11.44
), (
11.55
),
and the Young inequality, yields
that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\alpha\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}

(11.56)

\displaystyle\leq C(c_{0})\big(\omega^{-1}\!+\!t^{-\frac{n}{4}}\big)\big(\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\!+\!\|\hat{\phi}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\!+\!\omega\big(\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\!+\!\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big),

for
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
and
\omega\in(0,1)
.

1.4.
Finally, define the energy function:

\begin{split}\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{k+1}(t)=&\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\|\hat{\phi}^{k+1}(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}(\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Collecting (
11.51
), (
11.54
), and (
11.56
) yields that

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{k+1}+\alpha\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\leq C(c_{0})\big(\omega^{-1}+t^{-\frac{1}{2}}+t^{-\frac{n}{4}}\big)\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{k+1}+\omega\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}+\omega\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}.

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, implies that

\begin{split}&\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{k+1}(t)+\alpha\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq C(c_{0})\exp\Big(C(c_{0})\big(\frac{t}{\omega}+t^{\frac{1}{2}}+t^{\frac{4-n}{4}}\big)\Big)\Big(\omega t\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(t)+\omega\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\mathrm{d}s\Big).\end{split}

(11.57)

Choose
\omega\in(0,1)
and
T_{*}\in(0,\min\{1,T^{\prime}\})
small enough such that

\omega=T_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}},\qquad\exp\Big(C(c_{0})\big(T_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}}+T_{*}^{\frac{4-n}{4}}\big)\Big)T_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\min\big\{\frac{1}{4},\frac{\alpha}{4}\big\}.

Then we conclude from (
11.57
) that

\begin{split}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big(\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{k+1}(T_{*})+\alpha\int_{0}^{T_{*}}\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\leq C(c_{0})<\infty,\end{split}

which implies that the whole sequence
(\phi^{k},\boldsymbol{u}^{k},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k})
converges to some limit
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
in the following sense as
k\to\infty
:

\begin{split}&\phi^{k}\rightarrow\phi\ \ \ \text{in}\ \ L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\qquad\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{u}\ \ \text{in}\ \ L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\\
&\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{\psi}\ \ \text{in}\ \ L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(B_{R}))\qquad\text{for any $R>0$},\end{split}

(11.58)

where
B_{R}=\{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\boldsymbol{x}|\leq R\}
.

On the other hand, by virtue of the uniform estimates (
11.44
), there exists a subsequence (still denoted by)
(\phi^{k},\boldsymbol{u}^{k},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k})
converging to the limit
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
in the weak or weak* sense. According to the lower semi-continuity of norms, the corresponding estimates in (
11.41
) for
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
still hold. Therefore, it is direct to show that
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
is a weak solution of system (
3.2
) in the sense of distributions and
satisfies the following regularities:

\displaystyle\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

(11.59)

\displaystyle(\nabla\phi,\phi_{t})\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\ \ (\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t})\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{l}\boldsymbol{u}\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];H^{2-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T_{*}];D^{3-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\ \ l=0,1,

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\partial_{t}^{l}\boldsymbol{u}\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];D^{3-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad\sqrt{t}\partial_{t}^{l+1}\boldsymbol{u}\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];D^{2-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\ \ l=0,1.

2. Uniqueness.
Let
(\phi_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{1})
and
(\phi_{2},\boldsymbol{u}_{2},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{2})
be two regular solutions of
problem (
3.2
) satisfying the uniform estimates in (
11.41
). Set

\hat{\phi}=\phi_{1}-\phi_{2},\qquad\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}=\boldsymbol{u}_{1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{2},\qquad\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}=\boldsymbol{\psi}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\psi}_{2}.

Then
(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})
satisfies
(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})|_{t=0}=(0,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{0})
and the system:

\begin{cases}\hat{\phi}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\cdot\nabla\hat{\phi}+(\gamma-1)\hat{\phi}\diver\boldsymbol{u}_{1}=\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{1},\\[2.0pt]
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\cdot\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}+\nabla\hat{\phi}+L\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}=\boldsymbol{\psi}_{1}\cdot Q(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}})+\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{2},\\[2.0pt]
\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\cdot\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}+\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}+\nabla\diver\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}=\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{3},\end{cases}

(11.60)

where
\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{i}

(i=1,2,3)
are defined by

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\nabla\phi_{2}-(\gamma-1)\phi_{2}\diver\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\qquad\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{2}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u}_{2})-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{2},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{l=1}^{n}A_{l}(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}})\partial_{l}\boldsymbol{\psi}_{2}-B(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}})\boldsymbol{\psi}_{2}.

Next, let

\hat{\mathcal{E}}(t)=\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|\hat{\phi}(s)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}(s)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(s)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Similarly to the arguments in Steps 1.1–1.4 above, we can show that

\begin{split}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\hat{\mathcal{E}}(t)+\|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\leq F(t)\hat{\mathcal{E}}(t)\qquad\text{for some $F(t)\in L^{1}(0,T_{*})$},\end{split}

(11.61)

which, along with the Grönwall inequality and
\hat{\mathcal{E}}(0)=0
, leads to
\hat{\mathcal{E}}(t)=0
for all
t\in[0,T_{*}]
, that is,
(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})=(0,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{0})
, which leads to the uniqueness. Moreover, by the same argument used in Step 2 of the proof
for Lemma
11.1
, we see that (
3.10
) holds.

3. Time-continuity and spherical symmetry of the solutions.
The time-continuity follows directly from the same arguments as in Steps 2–3 in §
9.1
, except for replacing
T
with
T_{*}
. To show that
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
is spherically symmetric, taking form (
3.3
), let
\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
and set

\hat{\phi}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\phi(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}),\qquad\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}),\qquad\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\psi}(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}).

Then, following the analogous calculations in Steps 3.1–3.3 of the proof of Lemma
11.1
with
\boldsymbol{w}
replaced by
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}
, we
can show that
(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})
is also a solution to system (
3.2
) taking the initial data
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0})
. Finally, following the same argument as in Step 3.4 of the proof of Lemma
11.1
,
we obtain the spherical symmetry of
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
.

4. Initial data with
\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
in the 3-D case.
So far, we have derived Theorem
3.1

for the 2-D case (note that
\boldsymbol{\psi}\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
is a direct consequence of Lemma
C.4
and
\boldsymbol{\psi}\in C([0,T_{*}];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
. Now we focus on Theorem
3.1
for the 3-D case. Assume that the initial data
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0})
with
\phi_{0}>0
are spherically symmetric and satisfy (
3.8
). Then, according to the proof of Steps 1–3 above, there exists a unique spherically symmetric solution
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
of the nonlinear problem (
3.2
)–(
3.5
) in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
satisfying (
3.10
), and (i)–(iv) of Lemma
11.1
with
T
replaced by
T_{*}
. Thus, it remains to show that

\boldsymbol{\psi}\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}).

(11.62)

To this end, we introduce the so-called effective velocity:

\boldsymbol{v}(t,\boldsymbol{x}):=\boldsymbol{u}(t,\boldsymbol{x})+2\alpha\boldsymbol{\psi}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\boldsymbol{u}(t,\boldsymbol{x})+\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma-1}\nabla\log\phi(t,\boldsymbol{x}).

(11.63)

Then combining
\eqref{eqn1}_{2}
and (
3.6
) with (
11.63
) yields that the equation of
\boldsymbol{v}
takes the form:

\boldsymbol{v}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{v}+\frac{\gamma-1}{2\alpha}\phi(\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{u})=\boldsymbol{0},

(11.64)

where we have used the fact that
\nabla\boldsymbol{v}=(\nabla\boldsymbol{v})^{\top}
, since
\boldsymbol{v}
is spherically symmetric.

Next, we define the flow mapping
\boldsymbol{\eta}(t,\boldsymbol{x})
associated with
\boldsymbol{u}
:

\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\boldsymbol{u}(t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(t,\boldsymbol{x}))\qquad\text{with}\ \ \boldsymbol{\eta}(0,\boldsymbol{x})=\boldsymbol{x}.

(11.65)

Then, via the above flow mapping, (
11.64
) becomes

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\boldsymbol{v}(t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(t,\boldsymbol{x}))+\frac{\gamma-1}{2\alpha}(\phi\boldsymbol{v})(t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(t,\boldsymbol{x}))=\frac{\gamma-1}{2\alpha}(\phi\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(t,\boldsymbol{x})).

(11.66)

Solving the above ODE leads to

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{v}(t,\boldsymbol{\eta}(t,\boldsymbol{x}))

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{v}(0,\boldsymbol{x})\exp\Big(-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\gamma-1}{2\alpha}\phi(\tau,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\tau,\boldsymbol{x}))\,\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)

(11.67)

\displaystyle+\frac{\gamma-1}{2\alpha}\!\int_{0}^{t}(\phi\boldsymbol{u})(s,\boldsymbol{\eta}(s,\boldsymbol{x}))\cdot\exp\Big(\!-\!\int_{s}^{t}\frac{\gamma-1}{2\alpha}\phi(\tau,\boldsymbol{\eta}(\tau,\boldsymbol{x}))\,\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)\mathrm{d}s,

which, along with
\phi>0
, implies that

\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{v}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+CT_{*}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C(1+T_{*}),

that is,
\boldsymbol{v}\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Therefore, by (
11.63
), we derive claim (
11.62
).

The proof of Theorem
3.1
is completed.

11.1.4.
Proof of Theorem
3.3

First, it follows from Theorem
3.1
that there exist
T_{*}>0
and a unique
2
-order regular solution
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
of problem (
3.2
)–(
3.5
) in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfying (
2.7
) and (
3.10
), which is spherically symmetric taking form (
3.3
).

Next, based on (
3.1
), we define

\rho(t,\boldsymbol{x}):=\Big(\frac{\gamma-1}{A\gamma}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}(t,\boldsymbol{x}).

Clearly,
\rho(t,\boldsymbol{x})>0
in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
, due to
\phi(t,\boldsymbol{x})>0
. By a direct calculation, we have

\frac{\partial\rho}{\partial\phi}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\frac{1}{\gamma-1}\Big(\frac{\gamma-1}{A\gamma}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\phi^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma-1}}(t,\boldsymbol{x}).

Then multiplying
\eqref{eqn1}_{1}
by
\frac{\partial\rho}{\partial\phi}(t,\boldsymbol{x})
yields the continuity equation
\eqref{eq:1.1benwen}_{1}
, and multiplying
\eqref{eqn1}_{2}
by
\rho(t,\boldsymbol{x})
gives the momentum equations
\eqref{eq:1.1benwen}_{2}
.

Therefore,
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})
satisfies the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) in the sense of distributions and, clearly, satisfies (ii)–(iii) in Definition
2.1
with
s=2
and
T
replaced by
T_{*}
, and (
2.7
) with
T
replaced by
T_{*}
. In summary, the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) admits a 2-unique regular solution
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})
.

Finally, since
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u})
are spherically symmetric,
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})
are also spherically symmetric taking form (
2.8
). The proof of Theorem
3.3
is completed.

11.2.
Local well-posedness of the 3-order regular solutions with far-field vacuum

We now prove Theorem
3.2
in §
11.2.1
–§
11.2.3
.
At the end of §
11.2
, we also show that
this theorem indeed implies Theorem
3.4
.

11.2.1.
Linearization

We start with the proof of Theorem
3.2
by considering the linearized problem (
11.1
) with
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0})
,
\phi_{0}>0
, satisfying (
3.11
). In this case, the vector function
\boldsymbol{w}=(w_{1},\cdots\!,w_{n})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
given in (
11.1
) is supposed to be spherically symmetric satisfying
\boldsymbol{w}(0,\boldsymbol{x})=\boldsymbol{u}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})
and, for any
T>0
,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{w}(t,\boldsymbol{x})|_{|\boldsymbol{x}|=0}=\boldsymbol{0},\quad\boldsymbol{w}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=w(t,|\boldsymbol{x}|)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{|\boldsymbol{x}|}\ \qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T]$},

(11.68)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{l}\boldsymbol{w}\in C([0,T];H^{3-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{4-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad l=0,1,

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\partial_{t}^{l}\boldsymbol{w}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{4-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad l=0,1,2,

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\partial_{t}^{l+1}\boldsymbol{w}\in L^{2}([0,T];D^{3-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad l=0,1.

We then establish the global well-posedness of the linearized problem (
11.1
) with (
11.68
), which follows from classical arguments as in
[
17
,
24
]
and the similar discussion in the proof of Lemma
11.1
.

Lemma 11.6
.

Let
n=2
or
3
, and (
3.7
) hold. Assume that the initial data
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0})(\boldsymbol{x})
are spherically symmetric and satisfy (
3.11
). Then, for any
T>0
, there exists a unique solution
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
of the linearized problem (
11.1
) in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that (
3.10
) holds and

\displaystyle\mathrm{(i)}

\displaystyle\ (\phi,\boldsymbol{u})\ \text{with $\phi>0$ satisfies this problem in the sense of distributions};

\displaystyle\mathrm{(ii)}

\displaystyle\ \phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\in C([0,T];L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\ \ (\nabla\phi,\phi_{t})\in C([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\psi}\in C([0,T];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\ \ \boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt}\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\ \ \phi_{tt}\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T_{*}];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}));

\displaystyle\mathrm{(iii)}

\displaystyle\ \boldsymbol{u}(t,\boldsymbol{x})|_{|\boldsymbol{x}|=0}=\boldsymbol{0}\ \ \text{for}\ \ t\in[0,T],

\displaystyle\ \partial_{t}^{l}\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];H^{3-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{4-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad l=0,1;

\displaystyle\mathrm{(iv)}

\displaystyle\ \sqrt{t}\partial_{t}^{l}\boldsymbol{u}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{4-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad l=0,1,2,

\displaystyle\ \sqrt{t}\partial_{t}^{l+1}\boldsymbol{u}\in L^{2}([0,T];D^{3-2l}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad l=0,1.

Moreover,
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
is spherically symmetric which takes form (
3.3
).

11.2.2.
The uniform
a priori
estimates

Let
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
be a solution in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
obtained in Lemma
11.6
. We now establish the corresponding
a priori
estimates. For this purpose, we first choose a constant
c_{0}>0
such that

\begin{split}2+\big\|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\big\|_{L^{1}}+\|\nabla\phi_{0}\|_{H^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{H^{3}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{H^{1}}\leq c_{0}.\end{split}

(11.69)

We assume that there exist
T^{*}\in(0,T)
and constants
c_{i}
(
i=1,2,3
) such that
1<c_{0}\leq c_{1}\leq c_{2}\leq c_{3}\leq c_{4}
and

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}]}\|\boldsymbol{w}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\int_{0}^{T^{*}}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c^{2}_{1},

(11.70)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}]}\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{w}_{t})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T^{*}}\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w},\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c^{2}_{2},

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}]}\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w},\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T^{*}}\|(\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{w},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t},\boldsymbol{w}_{tt})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c^{2}_{3},

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}]}t\|(\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{w},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t},\boldsymbol{w}_{tt})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T^{*}}t\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}_{t},\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{tt})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c^{2}_{4},

where
T^{*}
and
c_{i}
(
i=1,2,3,4
) will be determined later, depending only on
c_{0}
and the fixed constants
(\alpha,\gamma,A,T)
.

First, we can follow the proof of Lemma
11.2
to derive the following estimates for
(\phi,\boldsymbol{\psi})
.

Lemma 11.7
.

For any
t\in[0,T_{1}]
with
T_{1}:=\min\{T^{*},(1+Cc_{3})^{-2}\}
,

\displaystyle\big\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}(t)\big\|_{L^{1}}\leq c_{0},\quad\|\phi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\phi(t)\|_{H^{1}}\leq Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-3},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k-1}\phi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-3}c_{k}\ (k=1,2),\ \ \|\boldsymbol{\psi}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}\cap D^{1}}\leq Cc_{0},\quad\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}c_{2}.

Proof.

We only show how the
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate (
n=2,3
) of
\boldsymbol{\psi}
for
t\in[0,T_{1}]
can be obtained.

When
n=2
, it follows directly from Lemma
C.4
that,
for
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

\|\boldsymbol{\psi}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq Cc_{0}.

When
n=3
, consider the flow mapping
\boldsymbol{\xi}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying

\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\boldsymbol{w}(t,\boldsymbol{\xi}(t,\boldsymbol{x}))\qquad\text{with $\boldsymbol{\xi}(0,\boldsymbol{x})=\boldsymbol{x}$}.

(11.71)

Then
\eqref{li4}_{3}
can be written as

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\boldsymbol{\psi}(t,\boldsymbol{\xi}(t,\boldsymbol{x}))=-(\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi})(t,\boldsymbol{\xi}(t,\boldsymbol{x}))-\nabla\diver\boldsymbol{w}(t,\boldsymbol{\xi}(t,\boldsymbol{x})).

Integrating the above over
[0,t]
and taking the
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
-norm of the resulting equality, along with (
11.70
), Lemma
A.1
,
and the Hölder inequality, yield that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\psi}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\Big(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big)\int_{0}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+Cc_{3}\int_{0}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{t}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+Cc_{3}\int_{0}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\mathrm{d}s+c_{3}\sqrt{t},

which, along with the Grönwall inequality and Lemmas
A.2
and
C.2
, implies that, for all
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\psi}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+Cc_{0}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{2}}+Cc_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{2}}+Cc_{0}\leq Cc_{0}.

∎

Next, we obtain the higher-order estimates for
(\phi,\boldsymbol{\psi})
.

Lemma 11.8
.

For any
t\in[0,T_{2}]
with
T_{2}:=\min\{T_{1},(1+Cc_{4})^{-2}\}
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{3}\phi(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-2},\quad\|\nabla^{2}\phi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-2}c_{3},\quad\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{0},

\displaystyle\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}c_{3},\ \ \|\phi_{tt}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big\|(\nabla\phi_{tt},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt})\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc_{3}^{14\gamma}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1. Estimates on
(\nabla^{3}\phi,\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi})
.
Applying
\partial^{\varsigma}
with multi-index
|\varsigma|=2
to
\eqref{li4}_{3}
, then multiplying the resulting equality by
\partial^{\varsigma}\boldsymbol{\psi}
,
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we obtain from (
11.70
), Lemmas
11.7
and
A.1
, and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{\varsigma}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{4}}+\|\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\|\partial^{\varsigma}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{1}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\|\partial^{\varsigma}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{3}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(c_{3}^{2}+c_{4}t^{-\frac{1}{2}})\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}.

Summing the above with respect to
\varsigma
over
|\varsigma|=2
leads to

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{3}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}+C(c_{3}^{2}+c_{4}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, yields that

\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq e^{Cc_{3}t}\big(\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+Cc_{3}^{2}t+Cc_{4}\sqrt{t}\big)\leq Cc_{0}.

(11.72)

for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
with
T_{2}:=\min\{T_{1},(1+Cc_{4})^{-2}\}
.

Next, for the
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate of
\nabla^{3}\phi
, it follows from (
3.10
), (
11.72
), Lemmas
11.7
and
A.1
, and the Hölder inequality that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{3}\phi\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla^{2}\phi\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{4}}+\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

(11.73)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla^{2}\phi\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{1}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{H^{1}}\!+\!\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-2}.

2. Estimates on
(\phi_{t},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t})
.

First, it follows from
\eqref{li4}_{3}
, (
11.72
),
and Lemma
A.1
that, for
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

(11.74)

\displaystyle+C\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{H^{1}}+1\big)\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{3}}\leq Cc_{0}c_{3}.

Then, for the
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate of
\nabla^{2}\phi_{t}
, it follows from (
3.10
), (
11.70
)–(
11.72
) and (
11.74
), Lemmas
11.7
and
A.1
–
A.2
, and the Hölder inequality that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\phi_{t}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\phi_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\!+\!\|\phi_{t}\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{4}}\!+\!\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{4}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{4}}\!+\!\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

(11.75)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\phi_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\phi_{t}\|_{H^{1}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{H^{1}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{1}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-2}c_{3}.

3. Estimates on
(\phi_{tt},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt})
.

We first apply
\partial_{t}
to both sides of
\eqref{li4}_{1}
and
\eqref{li4}_{3}
, respectively, to obtain

\displaystyle\phi_{tt}

\displaystyle=-\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\cdot\nabla\phi-\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\phi_{t}-(\gamma-1)(\phi_{t}\diver\boldsymbol{w}+\phi\diver\boldsymbol{w}_{t}),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{l=1}^{n}A_{l}(\boldsymbol{w}_{t})\partial_{l}\boldsymbol{\psi}-\sum_{l=1}^{n}A_{l}(\boldsymbol{w})\partial_{l}\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}-(B(\boldsymbol{w}_{t})\boldsymbol{\psi}+B(\boldsymbol{w})\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t})-\nabla\diver\boldsymbol{w}_{t}.

It follows from the above, (
3.10
), (
11.70
)–(
11.75
), Lemmas
11.7
and
A.1
, and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{4}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}

(11.76)

\displaystyle+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{4}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{4}}+\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{H^{1}}+C\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+C\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{1}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(c_{0}c_{2}c_{3}+\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big),

\displaystyle\|\phi_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\phi_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\|\phi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}\|\nabla\phi_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\|\phi_{t}\|_{H^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(c_{0}^{7\gamma-2}c_{2}+c_{0}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2}^{2}+c_{0}^{7\gamma-2}c_{1}c_{3}+c_{0}^{7\gamma-3}c_{3}\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-2}c_{2}c_{3},

\displaystyle\|\nabla\phi_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(\phi\boldsymbol{\psi})_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\phi_{tt}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\phi_{t}\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\phi\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\phi_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\|\phi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\phi_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\|\phi_{t}\|_{H^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(c_{0}^{7\gamma-1}c_{2}c_{3}+c_{0}^{7\gamma-3}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big).

Finally, the above inequalities, together with (
11.70
), yields that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\int_{0}^{t}\big\|(\nabla\phi_{tt},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt})\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc_{3}^{14\gamma+2}t+c_{0}^{14\gamma-6}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc_{3}^{14\gamma}.

(11.77)

The proof of Lemma
11.7
is completed.
∎

Finally, we derive the uniform energy estimates for
\boldsymbol{u}
.

Lemma 11.9
.

For any
t\in[0,T_{3}]
with
T_{3}:=\min\big\{T_{2},(1+Cc_{4})^{-28\gamma}\big\}
,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{0}^{2},

\displaystyle\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{u}_{t})(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{1}^{14\gamma-6}c_{2}^{\frac{n}{2}},

\displaystyle\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t})(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|(\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\boldsymbol{u}_{tt})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{2}^{14\gamma-6}c_{3}^{\frac{n}{2}},

\displaystyle t\|(\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\boldsymbol{u}_{tt})(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}s\|(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{tt})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{0}^{4}.

Proof.

The
H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimates of
\boldsymbol{u}
can be derived via the same argument as in Steps 1–3 of the proof of Lemma
11.4
. We thus only establish the
D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and time-weighted estimates of
\boldsymbol{u}
.
We divide the proof into three steps.

1.
D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate on
\boldsymbol{u}
.
Suppose that
Z(\boldsymbol{w})
is defined as in (
11.21
). We first show the
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate of
\nabla^{2}Z(\boldsymbol{w})
. Indeed, it follows from (
11.21
), (
11.70
), Lemmas
11.7
–
11.8
, and
A.1
, and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}Z(\boldsymbol{w})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(\boldsymbol{\psi},\boldsymbol{w})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|(\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi},\nabla\boldsymbol{w})\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{4}}

(11.78)

\displaystyle+C\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w})\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{\infty}+C\|\nabla^{3}\phi\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{H^{1}}\big)\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}+C\|\nabla^{3}\phi\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{2}c_{3}+Cc_{0}^{7\gamma-2}\leq Cc_{2}^{7\gamma-3}c_{3}.

Next, it follows from (
11.33
), (
11.70
), and the Young inequality that

\displaystyle\alpha\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\diver\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(c_{0}c_{2}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}+c_{0}c_{2}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{0}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+c_{0}^{7\gamma-3}c_{2}\Big)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(c_{0}c_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+c_{3}^{7\gamma-2}\Big)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(c_{0}^{2}c_{3}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{3}^{14\gamma-4}\big)+\frac{1}{8}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Integrating the above over
[\tau,t]
, together with (
11.70
) and the Hölder inequality, yields that, for all
t\in[0,T_{3}]
with
T_{3}:=\min\big\{T_{2},(1+Cc_{4})^{-28\gamma}\big\}
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\tau}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\Big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c_{0}^{2}c_{3}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+c_{3}^{14\gamma-4}t\Big)

(11.79)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c_{0}^{2}c_{3}\sqrt{t}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+c_{3}^{14\gamma-4}t\Big)\leq C(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c_{0}^{2}).

For
\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}
, it follows from
\eqref{li4}_{2}
, the time continuity of
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
, (
11.68
)–(
11.69
), Lemma
A.1
, and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to 0}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\phi_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{4}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}\phi_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{H^{3}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{H^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}^{2}.

Thus, based on the above estimates, we let
\tau\to 0
in (
11.79
) to obtain

\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc_{0}^{4}\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T_{3}]$}.

(11.80)

Besides, it follows from (
11.31
), (
11.38
), (
11.70
), (
11.80
), and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

(11.81)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c_{2}^{14\gamma-6}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{n}{2}}+c_{2}^{14\gamma-6}\big)

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c_{3}^{6}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{3}^{14\gamma-4}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\big(c_{0}^{4}+c_{2}^{14\gamma-6}c_{3}^{\frac{n}{2}}+c_{2}^{14\gamma-6}+c_{0}^{4}+c_{3}^{7}\sqrt{t}+c_{3}^{14\gamma-8}t\big)\leq Cc_{2}^{14\gamma-6}c_{3}^{\frac{n}{2}}.

Finally, it follows from (
11.25
), (
11.70
), (
11.78
), (
11.81
), Lemma
11.3
, and the classical regularity theory for elliptic equations in Lemma
A.13
that

\|\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\nabla^{2}Z(\boldsymbol{w}))\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\big(\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c_{2}^{14\gamma-6}c_{3}^{2}\big).

(11.82)

Integrating above over
[0,t]
yields that, for all
t\in[0,T_{3}]
,

\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+c_{2}^{14\gamma-6}c_{3}^{2}t\leq Cc_{2}^{14\gamma-6}c_{3}^{\frac{n}{2}}.

2. Time-weighted estimates on
\boldsymbol{u}
.
We first establish the
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate of
(Z(\boldsymbol{w}))_{tt}
. Indeed, it follows from (
11.21
), (
11.70
), (
11.76
), Lemmas
11.7
–
11.8
and
A.1
, and the Hölder inequality that, for all
t\in[0,T_{3}]
,

\displaystyle\|(Z(\boldsymbol{w}))_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(\boldsymbol{\psi},\boldsymbol{w})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|(\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t},\boldsymbol{w}_{t})\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{4}}

(11.83)

\displaystyle+C\|(\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt},\boldsymbol{w}_{tt})\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\|\nabla\phi_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}\big)\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|(\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t},\boldsymbol{w}_{t})\|_{H^{1}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle+C\|(\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt},\boldsymbol{w}_{tt})\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}+C\|\nabla\phi_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(c_{2}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}\!+\!c_{3}^{3}\!+\!c_{3}^{2}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\!+\!c_{3}\|(\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt},\boldsymbol{w}_{tt})\|_{L^{2}}\!+\!\|\nabla\phi_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}\big),

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{3}^{7\gamma+1}\big(\|(\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{tt},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t},\boldsymbol{w}_{tt})\|_{L^{2}}+1\big).

Next, formally applying
\partial_{t}^{2}
to (
11.21
) gives

\boldsymbol{u}_{ttt}+L\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}=(Z(\boldsymbol{w}))_{tt}.

(11.84)

Then multiplying above by
\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}
and integrating over
I
lead to
the following energy equality: for
t\in[\tau,T^{\prime}]
(
\tau>0
),
